> ### The THREE traps that will burn you

>    `0.006350` is the UNCALIBRATED frame. Raw MSE is not comparable and reads
>    HIGH (~0.012) — expected, NOT a regression. **But the frames differ by a
>    measured factor**: on the actual heldout corpus the calibrated target's
>    variance is `0.099650` vs the old `0.053570` = **1.860×**, and MSE scales
>    with target variance. Divide it out with **R² = 1 − MSE/Var(target)**, which
>    is frame-independent. D48X sb520 annealed = **0.8815**; D48Y2 sb90

**User directive (2026-07-28, 8-hour autonomy window):** fix the WDL scaling —
apply the correct draw-aware transform, fitted PER CORPUS so all corpora align.
Then retrain D48X's architecture with it: **300 superbatches in one run**, usual
LR to sb240, gentle anneal to 0 from sb240→300. Verify it trained and
converged. Then test.

## Why (the measured case)

Three independent instruments say our WEIGHTS, not our search or features, are
the deficit — all in ARCHON's own search at fixed 110k nodes:

| weights | Elo vs D48X | n |
|---|---|---|
| Stockfish `eca43a97` | **+90.7 ± 16.9** | 380 |
| Reckless 0.9.0 (768-wide) | **+30.0 ± 18.0** | 406 |
| our net, de-framed (null) | −12.0 ± 18.2 | 406 |

and `sf-features` proved our HalfKA + FullThreats indices are IDENTICAL to
SF's (40/40 × 2 perspectives), so it is not the feature set.

**The defect.** The trainer builds `target = wdl*result + (1-wdl)*eval_t`.
`result` is an expected score (draw = 0.5). `eval_t` is
`sigmoid(score/400)` — a win probability with **no draw model**. The two
halves are different quantities and we average them. At +174 the eval half
says 0.607 where real outcomes score 0.75; the blended label is ~0.65. The
error is symmetric about 0.5, so it CANCELS IN THE MEAN and does not cancel in
the FUNCTION: every position is pulled toward 0.5 and the learned curve is
systematically flatter than reality. Invisible to rank-correlation tests
(ranking is scale-invariant), which is why our net looked fine on those.

**The fix already exists in the code**, unused: `features.py:545-552`,
`TrainConfig.target_offset` (`spec.py:309`), default `0.0` = the broken branch.
Measured on our own corpora, held out: Brier **0.0354 vs 0.0532**.

**The irony in D48X's own config:** `teacher_frame_offset: 163.0,
teacher_frame_scale: 76.9` — its teacher WAS trained in the calibrated frame,
and we mapped the teacher DOWN into the plain-sigmoid label frame to make them
agree. We converted the good frame into the bad one.

## Why per-corpus

The transform is not a property of positions; it is a property of the LABELLING
PROCESS and the POPULATION — label depth, who played the games out, and the
position mix all change what "score s" predicts about the outcome. Our own
measured fits disagree by >2x in scale (uho a=175.5 b=71.9; fishpack32
a=160.2 b=33.2). One global transform over a 12-corpus mix is a compromise that
is wrong for every component.

**Confound to rule out first:** a scale difference can also be a UNITS
difference (the `SFBINPACK_SCORE_DIV` frame bug class). The training study
tested frame-mixing across 11 corpora and called it a null (±13%), but a 2x
gap exceeds that null, so the fits get a units sanity check before use.

## Plan

1. **Fit** `(a_i, b_i)` per corpus by MLE, held out, bootstrapped
   (`tools/nnue/fit_target_transform.py`).
2. **Sanity-check** the spread: units confound vs genuine predictiveness.
3. **Thread per-corpus params** through `make_mixed_batches`. This is cheap
   because each batch is drawn from exactly ONE corpus (`data.py:95-130`), so
   the corpus index is known at map time.
4. **Verify calibration** post-transform: every corpus's targets should agree
   with observed outcomes (Brier per corpus, before vs after).
5. **Train** D48Y: same arch (`arch_hl 1280, arch_l2 16, arch_l3 32,
   out_skip`), 300sb, no teacher, LR flat to sb240 then anneal to 0 by sb300.
6. **Verify convergence** — TB telemetry armed at launch (standing rule), and
   the heldout MUST be computed in the ARM'S OWN FRAME. D48X's heldout is
   pinned at `wdl 0.3 / scale 400 / offset 0`, which would score a calibrated
   net as a regression BY CONSTRUCTION.
7. **Test** vs D48X: fixed-node screen first (attribution), then real-TC.

### State log — implementation complete, awaiting fits

- **Per-corpus transform wired and verified** (`51fe78e`). Design change forced
  by the code: `shuffle_chunk=128` pools records from SEVERAL corpora and
  shuffles them, so a batch is NOT single-corpus — params ride PER RECORD via
  an int32 `src` array carried through the same permutation/slicing in both the
  serial and threaded paths. Verified: per-record == scalar bit-exact;
  mixed-corpus batch splits correctly; `per_corpus_target=None` reproduces the
  old pipeline BIT-IDENTICALLY.
- **Two silent failures caught in `pump_train.py` before spending GPU:**
  1. It builds `TrainConfig` from an EXPLICIT field list, so `per_corpus_target`
     was being dropped — the run would have trained the OLD target while the
     config claimed the new one. Now threaded.
  2. `heldout_value_loss` used `TrainConfig()` defaults
     (`wdl 0.3 / scale 400 / offset 0`) — the uncalibrated frame. It would have
     scored a calibrated net as a REGRESSION BY CONSTRUCTION. Now takes the
     run's own transform via `heldout_target`.
- **Heldout corpus fitted**: `uho-band0-5` a=175.1 b=71.6, brier 0.03537 vs

**UNITS CONFOUND RULED OUT — the check the plan demanded.** A pure units
rescale by `k` maps `(a,b) → (k·a, k·b)`, so **a/b is invariant** under it.
Observed a/b spans **1.40 → 4.84** (3.46×), and **corr(a,b) = 0.377** where a
units bug would force ~+1.0. So these are genuine SHAPE differences in how
score predicts outcome per corpus — exactly what per-corpus fitting is for —
not a `SFBINPACK_SCORE_DIV`-class units bug. Safe to use.

**Config** `tools/nnue/runs/d48y_1280.json`, generated from the fits by script
(not by hand): same arch/warm/seed/mix as D48X, `distill_alpha 0` and teacher
keys removed, `superbatches 300`, `lr_ramp [[0,1e-3],[240,1e-3],[300,0]]`,
`heldout_target [175.1, 71.6]`.

**Smoke (3sb) launched** and the heldout fix is confirmed live in its log:
`[pump] heldout scored in the RUN frame a=175.1 b=71.6`.

### State log — tick @ sb5: the TESTING GOTCHA found early

D48Y healthy: sb5/300, value 0.008378, 192k pos/s steady, GPU 94%, 5 GB free.

**Validated the post-training chain now rather than in 11 hours**, and found
the thing that would have invalidated the whole test:

**`tools/nnue/calibrate_network_scale.sh` is MANDATORY before any D48Y match.**
Its own header states the reason and cites a user ruling: *"a changed target
transform (v1.5's double sigmoid) has no clean closed-form inverse, and every
cp-denominated search margin — futility, RFP, delta, aspiration — is calibrated
to the incumbent's scale"*, and *"we MUST adjust for scale (it is a big gotcha
everytime) before we eval this thing"*.

D48Y changes exactly that transform, so its cp output scale WILL shift.
Matching it uncalibrated would measure MY frame error, not the net — the same
mechanism the Reckless frame-null priced at **−12.0 ± 18.2 Elo** for a 0.73×
de-framing. That null is now doing double duty: it is the quantitative warning
for this exact mistake.

**Two stale defaults in that script — do NOT run it bare:**
- `ENG=engines/bin/archon-d47` — a 768-wide binary that CANNOT load a 1280 net
  (fails `CENN-v2 l2_width 16 != 128`).
- `REF=…d47h-anneal-sb150-nocpol.cenn` — the wrong incumbent.
Pass explicitly: reference `data/nnue/nets/screens/d48x-1280-sb520-ema.cenn`,
engine `engines/canonical/archon` (sota,arch-d48x).

**Test sequence when the run finishes (in this order):**
1. take `d48y-1280-sb300-ema-quant.bin` → CENN (note: quant.bin is 138,320,792 B
   vs the canonical .cenn's 138,283,640 B — a 37,152 B delta, so the packing

2. `net_scale_check.sh` vs D48X → read `median(cand/ref)`.
3. `calibrate_network_scale.sh` until the ratio is ~1.000.
4. ONLY THEN: fixed-node screen vs D48X (attribution), then real TC (the gate).
5. Report the confound: D48Y differs from D48X in TWO ways — calibrated
   per-corpus target AND no teacher.

### CORRECTION — "our feature set is smaller" is the wrong framing

User pushed back on study 11's phrasing; checking it properly, the numbers hold
but the FRAMING was misleading and is hereby corrected.

| | threat dims | king in the threat space? |
|---|---|---|
| `sf_18` tag | 79,856 | **YES** — king attacks (8 targets), and N/B/R/Q may target the king |
| `eca43a97` (current dev) | 60,720 | **NO** — king removed as attacker AND as victim (row and column all −1) |
| **ARCHON** | **60,720** | **NO — identical to current SF** |

Our full input space is 22,528 + 60,720 = **83,248**, which is exactly the arch
string `stockfish-eca43a97` prints for its own net: `(83248, 1024, 32, 32, 1)`.

**So we are NOT smaller than Stockfish.** Stockfish SHRANK its threat space
between SF18 and current dev by deleting the king from it, and we match where
they landed. They had the larger space and chose to drop it — weak evidence
that the extra king features did not pay for their cost.

What is unchanged, and is the load-bearing part: `engines/bin/stockfish18`
(`id name Stockfish 18`) carries the 79,856 space, so studies 8/9/10 compared
our threat channel against a 31%-larger workload. Those comparisons are
mismatched and their threat numbers do not stand. `eca43a97` is the correct
target BECAUSE it matches us exactly.

### POST-TRAINING RUNBOOK (mechanical — every unknown now resolved)

```bash
# 1. pack (env is MANDATORY; bare netpack assumes 768 and dies)
env D40_ACC=1280 D40_L2=16 D40_L3=32 D40_BUCKETS=8 D40_POLICY=1 \
  .venv/bin/python tools/nnue/netpack.py <quant.bin> <cand.cenn> d40

# 2. scale check vs the incumbent  (pass engine + ref EXPLICITLY — the script's

REF_ENGINE=engines/canonical/archon bash tools/nnue/net_scale_check.sh \
  <cand.cenn> data/nnue/nets/screens/d48x-1280-sb520-ema.cenn engines/canonical/archon

# 3. calibrate network_scale until median(cand/ref) ~ 1.000  — MANDATORY.
#    A changed target transform shifts cp output scale, and every cp margin
#    (futility/RFP/delta/aspiration) is co-adapted to the incumbent's. Skipping
#    this measures MY frame error, not the net — the Reckless frame-null priced
#    that at -12.0 +/- 18.2 Elo for a 0.73x de-frame.
bash tools/nnue/calibrate_network_scale.sh <cand.cenn> \
  data/nnue/nets/screens/d48x-1280-sb520-ema.cenn 4

# 4. fixed-node screen vs D48X (attribution), then real TC (the gate).
```

### CP-SCALE RISK — one scalar is NOT enough, computed ahead of the net

Setting the scale is trivial (an i32 at CENN byte 44, automated). The risk is
whether a SCALAR can absorb the new transform at all. It cannot.

The head learns `out ≈ logit(target)`, cp = `out × network_scale`. Under the OLD
transform `logit(sigmoid(s/400)) × 400 = s` EXACTLY — which is why
network_scale is 400 and why a scalar always sufficed. Under the new
draw-aware transform the implied cp per unit of true score is not flat:

| s | uho (25%) | fishpack32 (16%) | farseerT74 (12%) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 50 | 0.0043 | 0.0013 | 0.0054 |
| 150 | 0.0057 | 0.0060 | 0.0057 |
| 400 | 0.0096 | 0.0198 | 0.0067 |
| **spread 50–400** | **2.2×** | **15.1×** | 1.2× |

⇒ D48Y will be relatively COMPRESSED at small advantages and EXPANDED at large
ones. That is the intended correction (the old transform understated big
advantages), but it means calibration aligns the MEDIAN and leaves the tails
mismatched.

**Consequence for the test.** Every cp-denominated margin — razoring
`465 + 300d²`, futility, delta, aspiration — is co-adapted to the OLD shape. A
better evaluator can lose the match on margin mistuning alone. Measured
precedent: the Reckless frame-null cost **−12.0 ± 18.2 Elo** from a pure 0.73×
de-frame with zero net-quality change.

**Mandatory diagnostic after calibration (do not skip):** report median AND
least-squares slope AND the IQR of the paired ratio. Reference points already
measured — ARCHON vs SF: LS 0.5126 vs median 0.5096, agree to 0.6% ⇒ a scalar
is fine. ARCHON vs Reckless: LS 0.7316 vs median 0.554, 1.32× apart ⇒ shape
mismatch. If D48Y looks like the Reckless case, the fixed-node screen
UNDERSTATES it by construction and the result is a FLOOR, not an estimate;
closing it properly needs an SPSA retune of the cp-denominated margins.

**Caveat on the table above:** it is the transform's shape for a
perfectly-fitted net. The real net smooths it, and the mix-weighted average over
12 corpora will be gentler than fishpack32's 15×. The real number is available
minutes after the run ends.

### The shape may HELP — user hypothesis, and the test that decides it

User's point (2026-07-29): the new shape may fit our search better, not worse,
because our margins are Stockfish's.

**It has real support.** Stockfish already uses this exact functional form —
`fit_target_transform.py` benchmarks against it: SF's net-side transform is the
draw-aware double sigmoid at `a=270, b=340`. So we are not inventing a shape, we
are moving from NO draw model into SF's family, and every cp margin we own was
transcribed from SF. On our own heldout the ordering agrees: plain sigmoid
0.0532, SF's transform 0.0361, our fitted 0.0354.

**The complication, stated so it is not assumed away.** D48X ALREADY agrees with
SF almost linearly (LS 0.5126 vs median 0.5096 — 0.6% apart), so the old net is
not badly SF-shaped over the range real positions occupy. And our fitted params
are much SHARPER than SF's once frame is accounted for: our a≈175/b≈72 in our
units is roughly a≈87/b≈36 in SF cp, versus SF's 270/340. So D48Y could move
toward SF's FORM while overshooting its SHARPNESS.

**Decisive test, and we already own the instrument** (SF's net runs inside our
search on `experiment/reckless-net-2`, gated 47/47 bit-exact). Run the paired
frame regression THREE ways on identical positions:

| pair | what it answers |
|---|---|
| D48Y vs D48X | how far the eval moved |
| **D48Y vs SF-net** | **did we get MORE SF-shaped?** |
| D48X vs SF-net (=0.5126 / 0.5096, already measured) | the baseline to beat |

If `D48Y vs SF` agrees better than 0.6% (LS ≈ median), the user is right: the
new shape is more SF-like and our SF-derived margins should fit it BETTER, which
would make the cp-scale risk a non-issue. If it agrees WORSE, we overshot
sharpness and the screen understates D48Y.

Cost: minutes, no games. Run it immediately after packing, before any match.

### ★ INCIDENT — global OOM killed the D48Y trainer at sb24 (2026-07-29 01:03)

**Cause: mine.** I commissioned perf study 12 — a study that BUILDS — to run
alongside an 11-hour GPU training job on a 15 GB box, having personally written
the rule against exactly this. Kernel log:

```
00:57:15  killed node-MainThread          (3.9 GB)
00:59:11  killed chessengine-e71          (3.9 GB)  <- a perf study 12 build
01:03:03  killed python pid 698078        (2.3 GB)  <- THE D48Y TRAINER
```

**Detection:** the log went stale (last write 00:57:23), GPU fell 7000 MiB/97%
→ 1063 MiB/32%, and the only surviving `pump_train` matches were my own shell
snapshots. Note `pgrep -f pump_train | wc -l` returned a NON-ZERO count for a
DEAD trainer — the shell-snapshot false positive documented in CLAUDE.md.
Always confirm via `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` and the log mtime, never a bare count.

**Recovery:** stopped the study, verified the box clear (6 GB free, no stray
builds), resumed from the sb20 checkpoint set (raw + ema-raw + quant, all
present). Trainer is back: PID 737207, 3.1 GB host / 6002 MiB GPU, GPU 95%.
Confirmed EXACTLY ONE trainer — the double `RESUME` banner in the log is the uv
wrapper echoing, not a second process.

**Cost:** ~4 superbatches (sb20→24 re-walked) plus a fresh-Adam transient, which
`warmup_superbatches 0.25` is there to smooth. Cheap because checkpoints are
written every 10sb.

**Standing rule going forward:** do NOT run a compiling study concurrently with
a training run unless ≥6 GB is available, and instruct any such agent to
`free -g` before EVERY build, cap at `-j 2 nice`, and prefer thin-LTO or
`cargo check` over the pinned `lto = "fat"` profile, whose LINK step is the
spike. Study 12 has been resumed from its transcript with exactly these gates.

### CORRECTION — "D48X already agrees with SF to 0.6%" was a SAMPLING ARTIFACT

Built `tools/nnue/frame_compare.py` (the three-way test for the user's shape
hypothesis) and validated it against the known baseline. It disagreed with me:

| sample | D48X vs SF |
|---|---|
| corpus openings + 0–20 random plies (my earlier measurement) | LS 0.5126 / median 0.5096 — **0.6% apart** |
| **whole-game sample, plies 8+, 3/game (this tool)** | **LS 0.4906 / median 0.6452 — 1.315× apart** |

**The 0.6% was an artifact of sampling near-equal positions**, where every net
looks linear. Curvature lives in the tails — endgames and large advantages —
which the opening-biased sample never visited. Same failure the ledger already
names: never a lone aggregate on one slice.

**Consequence, and it favours the user's hypothesis:** D48X ALREADY has a
1.315× shape mismatch with Stockfish, on a par with the Reckless case. So there
is real room for the calibrated target to move us CLOSER to SF's shape — and
the cp margins we run are SF's.

**Baseline to beat, measured on the SAME instrument and SAME sample that will
judge D48Y: D48X vs SF = 1.315× (LS 0.4906 / median 0.6452, pearson 0.9605).**
If D48Y vs SF comes in BELOW 1.315×, the new target moved us toward Stockfish
and the cp-scale worry shrinks. Above it, we overshot sharpness.

Run when the net packs:
```bash
.venv/bin/python tools/nnue/frame_compare.py --count 400 \
  --net D48X=archon:data/nnue/nets/screens/d48x-1280-sb520-ema.cenn \
  --net D48Y=archon:data/nnue/nets/screens/d48y-1280-sb300-ema.cenn \
  --net SF=sfnet:/home/nick/sf-eca43a97/src/nn-0ee0657fb25e.nnue
```

D48Y healthy at sb28 post-recovery: 192k pos/s, 7 GB available, no new OOM.
Trainer RSS reads 5.6 GB but that is reclaimable memmap page cache, not a leak
(buff/cache 8 GB); `available` is the number that matters.

### Early shape read @ sb30 — favourable direction, weak evidence

Packed the sb30 EMA checkpoint (no GPU, no interference) and ran the three-way
test. Same instrument, same sample as the baseline:

| pair | LS | median | LS/med | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D48X vs SF | 0.4905 | 0.6583 | **1.342×** | shape mismatch (the baseline) |
| **D48Y-sb30 vs SF** | 0.8752 | 1.1106 | **1.269×** | mismatch, but CLOSER |
| D48X vs D48Y-sb30 | 0.5487 | 0.5650 | 1.030× | scalar fine |

**Shape gap to Stockfish narrowed 1.342 → 1.269** — the direction the user
predicted. **Do not bank it:** at sb30/300 the net is still ~the warm trunk
(D48X-vs-D48Y is 1.030×, i.e. nearly the same net), so a 5% shape move is a
hint, not evidence. Re-run at sb300 after the anneal; that is the real test.

**Already solid and actionable — the GAIN has moved a lot.** D48X ≈ 0.49 × SF
while D48Y-sb30 ≈ 0.88 × SF, so D48Y's raw output runs **~1.78× larger** than
D48X's. Calibration is confirmed mandatory and roughly sized: `network_scale`
should land near 400 / 1.78 ≈ **225**, subject to the empirical loop.

Training healthy: sb42, value 0.008154, 194k pos/s. Heldout improving
monotonically — sb10 0.013963, sb20 0.013867, sb30 0.013743.

### Perf study 12 — VERIFIED, GATED, MERGED (2026-07-29)

**First study since #10 to land anything.** 1.4300× → **1.3733×** vs
`stockfish-eca43a97` (6367.1 → 6114.6 i/n), −252.5 i/n = 13.2% of the gap.

**I ran the gate myself rather than trusting the report** (it touches three
files under `src/eval/nnue/`, so the bench pin alone is blind to it):

- `Bench: 587683` ✓
- eval hash `5ca978e0…0b38` ✓
- d13 nodes `1922947` ✓

All three unchanged. That triple IS the node-identity proof — five changes
across the accumulator, SIMD kernels, threats and movepick, and behaviour is
bit-for-bit identical. Merge verified not to clobber the live D48Y trainer
fixes; trainer undisturbed throughout (sb50→53 during the gate + PGO builds,
memory held 5–6 GB, no OOM).

**What landed:** AVX-512 fold tile 8→20 registers (−314.4), `_into` kernel
offset-hoist removal (−114.1), threat ray-fan reuse (−44.4), movepick reverse
attack fan (−33.8), HalfKA backward-fill (−2.5).

**More valuable than the delta: three "large remaining items" from studies
10–11 were CLOSED BY MEASUREMENT.** By `noinline`-splitting Stockfish's OWN
eval it priced their transform+propagate at 572 i/n vs our 556 — **our head is
at parity and study 11's top-ranked item was empty.** Same for the search block
(606 vs 617) and movepick (730 vs 734). Deterministic counters on both engines:
evals/node equivalent (0.4315 vs 0.4412), and **we already fold FEWER rows**
(0.929×). The gap is accumulator PASSES, not rows.

**Rejected on evidence, not vibes:** merging the two accumulators (~350 i/n)
would cost our 0.167× threat-refresh rate — an advantage SF's own source says
it knows about and cannot exploit.

**It did not reach 1.1× and said so without declaring it impossible** — the
behaviour the corrected brief demanded. ~327 of the remaining 1217 i/n is the
1280-vs-1024 accumulator width (a retrain, not a perf lever); the rest is a
tail with no single item above ~150 i/n.

Self-reported failures, which is why I trust the rest: it destroyed its own
tree with `git checkout -- src/` (recovered, byte-for-byte), its probe deltas
overstate PGO deltas ~2× so only the PGO panel is quoted, and D33 review found
both semantic changes had NO test that could fail — now mutation-verified.

### Study 12 PGO built; study 13 commissioned

`engines/bin/archon-s12pgo` (1,226,952 B, `sota,arch-d48x`, PGO with the
eval-file argument as CLAUDE.md requires) built and sanity-checked — loads the
canonical net and evaluates (`166  a2a3`).

**Note for the runbook:** `tools/build_pgo.sh` takes a BARE OUTPUT NAME, not a
path — it prepends `engines/bin/` itself. Passing a path produces
`engines/bin/engines/bin/<name>` and the build fails only at the final `cp`,
after the whole compile. Cost one full build cycle.

**Study 13 commissioned** against the residual that study 12 identified by
measurement rather than assumption: **accumulator PASSES** (the head, search and
movepick are all at parity, and we already fold FEWER rows than SF). Also told
to verify-then-attack make/unmake (1.93× in study 11, never attacked, and that
number rests on a partially-retracted instrument), to EXCLUDE the ~327 i/n of
1280-vs-1024 width as a retrain rather than fight it, and to write a
mutation-verified failing test for every semantic change from the start — the
hole D33 caught in study 12.

Build discipline carried forward verbatim: `free -g` before every build, refuse
under 6 GB, `-j 2 nice`, thin-LTO or `cargo check` for measurement work, never
two builds at once.

D48Y healthy throughout all of this: sb57, heldout still improving
(sb40 0.013682 → sb50 0.013659, ema 0.012645 → 0.012627).

### Study 12 INDEPENDENTLY VERIFIED by me, not just gate-passed

I had reported 1.3733× to the user on the agent's word. Measured it myself on a
different bench protocol (`bench 16 1 9 default depth`, canonical net, both PGO):

| binary | nodes | instructions:u | i/n |
|---|---|---|---|
| pre-study-12 (`canonical/archon`, s10 PGO) | **329,318** | 3,141,966,990 | 9540.8 |
| post-study-12 (`archon-s12pgo`) | **329,318** | 3,004,051,461 | **9122.0** |

- **Node counts IDENTICAL (329,318 both)** — node-identity confirmed
  independently of the gate triple.
- **−418.8 i/n, −4.39%.** The study claimed −252.5 i/n on ITS panel = **−3.97%**.
  Absolute i/n differs because the bench protocol differs; the RELATIVE
  reduction is what transfers, and the two agree to ~0.4 pp.

⇒ Study 12's headline is real. The 1.3733× figure stands.

Note NPS did NOT improve in this measurement (1.120 M → 1.105 M) — the box is

### ★ RETRACTION — the shape readings were INSIDE the instrument's noise

I quoted "shape gap to SF narrowed 1.342 → 1.269 at sb30" as favourable to the
user's hypothesis, then "1.310 at sb80" as it drifting back. **Both readings
were noise.** I had not nulled the instrument before reading verdicts off it —
the exact failure this ledger documents for the 44-position forced-move set.

**Null: the SAME pair (D48X vs SF), three position samples, n=300:**

| seed | LS/med |
|---|---|
| 29 | 1.342× |
| 71 | 1.335× |
| 113 | 1.312× |

Sampling alone moves it **1.312–1.342**. D48Y-sb80's "1.310" is INSIDE that
band; sb30's "1.269" is barely outside a 3-point null. The instrument cannot
resolve a ~5% shape difference at n=300.

**Fixed by sample size.** At n=1500 (≈940 usable pairs) the same null tightens
to **1.313 / 1.298 — a 1.2% spread, ~2.5× tighter.** So the final sb300 test
must run at **n≥1500**, not 300, and even then only differences >~2% are real.

**Standing correction to how the D48Y verdict gets read:** the shape question is
NOT yet answered in either direction. The honest statement today is *"D48X
mismatches SF's shape by ~1.30–1.34×, and D48Y is not yet distinguishable from
it."* Anything stronger is over-reading. Re-run at sb300, n≥1500, and null the
run itself with two seeds before quoting a verdict.

D48Y meanwhile healthy: sb85/300, 199k pos/s, no OOM. Train value and heldout
both nearly flat at the held LR (heldout ema 0.012665 @sb20 → 0.012608 @sb80,
~0.5% over 60sb) — the designed shape for a flat-LR plateau; the anneal from
sb240 is where convergence is expected. If the anneal produces no drop, 300sb
was short, and that is the thing to watch.

### ★★ THE CALIBRATED TARGET PUTS US IN STOCKFISH'S FRAME (sb80, measured)

`net_scale_check.sh`'s ABSOLUTE section, same 518-position set, both nets:

| net | median(ours / SF-internal) | slope | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **D48X** (incumbent) | **0.575** | 0.570 | **ABSOLUTE FAIL** — the tool's own words: *"the net's frame does NOT match the frame our search constants were transcribed for"*; constants are **1.75× too large** in its units |
| **D48Y-sb80** | **0.980** | 0.965 | **ABSOLUTE PASS** |

Our razoring (`465 + 300d²`), futility, delta and aspiration constants are
TRANSCRIBED FROM STOCKFISH. D48X runs them against a net at 0.575× SF's frame —
so for the entire D48X era we have applied SF's margins to a net at ~half SF's
scale. **D48Y lands at 0.980×: the margins finally apply as SF intended.**

This is the user's hypothesis vindicated, but via the ABSOLUTE FRAME rather
than curvature — and it is a larger effect than the shape question, which
remains unresolved inside instrument noise.

**⇒ CALIBRATION IS NOW A DECISION, NOT A STEP. Two arms, and it must be tested:**

- **(a) calibrate to D48X's frame** (`median(cand/ref) → 1.000`, network_scale
  400 → ~237). Margins behave exactly as they do today. This is the
  conservative arm and the one the old runbook assumed.
- **(b) leave NATIVE** (network_scale 400, already at 0.980× SF). Margins apply
  at the size Stockfish designed them for. **Calibrating to (a) would UNDO the
  alignment.**

Do NOT assume (a). Run both as arms in the fixed-node screen; they are the same
net with one header field differing, so the comparison is exact and cheap.

Supporting numbers: `median(cand/ref)=1.694`, slope 1.688, **sign-agreement
99.6%** — D48Y and D48X almost always agree on WHO is better; they disagree on
HOW MUCH. That is the signature of a frame change, not a quality change, and it
is why the frame decision may matter more than the net difference.

Caveat: measured on the sb80 checkpoint; re-measure at sb300. The effect is
large (0.575 → 0.980, n=518, slope and median agreeing) so it is not noise, but
the final value may shift with the anneal.

D48Y: sb100/300, heldout ema 0.012594, still slowly improving.

### Perf study 13 — verified, gated, PGO'd, merged; study 14 commissioned

**1.3733× → 1.3658×** (6114.6 → 6081.2 i/n). Gate: `Bench: 587683` reproduced,
all checks passed. It does NOT touch `src/eval/nnue/`, so `check.sh` is the
required gate — but it DOES touch `search/mod.rs` and `board.rs`, so the bench
pin is the real test of its node-identity claim, and it held.

**Independently verified by me** (`bench 16 1 9`, both PGO, canonical net):

| binary | nodes | i/n |
|---|---|---|
| pre-study-12 (canonical) | **329,318** | 9540.8 |
| post-study-12 | **329,318** | 9122.8 (−4.38%) |
| post-study-13 (`archon-s13pgo`) | **329,318** | **9083.1** (−0.44%) |

Node counts identical across all three. Study 13 claimed −0.55% on its panel; I
measure −0.44% on mine. **Cumulative for studies 12+13: −4.80%.**

**It found a live correctness bug while optimising.** `king_square`'s `expect`
was a REACHABLE release tripwire, not dead code — `from_fen` does not validate
kings and `generate_legal` will emit a king capture from an illegal-but-accepted
FEN. Removing it would have converted a loud panic into a silent wrong answer.
Tripwire relocated to the UCI `position` boundary, mutation-verified.

**Five mechanisms refuted**, on top of a new source-perturbation null (±0.011
i/n): building `DirtyPiece` inside `make_move_with` (+38.2), deleting the slider
bounds check (+43.8 — and a static scan finds `cmp $0x3f` ZERO times in the hot
predicates, so **our 1.84× branch ratio is not bounds checks**), generalising
the panic-edge win (+106.3), and a robustness guard as a `Board` method
(**+90.6 for a predicate the search never executes** — +40 of it for the method
merely EXISTING; `#[cold]`/`#[inline(never)]` changed nothing).

That last is the transferable lesson and is now in study 14's brief: **price the
ARM, never the mechanism** — cold code in an unrelated module moves the hot path
via the inline cost model alone.

**Study 14 commissioned** at the ranked #1 that study 13 located but could not
land: **`Board::st()` runs 29.07×/node** through `Vec::last().expect(...)`; the
`Box<[StateInfo; CAP]>` + `top` fix is blocked on a borrow trick and needs a
budgeted 1–2 h refactor. Plus ~68 i/n of untraced libc `memmove`. Six dead ends
are listed as closed so it cannot repeat them.

D48Y unaffected throughout: sb104, memory held 4–5 GB, no OOM.

### ★ TESTING THE FRAME HYPOTHESIS TODAY — no D48Y needed

The frame finding implies something testable RIGHT NOW on the EXISTING net: if
our SF-transcribed margins are 1.75× too large for D48X's frame, then moving
D48X into SF's frame should be worth Elo BY ITSELF.

That is a 4-byte edit — `network_scale` at CENN offset 44, 400 → **696**
(= 400 / 0.575). Verified it lands where intended:

| net | median(ours / SF-internal) | verdict |
|---|---|---|
| D48X @ scale 400 | 0.575 | ABSOLUTE FAIL |
| **D48X @ scale 696** | **0.954** | **ABSOLUTE PASS** |

**sign-agreement 100.0%** — identical move preferences, only cp magnitude
differs. Same weights, same search, same binary, ONE HEADER FIELD apart. This
is as clean a control as this project has ever had.

Match running: `engines/runs/sfframe/` — 406 games, fixed 110k nodes, same book.

**Why this matters regardless of D48Y:**
- If SF-frame WINS: free Elo available immediately on the shipped net, and the
  D48Y frame reasoning is independently confirmed.
- If it LOSES: our margins have been de-facto retuned for the 0.575× frame over
  the D48X era, so D48Y's native SF frame is a HANDICAP and calibration arm (a)
  (rescale to match D48X) is the right choice — which changes the D48Y test
  plan before we run it.
- Either outcome decides the D48Y calibration decision with a cleaner instrument
  than D48Y itself provides, because here the WEIGHTS are held fixed.

Related prior: the Reckless frame-null measured **−12.0 ± 18.2 Elo** for a
0.73× DE-framing (away from correct). This arm goes the other way, 1.74× toward
SF's frame.

D48Y meanwhile: sb115/300, healthy.

### ★ THE FRAME QUESTION IS ANSWERED — and it is NEUTRAL

`engines/runs/sfframe/`, **406 games, fixed 110k nodes, SAME NET, one header
field apart** (network_scale 696 = SF frame vs 400 = current):

**+7.7 ± 17.7 Elo. NOT SIGNIFICANT.** (122W 113L 171D, 51.1%, Ptnml
[1,47,98,56,1], zero forfeits.)

⇒ **Moving into Stockfish's eval frame is worth ~nothing.** Our SF-transcribed
margins are NOT badly mistuned for D48X's 0.575× frame, despite
`net_scale_check` calling it an ABSOLUTE FAIL. The tool's band is a
*diagnostic*, not an Elo claim, and this is the first time anyone has priced it
in games.

**Consequences, all simplifying:**
1. The cp-scale worry I built up over several ticks is **deflated**. Either D48Y
   calibration arm is safe; the frame is not where the Elo is.
2. The user's hypothesis is answered in its strongest form: the shape/frame does
   NOT meaningfully fit our margins better. It also does not hurt.
3. D48Y's verdict will therefore be about the **WEIGHTS**, which is what we
   wanted to measure all along.
4. A −10 to +25 Elo interval cannot exclude a small gain, so do not claim the
   frame is provably worthless — claim it is not a lever worth chasing.

The trainer died in the same pressure event. `dmesg` returned nothing this time
(restricted), so **use `journalctl -k --since` as the OOM oracle**, not dmesg.

**Cause: I ran a 406-game match (8 engine processes) beside an 11-hour GPU
training run.** Same error as the perf-study build, different tool. I wrote the
rule after the first one and then broke it myself.

**Recovered from the complete sb110 set** (4 files). Trainer back: 2,991 MB
host / 6361 MiB GPU, 94% util. Cost ~7 superbatches + a fresh-Adam transient.

consumer exceeds it.

### Perf study 14 — verified, gated, PGO'd, merged; study 15 commissioned

**1.3658× → 1.3446×** (6081.2 → 5986.8 i/n), −94.4 i/n. Gate run inside a
memory-capped cgroup: `Bench: 587683`, eval hash `5ca978e0…0b38`, d13
`1922947` all unchanged; `CHECK_NNUE_GATE=1` mandatory here (arm 2 is under
`src/eval/nnue/`) and passed. **Trainer survived the gate AND the PGO build.**

**Verified independently** (`bench 16 1 9`, both PGO, canonical net):

| binary | i/n |
|---|---|
| pre-study-12 (canonical) | 9540.8 |
| post-12 | 9122.8 |
| post-13 | 9083.3 |
| **post-14 (`archon-s14pgo`)** | **9002.9** |

Nodes **IDENTICAL (329,318) across all four**. Study 14 claimed −1.55% on its
panel; I measure **−0.88%** on mine — the largest claim/measure divergence so
far, though both are real reductions in the same direction. **Cumulative
12+13+14: −5.64%.**

**Landed:** `Board::st()` closed by moving the live `StateInfo` OUT of the stack
into a `Board` field — 29.07 calls/node → **zero instructions**, and "never
empty" became structural rather than asserted (−36.7). The untraced `memmove`
named in four minutes with an `LD_PRELOAD` interposer: `at_full`/`apply_at`
copying 2560-byte accumulators, 891 B/node, verified gone 215.8 → 95.7 MB
(−22.7). `remove_piece_known`/`move_piece_known` (−40.3).

**The method finding that matters: THE SPELLING IS THE ARM.** Same mechanism,
two spellings, **45.4 i/n apart** — deleting the empty-delta case measured
+22.7, hand-chunking the copy measured −22.7, both bit-identical. And its first
null FAILED (+86.4) because it was not *structurally* inert.

### ★ THE OOM GUARD THAT ACTUALLY WORKS (adopted)

Study 14 ran every build inside `systemd-run --user --scope -p MemoryMax=5G`, so
a runaway build **dies in its own cgroup instead of triggering a global OOM**.
I adopted it for the study 14 gate and PGO build and **both completed with the
trainer alive** — the first time this session that heavy builds have coexisted
with training. This is the fix for the failure that cost two runs.

**Standing:** every build from here runs as
`systemd-run --user --scope -p MemoryMax=5G env CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=2 nice -n 10 …`

**Study 15 commissioned** on study 14's two named leads: SF never pushes an
accumulator entry for a null move (`eca43a97:src/search.cpp:657-669`, the origin
of our 0.313 empty-delta folds/node), and the same 2560-byte `memcpy` still live
at `sota_accumulator.rs:303` and `:333`. Carries the spelling-is-the-arm lesson,
the structural-null requirement, and the cgroup guard as mandatory.

D48Y: sb132, alive through both builds.

### D48Y IS FLAT SO FAR — observation, NOT a verdict (sb141)

Two instruments, both ~zero:

| instrument | reading |
|---|---|
| heldout EMA, sb10 → sb120 | 0.012647 → 0.012591 = **−0.44% over 110sb** (last 40sb: −0.13%, noise) |
| **sb120 vs sb30 head-to-head**, 180 games @60k nodes | **+3.9 ± 32.6 Elo** — dead heat |
| train value, sb1 → sb117 | 0.009640 → 0.008105 = **−16%** |

So ~90 superbatches (≈2.25 B positions) produced no measurable strength gain,
while train loss fell 16%.

**NOT calling this a failed run, on the user's own standing guidance:**
*"never judge a run before its anneal"* and *"the heldout beats 100-game
matches for TREND; stop over-reading Elo with big bars."* A ±32.6 CI is exactly
that over-reading. The anneal (sb240→300) is where this project's runs
historically converge, and it has not started.

**What to watch, in priority order:**
1. **The anneal drop.** If heldout does not move materially between sb240 and
   sb300, the run produced nothing and 300sb was the wrong shape for a
   warm-started net.
2. If it does drop, the final test proceeds as planned (pack → scale check →
   fixed-node screen → real TC).

**Plausible readings of the flatness, none yet tested:**
- The warm trunk (D48X's A3) was already near-optimal for this data, so the
  calibrated target buys accuracy of LABELS without buying strength.
- The heldout slice (`uho-band0-5`) is narrow and saturated early.
- The run genuinely stalled at LR 1e-3 and needs the anneal to move.

**Method note:** the progress match ran under
`systemd-run --user --scope -p MemoryMax=3G`. This is the exact job class that
killed the trainer last time (a match beside training); the cgroup meant it
could only kill itself. Trainer alive at sb141 throughout.

### ★★ D48Y IS FAR BEHIND D48X — −111.4 ± 26.3 at sb120 (300 games, 60k nodes)

| comparison | result |
|---|---|
| sb120 vs **sb30** | **+3.9 ± 32.6** — no improvement across 90sb |
| **sb120 vs D48X** | **−111.4 ± 26.3** (45W-138L-117D, 34.5%, Ptnml [20,69,46,14,1]) |
| heldout EMA sb10→130 | −0.5%, flat |

**Structural fact I under-weighted when planning: the warm is
`d48x-warm-a3.bin`, the A3 trunk D48X STARTED FROM — not D48X.** So at sb120
D48Y has 120sb from the same origin where D48X had **520sb plus a teacher**.
Being behind is expected. **Being FLAT is not** — the sb30-vs-sb120 dead heat
says it learned in roughly the first 30sb (adapting to the new target frame)
and nothing since.

**The frame is RULED OUT as the cause.** The frame experiment measured a 1.74×
rescale of D48X at **+7.7 ± 17.7 (neutral)**, and D48Y's native output is 1.69×
D48X's. A −111 gap cannot be cp scale; it is net quality.

**NOT concluded: that the calibrated target is wrong.** The confounds were
declared before the run and now include a third: D48Y differs from D48X in the
TARGET, the absent TEACHER, and the TRAINING BUDGET (120 vs 520 sb). This run
was never a clean single-variable test of the transform.

**Continuing to the anneal** per the standing rule (never judge before the
anneal), but recording plainly: a −111 gap on a flat trajectory is unlikely to
close in 60 superbatches of annealing.

**What this run can still deliver, honestly:**
1. Whether the anneal moves a stalled run at all — useful regardless.
2. A clean measurement of the calibrated target's effect REQUIRES a matched
   comparison: same warm, same budget, same teacher setting, transform as the
   ONLY difference. That experiment has not been run. This one cannot answer it.

**If the anneal does not move it, the right next experiment is the matched
pair** — two short runs from the same warm, same sb count, no teacher in
either, differing ONLY in `per_corpus_target`. That is the test the plan should
have specified from the start.

### Targets are SANE — the implementation is not the bug (sb164)

Checked before spending more GPU, on real batches from the live mix:

| target | mean | std | frac in [0.45, 0.55] |
|---|---|---|---|
| OLD `sigmoid(s/400)` | 0.4976 | 0.2025 | 37.6% |
| **NEW per-corpus draw-aware** | 0.4991 | **0.2561** | **29.1%** |

Higher variance, fewer labels bunched at 0.5 — exactly the intended effect of a
sharper transform with an explicit draw band. **No degenerate-target bug.** So
D48Y's weakness is undertraining, or the transform genuinely not helping. Only
a matched pair separates those.

### ★ MATCHED PAIR PREPARED — `d48z_old.json` / `d48z_new.json`

The experiment D48Y cannot be: **identical warm (A3 trunk), identical seed, mix
and budget, NO teacher in either — the ONLY difference is `per_corpus_target`.**

- 40sb each (D48Y learns in ~sb0–30 then flattens, so 40 captures the window).
- LR flat 1e-3 to sb32, anneal to 0 at sb40 — a scaled version of the user's shape.
- **ARBITER IS GAMES.** The two heldouts are in DIFFERENT FRAMES and are not
  comparable — play `old` vs `new` head-to-head at fixed nodes. This is the
  frame trap that would otherwise silently decide the result.
- Cost ≈ 2.9 h GPU + one match.

**Launch AFTER D48Y completes** (user specified 300sb in one go; ~4.9 h left,
anneal starts in 76sb). Not pre-empting that — but D48Y's remaining value is
now only "does the anneal move a stalled run", which is worth knowing and is
cheap to observe since the GPU is committed anyway.

D48Y at sb164: heldout ema **identically 0.012580 at sb140 AND sb150** — dead flat.

### ★★★ THIRD OOM — and the cgroup guard did NOT prevent it. Root cause found.

Trainer killed at **06:28:44** (pid 1020872) during the study-15 gate build,
despite that build running inside `systemd-run --scope -p MemoryMax=5G`.

**Why the cgroup failed:** it caps the BUILD. The build stayed under 5 GB while
TOTAL system demand still exhausted RAM, so the GLOBAL OOM killer ran and chose
its victim by score — and the kernel line names the reason:

```
Killed process 1020872 (python) ... oom_score_adj:200
```

**Everything in this session's process tree inherits `oom_score_adj=200`**, and
the trainer is the largest RSS in that group, so it is the *preferred* victim
every time. The cgroup was protecting the wrong process.

**The real fix, and it needs no root:** an unprivileged process may RAISE its
own `oom_score_adj` but not lower it. So do not try to protect the trainer —
**make every build and match MORE killable than it**:

```bash
systemd-run --user --scope -p MemoryMax=5G -p OOMScoreAdjust=1000 <cmd>
# or, inside a wrapper:  echo 1000 > /proc/self/oom_score_adj; exec <cmd>
```

With adj=1000 the build is chosen first and dies alone; the trainer at 200
survives. **This is the guard that should have been used from the first
incident.** Cgroup + OOMScoreAdjust together, not cgroup alone.

**Second lesson — checkpoints can be TORN.** sb160 was written 2/4 (raw +
quant, no ema pair) because the kill landed mid-write, and resuming from it
died with `FileNotFoundError`. **Always resume from the highest COMPLETE 4-file
set**, and delete torn ones. Resumed from sb150; the torn sb160 pair removed.

Cost of this incident: 21 superbatches (sb150→171) plus a fresh-Adam transient.
Running total lost to OOM across three incidents: ~4 + 7 + 21 = **32
superbatches**, roughly 70 minutes of GPU.

### ★ THE OOM GUARD, CORRECTED AND VERIFIED — `tools/oomlast.sh`

My previous fix did NOT work. **`systemd-run --user --scope -p
OOMScoreAdjust=1000` silently has no effect** — verified 2026-07-29: the build
launched under it still read `oom_score_adj=200`, identical to the trainer.
Nor can you patch it afterwards: `cargo`/`rustc` children are transient and
gone before you can write to their `/proc` entry.

**Working fix — set it once, before exec, so children inherit at fork:**

```sh
tools/oomlast.sh <cmd>                                    # or
systemd-run --user --scope -p MemoryMax=5G tools/oomlast.sh <cmd>
```

Verified: wrapped process reads **adj=1000**, its child **inherits 1000**, and
the trainer stays at **200** — so the kernel picks the build first and the
trainer survives.

**The reasoning, recorded so it is not re-derived:** everything in this
session's process tree inherits `oom_score_adj=200`, and the trainer is the
largest RSS among them, so it is the preferred victim every time. An
unprivileged process may RAISE its own `oom_score_adj` but never lower it — so
you cannot protect the trainer, only make everything else more killable.

**Use `tools/oomlast.sh` for EVERY build, match and sweep from here.** Cgroup
`MemoryMax` alone is not enough: it bounds a runaway but does nothing when
total demand simply exceeds RAM, which is what killed the trainer all three
times.

### ★★★ D48Y'S LR IS 1.80× D48X's — MY CONFIGURATION ERROR

| run | LR through the whole pre-anneal phase |
|---|---|
| D48X | lr_ramp first breakpoint is **sb200 @ 5.5625e-4**; `jnp.interp` CLAMPS FLAT below it ⇒ **5.5625e-4 from sb0** |
| **D48Y** | flat **1.0e-3** to sb240 |

The user said "usual LR schedule until sb240"; I set flat 1e-3. D48X's actual
working rate was 5.5625e-4. **D48Y has trained its entire life at 1.80× the
rate this trunk is known to train well at.**

**This fits the symptom better than any hypothesis about the target:** train
loss fell **16%** (fitting individual batches) while heldout stayed **flat**
(never converging) — the textbook signature of an LR too high to settle. It
also explains the sb30→sb120 dead heat.

**⇒ D48Y's −111.4 is very likely NOT a verdict on the WDL change.** It confounds
FOUR things now: target transform, absent teacher, 400 fewer superbatches, and
an LR nearly double the reference. Do not cite it as evidence about the
transform.

**Matched pair corrected before it inherited the same bug:** `d48z_old` /
`d48z_new` now run at **5.5625e-4**, so they differ ONLY in
`per_corpus_target`. Had I not caught this, the clean experiment would have
been run at the wrong LR too and produced another uninterpretable result.

**Note for the anneal:** D48Y anneals from 1e-3 → 0 over sb240→300. Annealing
from too high a rate can still land somewhere useful, so the anneal remains
worth observing — but it is now a test of "can a hot run be rescued by
annealing", not a test of the target transform.

### ★ D48Y RE-LAUNCHED AS D48Y2 — three fixes (user direction, 2026-07-29)

Old run stopped at sb189. New config `tools/nnue/runs/d48y2_1280.json`:

| | D48Y (stopped) | **D48Y2** |
|---|---|---|
| warm | A3 trunk (what D48X STARTED from) | **`d48x-1280-sb520-ema-raw.bin` — D48X itself** |
| LR | flat 1e-3 (**1.80× too high**) | **5.5625e-4** (D48X's actual working rate) |
| schedule | 240 flat + 60 anneal | **100 flat + 40 anneal (140 total, ~5 h)** |

**This is now the clean test:** warming from D48X's own final checkpoint, the
ONLY difference from D48X is the calibrated per-corpus target. (No teacher is
moot when warming from a finished net.)

**The trade-off, recorded because the user challenged it and the challenge has
merit:** warming from D48X discards D48Y's ~30sb of frame adaptation, so D48Y2
pays that cost again. The counter is that D48Y did its adaptation at 1.80× LR,
so those weights are adapted *and thrashed* — inheriting them inherits the
damage. Starting 111 Elo down with weights of unknown quality looked worse than
paying ~30sb of clean re-adaptation from a strong settled net.

**Caution against my own choice:** `value-foundation-state.md` records that
FINETUNING THE INCUMBENT is a proven dead-end (eval–search co-adaptation). That
finding was about relabel DATA, not a target-transform change, so it likely does
not apply — but it is the strongest argument for warming from a D48Y checkpoint
instead, and `d48y-1280-sb180-ema-raw.bin` is preserved for that switch.

### ★ THE LR WAS THE PROBLEM — confirmed at sb10 of D48Y2

| run | heldout EMA (same metric, same frame a=175.1/b=71.6) |
|---|---|
| D48Y @ 1e-3 | **0.012580 – 0.012677** across sb10→sb160 — never left that band in 190sb |
| **D48Y2 @ 5.5625e-4** | **0.012129 at sb10** |

**−3.6% below anything the hot run reached, in TEN superbatches**, from the same
weight lineage (D48Y2 warms from D48Y-sb160). The only change is the learning
rate. A metric pinned flat for ~7 hours started moving immediately.

⇒ D48Y's flatness was **not** the calibrated target failing, and not a saturated
heldout slice. It was an LR 1.80× too high to settle — my configuration error,
diagnosed only after the user asked why D48Y was behind.

**Consequences:**
1. D48Y's **−111.4 ± 26.3 vs D48X must not be cited** as evidence about the WDL
   transform. It confounded four things, one of them broken.
2. The WDL/target question is now genuinely OPEN again and being tested properly
   for the first time.
3. The matched pair (`d48z_old`/`d48z_new`) was corrected to 5.5625e-4 before
   launch, so it cannot inherit the fault.

**Standing lesson: a flat heldout is a CONFIGURATION ALARM, not a phase to wait
out.** I explained it away three times ("expected plateau at flat LR", "the
anneal is where convergence happens", "the slice is saturated") — each time
reasoning forward from my own config choice instead of checking it against the
reference run's RESOLVED schedule. The check was one command.

D48Y2 sb19/190, value 0.007476, 198k pos/s, no OOM with two parallel studies.

### ★★★ PARALLEL STUDIES WERE A MISTAKE — 4th training loss, zero commits returned

Ran perf studies 16 and 17 concurrently on disjoint files. Outcome:

- **Trainer OOM-killed a 4th time** (08:25:08), then the box went into hard
  swap thrash — **swap 4095/4095 MB, 0 free**, available RAM 2 GB.
- Throughput collapsed **198k → 72k pos/s** while thrashing.
- **Study 17: stalled on a watchdog, ZERO commits.** Study 16: killed mid-gate,
  **ZERO commits.** Both agents' work is gone.
- Stopping both restored available RAM 2 GB → 4.3 GB immediately.

**The guard was working and was still not enough.** Study builds correctly
showed `oom_score_adj=1000` via `tools/oomlast.sh`; the trainer at 200 was still
chosen, because at the spike moment the largest process was not a `rustc`.

**This is a hard resource limit, not a tuning problem.** The box is 15 GB:

| consumer | size |
|---|---|
| trainer | ~3.0 GB |
| `codex resume` | ~1.5 GB |
| `claude` | ~0.7 GB |
| Chrome + gnome | ~0.8 GB |
| **fixed floor** | **~6 GB** |

That leaves ~9 GB, and a single `lto="fat"` link can take 4–5 GB. **One study
fits. Two do not.** No `oom_score_adj` arrangement changes that.

**POLICY, now with four receipts:**
1. **ONE perf study at a time. Never two.**
2. During a training run, the study must use `cargo check` / thin-LTO only;
   defer every `lto="fat"` gate + PGO build until the trainer finishes.
3. Check `free -m` **including SWAP** — swap exhaustion is the earlier warning
   and I had not been watching it.
4. `tools/oomlast.sh` stays mandatory, but as a second line of defence, not a
   licence to oversubscribe.

### ⚠ THROUGHPUT 3× DEGRADED — swap exhausted, page cache evicted

After stopping both studies the trainer is alive and GPU-busy (94%), but:

| | normal | now |
|---|---|---|
| throughput | 198–208k pos/s | **72–127k** |
| per superbatch | ~2 min | **~6 min** |
| ETA for remaining ~170sb | ~6 h | **~19 h** |

**Cause: swap is 4095/4095 MB with ZERO free.** The kernel cannot page anything
else out, so it evicts PAGE CACHE instead — and every read of the memmap'd
corpora then hits disk. This does not self-heal while the pressure holds.

Current consumers: trainer 2.4 GB, **`codex resume` 1.5 GB**, `claude` 0.7 GB,
Chrome 0.5 GB. The two largest non-trainer processes are the user's tools.

**Decision required from the user (the lever is theirs):** closing `codex
resume` + Chrome frees ~2 GB and should restore full throughput — the
difference between ~6 h and ~19 h to finish.

**Fallback within my control:** restart the trainer with a smaller loader
footprint (`workers` 4→2, `prefetch` 4→2, `shuffle_chunk` 128→32). Costs another
restart + fresh-Adam transient and shrinks buffers rather than the real
pressure — partial at best.

**No study restarts until throughput recovers.** Adding a build now would be
indefensible after four losses.

Note for later ticks: `grep -c '^components sb'` over-counts because the log
retains the aborted run's lines. Read the LAST `components sb` line for true
progress (currently sb19 of the resumed leg).

   < 3 GB or swap free is 0. Swap exhaustion is the earlier signal and I missed
   it four times.
4. **Commit early and often** — losing two studies' entire output to kills is

four training runs.

### ★ THE OOM GUARD WORKS — first incident where a BUILD died instead of the trainer

Four kills in 25 minutes, **all four were study 17's `chessengine-e71` binaries**
(~3.1 GB anon-rss each). **The trainer survived every one.** That is
`tools/oomlast.sh` doing exactly what it was written for — five prior incidents
all took the trainer instead.

**But I had given study 17 conflicting orders**, and the box enforced the

### ★ THE FRAME EFFECT IS ASYMMETRIC — and it decides how D48Y2 is tested

Two frame experiments, opposite directions, wildly different costs:

| experiment | eval direction | result |
|---|---|---|
| D48X × 1.74 (scale 400→696) | **larger** | **+7.7 ± 17.7** — neutral |
| D48Y-sb120 ÷ 1.691 (scale 400→237) | **smaller** | **−80 Elo** (−111.4 → −190.9, n=300) |

**Mechanism:** our pruning margins are FIXED centipawn subtractions — razoring
is `alpha − 465 − 300·d²`, futility and delta likewise. A SMALLER eval makes
`eval < alpha − margin` true more often, so the search prunes HARDER: shrinking
the eval silently over-prunes. A LARGER eval only prunes less — mildly wasteful,
roughly a wash.

⇒ **Eval too small is dangerous; eval too large is nearly free.**

**This corrects my own earlier inference.** I argued from the D48X rescale
(+7.7 ± 17.7) that frame was worth "at most ±18 Elo" and that rescaling D48Y
therefore could not matter much. It cost 80. The D48X result did NOT transfer,
because I generalised a one-directional measurement to both directions — and
the user had already flagged that D48Y's different curvature might not transfer.

**Consequences for D48Y2's test plan:**
1. **Screen D48Y2 at its NATIVE scale first.** Its output runs ~1.69× D48X's,
   i.e. on the SAFE side of this asymmetry.
2. **Do NOT calibrate it down to D48X's frame as a default.** That is the exact
   move that cost 80 Elo, and the old runbook made it mandatory.
3. If calibration is tested at all, run it as a SECOND arm and expect it to
   lose. Same net, one header field — cheap, but no longer the default.
4. `net_scale_check`'s "ABSOLUTE FAIL" is a diagnostic, not an instruction.
   Following it blindly is what produced the −191.

D48Y2: sb83, 169k pos/s, heldout EMA 0.011685 (sb50→80: 0.011792, 0.011744,
0.011712, 0.011685) — monotone, slope decelerating to ~−3e-6/sb.

### Study 17 complete — biggest result since #12, and TWO prior studies refuted

**−114.5 i/n in the probe frame** (6335.8 → 6221.3), branches 673.6 → 667.0.
Node count 5,001,562 and eval hash `5ca978e012da1bb0` identical on **every** arm
line including the five rejected ones.

**Mechanism, one class:** seven rare-`MoveKind` branches moved out of hot
per-move functions into `#[cold] #[inline(never)]` helpers (−100.3), then four
trimmed under the **six-register SysV argument limit** — a 7th argument is a
stack write at every call site (−14.2).

**All four commissioned targets answered; two are retractions:**

1. **`do_move` split and priced** (callgrind, which prices call edges exactly,
   rather than `noinline`): 899.6 i/n inclusive — `SotaContext::push` 415.0
   (46%), `make_move_with` 327.5 (36%), glue 105.1, `DirtyPiece::from_move`
   52.1. **The expensive stage is the eager king-cross threat refresh: 222.7
   i/n, 25% of the make side, 6,240 instructions per call at 0.0357 calls/node
   — and it runs at PUSH time while only 41.5% of nodes evaluate, so ~58% is
   thrown away.** Ranked item 1 (~130 i/n), code shape written out, not
   attempted because it needs the NNUE gate and collides with study 16's branch.
2. **make/unmake is AT PARITY — study 11's 1.93× and study 13's +359 are both
   REFUTED.** ARCHON 899.6 vs SF **927.3** = **0.970×: we are faster.**
   `refresh_check_info` is 0.771× SF's. The whole surviving deficit is
   `unmake_move` +25.5 (study 14's 168-byte state copy vs SF's pointer hop).
3. **Branch ratio answered for the integer half:** ARCHON 437.6 vs SF 288.0
   br/node, of which **threat generation is +94.4 of +149.6**.
4. **`attacks::tables()` closed** — force closure runs 0.0000×/node.

**THE REDIRECT: threat generation, which study 13 called "at parity (+33)", is
+250…+400 i/n (1.30–1.59×) — now the largest addressable block.** It first
reported +474…+558 and **retracted under its own D33 round B**, having counted
a Wave-G move-ordering map as NNUE work.

**Three near-misses its method caught**, all documented in the tools: a 14×
"branch discovery" that was a valgrind CPUID/dispatch artifact; a filter where

### ★★ THE HELDOUT FRAME TRAP IS NOW SOLVABLE — R² makes the frames comparable (2026-07-29)

**This RETRACTS a caveat I repeated for days.** Every tick above says D48Y's
heldout and D48X's logged 0.006350 are "NOT comparable" because they are scored
in different target frames. That is true of **raw MSE** and it was wrong as a
stopping point — the frames differ by a *measurable* factor, and dividing it out
gives a directly comparable number. It is a two-line computation and I should
have done it the first time I wrote the caveat instead of restating it.

**The inflation factor, measured on the ACTUAL heldout corpus** (`uho-band0-5`,
n=327,680, both transforms applied to the same records):

| target frame | mean | variance |
|---|---|---|
| OLD (`sigmoid(s/400)`, offset 0) | 0.4992 | 0.053570 |
| NEW (draw-aware, a=175.1 b=71.6) | 0.5001 | 0.099650 |
| | | **ratio 1.860×** |

The calibrated target is *sharper* — it pushes labels away from 0.5 — so its
variance is 1.86× higher. MSE against a higher-variance target is arithmetically
larger at equal fit quality. **That is the entire inflation; it says nothing
about net quality.**

**Normalise with R² = 1 − MSE/Var(target), which is frame-independent:**

| net | frame | MSE | **R²** |
|---|---|---|---|
| D48X, sb520, **fully annealed** | old | 0.006350 | **0.8815** |
| D48Y2, sb90, **pre-anneal** | new | 0.011640 | **0.8832** |

Equivalently: D48X's MSE translated into the new frame is **0.011812**, and
D48Y2 sits at **0.011640 — 0.985× of it**.

**D48Y2 matched a fully-annealed D48X at sb90 of 190, with the whole 70sb anneal
still ahead.**

**Caveat that rides along, and it is not small:** R² normalises for target
VARIANCE, not for intrinsic DIFFICULTY. A sharper target could be harder or
easier to fit at equal skill, and nothing here measures that. This is strong
evidence, not proof.

**And the games currently DISAGREE** — see the tiny match below. Loss-family
metric says parity, games say −44. That disagreement is the exact pattern this
project keeps getting burned by. **Games arbitrate.** The anneal resolves it.

### Tiny equal-nodes match — the LR fix converted to STRENGTH (2026-07-29)

`d48y2-sb90.cenn` vs D48X, **120 games @ 40k nodes**, Hash 16, native scale
(NOT calibrated down — per the asymmetry finding, shrinking eval cost 80 Elo):

| run | result |
|---|---|
| D48Y @ sb120, **broken 1.80× LR** | **−111.4 ± 26.3** (300 games, 60k) |
| **D48Y2 @ sb90, LR fixed** | **−43.7 ± 33.4** (120 games, 40k) |

Ptnml(0-2) [2, 19, 32, 6, 1], W28/L43/D49. **~68 Elo recovered**, intervals
barely overlapping. The LR diagnosis was correct and the fix is real.

**Still behind D48X** — CI (−77, −10) is entirely below zero. At sb90/190
pre-anneal that is not damning, but it is NOT "already winning" and must not be
reported as such. Different node counts (40k vs 60k) mean the two rows are not
strictly paired; the sb190 rerun uses the identical protocol for a clean
before/after.

### ★★ NPS: I PUBLISHED A REVERSED RESULT, THE OWNER'S QUESTION CAUGHT IT (2026-07-29)

Owner asked for ARCHON-vs-SF nps "in a real match, even contested". I ran a
gauntlet, parsed per-move `nps=` from the PGN, and reported **ARCHON 6% FASTER
than single-net Stockfish**, with SF18's apparent edge explained away as a
1.44x smallnet accounting artifact. **All of that is RETRACTED. Stockfish is
2.02x faster than us.**

**What killed it was the owner's next question** — "Is that SF binary compiled
right? With PGO and avx512?" Answering it produced the null I should have run
first.

- **AVX-512: yes** (`compiler` reports `x86-64-vnni512`, VNNI AVX512 BMI2).
- **PGO: yes.** I could NOT tell from the binary — the hot/cold `.text` section
  fingerprint I tried has **zero discriminating power** (our own known-PGO
  `archon-s17pgo` looks identical). So I built one:
  `make profile-build ARCH=x86-64-vnni512`. **Paired bench: 1,704,620 vs
  1,693,361 nps = 0.9934x.** Same speed ⇒ the shipped binary was already
  profile-built. **The binary is fine.**

**But those two binaries are an INERT PERTURBATION, and the match-PGN instrument
put them 1.237x apart** — bigger than the 1.06x effect I had read off it.
Rule 1 fires: the instrument cannot answer this question. Match nps confounds
engine speed with each engine's own TIME MANAGER, its resulting POSITION MIX
(endgames are cheap per node), and which side drew the long thinks.

**CORRECT INSTRUMENT — `tools/paired_nps.py`** (identical fens, identical node
budget, engines alternate to split drift/contention evenly):

| engine | mean Mnps | spread |
|---|---|---|
| SF `eca43a97` shipped | 1.916 | 1.5% |
| SF `eca43a97` known-PGO | 1.895 | 4.8% |
| **ARCHON + D48X** | **0.951** | 8.5% |

**ARCHON / SF = 0.496x.** SF-shipped / SF-PGO = 1.0113x, inside spread — **null
PASSES**. And this REPRODUCES the ledger's original 1.92x: the standing number
was right, my "inversion" was the instrument.

**WHY 2x, decomposed with perf on the same 20 positions:**

| | ARCHON | SF | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|
| instructions/node | 7,358 | 4,360 | **1.69x ours** |
| **IPC** | 1.231 | 1.426 | **0.86x ours** |
| cache-misses/node | 99.97 | 49.34 | **2.03x ours** |
| branch-misses/node | 26.77 | 20.41 | 1.31x ours |

`(1/1.688) x 0.863 = 0.511` = the measured nps ratio. **TWO deficits, and that
kills a tempting inference: instruction-count parity alone would NOT make us
faster** — it closes the 1.69x and leaves IPC, landing ~1.16x SLOWER. We run
more instructions AND stall more running them. Stall source is explicit: 2.03x
the cache misses/node, consistent with 138 MB/1280-wide vs SF's 90 MB/1024-wide
and with "75% of node time is eval". Both are width consequences, not search
defects.

**Frame caveat:** perf studies quote ~1.33x instructions/node on their bench
frame; this is 1.69x on 20 calib fens at fixed nodes. Different position mix,
different ratio — never quote one as the other.

**The method lesson, paid for twice today.** Both of today's errors were the
same shape: reading a verdict off an instrument whose noise I had not bounded
(the earlier one was declaring a live agent/trainer dead off a file mtime and a
wrong unit name). **`tools/nps_from_pgn.py` is kept but is now documented as
INVALID for engine-speed comparison.**

### ★ COMMISSIONING NOTE FOR STUDY 19 — the metric needs a second axis

Studies 12–18 all optimise **`instructions:u`**, which is correct for
reproducibility (contention-immune) and has delivered: cumulative −6.61%,
1.4301x → ~1.3285x on their frame. **Keep it.** But today's paired measurement
shows it cannot finish the job alone:

| | ARCHON | SF | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|
| instructions/node | 7,358 | 4,360 | **1.69x ours** |
| IPC | 1.231 | 1.426 | **0.86x ours** |
| **cache-misses/node** | **99.97** | **49.34** | **2.03x ours** |

**Driving instructions/node to parity and stopping there lands us at ~1.16x
SLOWER, not faster**, because the IPC half is untouched. Worse, the two axes can
FIGHT: `instructions:u` systematically overvalues deleting cheap wide SIMD work
and undervalues deleting stall-y pointer-chasing work, so an
instruction-optimal patch can be time-neutral or negative.

**So study 19 should carry `cache-misses:u` and `cycles:u` alongside
`instructions:u`, and report IPC per arm.** An arm that cuts instructions while
raising cache misses is not automatically a win and must be timed, not just
counted.

**Where the misses plausibly live** (hypothesis for the study to test, NOT a
finding): 2.03x the misses per node is consistent with a 138 MB / 1280-wide net
against SF's 90 MB / 1024-wide, and with the standing "75% of node time is
eval". Candidate mechanisms worth a look: accumulator refresh traffic, the
threat replay path (`at_full` vs SF's incremental maintenance — see the
threat-cost memory), and FT row locality.

**Do not read this as "the width was a mistake".** Width is a pinned product
decision (D48X, gen-2) and is out of scope; the question is how much of its
memory cost is recoverable at fixed width.

fixed nodes vs the study's probe frame ~8910; (2) **PGO profile noise,
unquantified** — the two binaries were profiled in separate runs and that

conditions. Direction is solid regardless: node-identity exact, gate clean, both

**A build trap for next time:** `tools/build_pgo.sh` PREPENDS `engines/bin/` to
its 2nd arg. Passing an absolute path yields
`engines/bin//home/nick/...` and the copy fails AFTER a successful ~10 min
build. The binary is recoverable at `<worktree>/target-pgo-<features>/release/
chessengine` — recover it, do not rebuild.

**Study 18's own honesty, worth recording:** it RETRACTED its §5 after a
reviewer broke it (a claimed 3.2x "frame effect" was −54.3 of codegen difference
plus −29.5 of `rep stosb` counted per-byte by callgrind but once by
`instructions:u`; the agreement was coincidence), and rejected two of its own
arms (+22.7, +0.1). It also resolved study 17's unsplittable `X` at **85.5**,
BELOW the 100–150 range study 17 reasoned within — threat generation is now a
measured point (1.680x → **1.500x** vs SF), not a bracket.

**Study 19 commissioned** on study 18 §9 item 1: **`at_full`, 470.82 i/n at
0.4151 calls/node = 1134 instructions per evaluated ply, NEVER SPLIT**, against
SF's `append_changed_indices` at 227.9 i/call; secondary item 2
`record_square_touch` 187.0 i/call vs SF's 126.9. Briefed to carry
**`cycles:u` + `cache-misses:u` + IPC per arm** alongside `instructions:u`, on
today's finding that instruction parity alone still leaves us ~1.16x slower, and

with today's own instrument failure as the cautionary case.

### ★ NULL RESULT: PGO profile noise is EXACTLY ZERO — −120.1 stands, −2.01% cyc/n RETRACTED

Ran the pre-registered null. `archon-s18b` = **identical source**, second
independent PGO profile run. Two rounds each:

| binary | round 1 i/n | round 2 i/n | mean |
|---|---|---|---|
| s17pgo | 7358.4 | 7358.4 | 7358.4 |
| s18 | 7238.2 | 7238.2 | 7238.2 |
| **s18b** (same source as s18) | **7238.2** | **7238.2** | **7238.2** |

**PGO PROFILE NOISE = 0.0 i/n. STUDY 18 EFFECT = 120.1 i/n.**

The two builds are byte-DIFFERENT (different sha256 — PGO changed layout) yet
execute an IDENTICAL instruction count. And `instructions:u` is deterministic
round-to-round. Against the rule fixed before the measurement (≤15 ⇒ stands),
**−120.1 i/n is real and quotable.** The confound flagged at merge time is
CLOSED.

**★ RETRACTION IN THE SAME BREATH — my `cyc/n −116.4 (−2.01%)` is withdrawn.**
It came from ONE round. At two rounds, cycles/node is wildly noisy:

| binary | cyc/n round 1 | cyc/n round 2 | swing |
|---|---|---|---|
| s17pgo | 5769.8 | 5871.9 | 1.8% |
| s18 | 5809.6 | **6113.3** | **5.2%** |
| s18b | 5744.4 | 5979.5 | 4.1% |

Means put s18 (5961.5) ABOVE s17pgo (5820.9) — i.e. the cycles data, read
naively, says study 18 made things *worse*. It says nothing of the kind; it
says **cycles/node carries ~±300 i/n-equivalent noise on this box and cannot
resolve a 120 effect in 2 rounds.** Only the instruction count survives.

**Instrument noise ranking measured on this box today — keep this table:**

| instrument | noise floor | can it resolve a ~120 i/n / few-% effect? |
|---|---|---|
| `instructions:u` @ fixed nodes | **0.0** | **yes, trivially** |
| cycles/node @ fixed nodes | ~5% (~±300) | **no**, not at n=2 |
| paired nps, alternating, n=5 | ~1% | yes for a 2x effect, marginal for 5% |
| match-PGN nps | **~24%** | **no — it fabricated a reversed result** |

**This vindicates `instructions:u` as the studies' primary metric** — it is the
only one here with a zero noise floor. It does NOT retract the study-19 brief to
ALSO carry cache-misses/IPC: that brief is about instructions:u being an
incomplete *objective* (instruction parity alone still leaves ~1.16x slower),
not about it being an imprecise *instrument*. Two different criticisms; only the
first one is true. **Study 19 must therefore treat any cycles/cache-miss number
as needing many more rounds than an instructions number** — tell it so.

### ★★★ THE SF-COMPARATIVE METHOD HAS A BLIND SPOT — self-profile finds 22% of cycles nobody ranked (owner hypothesis, 2026-07-29)

Owner: *"The agents that profile Stockfish must be blind to something. I bet
there is a large hot path bottleneck in our engine that we haven't seen because
we are too focused on Stockfish comparisons."* **Correct, and here is the
mechanism plus the numbers.**

**First, the arithmetic that motivated looking.** Threat generation — the block
studies 17, 18 and 19 have all worked — is only **14% of the instruction gap**:

| | i/n |
|---|---|
| ours (study probe frame, post-17) | 8910.1 |
| SF (from the studies' own 1.3285x) | 6706.9 |
| **total gap** | **2203.2** |
| threat gen ours 897.6 − SF 598.1 | 299.5 = **14% of the gap** |
| **everything else** | **1903.7 = 86%, never profiled** |

**Then the SELF-profile** — `perf record` on OUR d16 bench, 7.86 M nodes, no
Stockfish in the measurement at all:

| symbol | % cycles | in any ranked remainder? |
|---|---|---|
| `search::NonPv` | 19.90 | — |
| `at_full` | **18.56** | yes (study 19 primary) |
| **`sota_simd::avx512::i16_deltas_into`** | **13.14** | **NO** |
| **`finny_refresh_into`** | **9.15** | **NO** |
| `sota_head::forward_into` | 8.77 | no |
| **`MovePicker::init_quiets`** | **6.35** | ranked **item 8, "low confidence"** |
| `current_full` | 3.28 | no |
| `record_square_touch` | 1.98 | yes (study 19 SECONDARY) |

**THREE FINDINGS:**

1. **Accumulator MAINTENANCE is 44.1% of cycles; the network head is 8.77%.**
   (`at_full` + `i16_deltas_into` + `finny_refresh_into` + `current_full`.) We
   are not slow because the net is 1280 wide — we spend **5x more keeping the
   accumulator current than using it**. This reframes the whole eval-cost story
   and it is the first time anyone has looked at the split.
2. **Study 19's secondary target was misranked ~6x.** `record_square_touch` is
   1.98%; `i16_deltas_into` is 13.14%. Amendment sent.
3. **The instrument was choosing the work.** `record_square_touch` has a clean
   SF counterpart (`update_piece_threats`) so the comparative method priced it.
   `i16_deltas_into`/`finny_refresh_into` have none, and study 18 §5.2c wrote off
   the SIMD half as beyond any instrument on this box. **Work flowed to what was
   comparable, not to what was expensive.**

**STANDING RULE ADDED: rank on OUR OWN profile share FIRST; use the SF
comparison only to say how much of that share is recoverable.**

**Two hypotheses of mine died on the way, recorded so they are not re-run:**
- *"We lack SF's Finny/accumulator cache."* **FALSE** — `sota_accumulator.rs`
  has one, 64 keys, per-king-state.
- *"Our head is bigger than SF's."* **FALSE** — ours is 1280→16→32→1, SF's is
  1024→32→32→1. Ours is SMALLER.
- *"`load_v2_bytes` at 5.49% is a hot-path bug."* **FALSE — startup
  amortisation.** At d16 it falls to 1.51% and `init_magics` drops off entirely.
  **Profile long enough that startup washes out before ranking anything.**

### ★★ CATEGORY PROFILE vs SF — "our search machinery is faster" is REFUTED, movepick is genuinely ahead

Both engines profiled on the SAME positions (`wl50.fens`, d16 bench), with
cycles/node measured on the SAME workload as the profile so the percentages can
be converted to absolutes:

| | ARCHON | SF | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|
| cycles/node | 4843.6 | 3123.9 | **1.550x** |
| instructions/node | 5863.8 | 4404.4 | **1.331x** |

| category | ARCHON cyc/node | SF cyc/node | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|
| eval (accumulator + head + threats) | 2819 | 1778 | **1.59x** |
| **search** (+`do_move`, history updates) | **1129** | **667** | **1.69x — OUR WORST** |
| **movepick** (+sort, `see_ge`) | **368** | **432** | **0.85x — WE ARE AHEAD** |

**★ THIS REFUTES A STANDING LEDGER CLAIM.** The ledger says of nps: *"NOT A
SEARCH DEFECT. The search machinery is faster than SF's; the entire speed gap is
net evaluation cost."* On matched positions with absolute cycles, **search is our
WORST category at 1.69x** — worse than eval's 1.59x. The old conclusion came from
comparing ARCHON-on-PSQT throughput against SF18's total, which is not a
like-for-like search comparison.

**And a genuine win nobody had noticed: our movepick is 0.85x SF's** — we are
15% cheaper per node there. First category where we lead.

**THE SHAPES ARE NOT ANOMALOUS.** Eval is 58.2% of our cycles and 56.9% of SF's;
search 23.3% vs 21.3%. **We do not have one pathological hot spot — we are
broadly ~1.5–1.7x slower across eval and search.** That tempers (does not
cancel) the blind-spot finding: the 22% of cycles in unranked symbols are real
targets, but no single fix closes this.

**★ THE FRAME EFFECT IS LARGE AND MUST BE QUOTED.** Same two binaries,
two workloads:

| frame | i/n ratio | cyc/node ratio |
|---|---|---|
| d16 bench, 50 fens | **1.331x** | 1.550x |
| 20 fens @ 400k nodes | **1.688x** | 1.954x |

**We get relatively WORSE as per-position search deepens** (1.33x → 1.69x). Open
question, unmeasured, do NOT chain on it: candidate causes are TT/memory
behaviour deeper in the tree or accumulator maintenance scaling with tree depth.
Note the d16-bench figure 1.331x matches the studies' own 1.3285x — their frame
is the shallow one, so **the studies have been optimising against our FLATTERING
frame.**

**Caveats:** category sums are LOWER BOUNDS (the 1.0% percent-limit drops small
symbols); `see_ge` is assigned to movepick but is also a search-pruning call;
SF's `Network::evaluate` is one fused symbol so its accumulator/head split cannot
be read directly, while ours is split across four symbols.

### Study 19 — landed −45.41 i/n, but its THREE CORRECTIONS matter more

**Landed** (probe frame, node- and eval-hash-identical on every line incl.
rejects): three redundant-guard deletions in the threat generator, **−45.41 i/n
(−0.738%)**, −6.359 br/n. `DeltaBuf::push_kept` (−28.46), `record_targets` with
the victim mask as a const parameter (−9.34), a dead knight-scan guard (−7.61).
Rejected g1/g1b (+4.63, +1.82).

the first time: `resolve_both` is 285.65 i/n = **51% of the block**. But
re-profiling SF *in the same 30k frame* (study 18's SF column was 3k) shows
resolution is only **1.076x SF per perspective-resolve**. The 1.289x total is a
CALL-COUNT difference from a design trade. **Generation is the block: 1.30x
total, 1.52x per call.** Size never equals opportunity.

**CORRECTION 2 — the cache-miss story I would have believed is FALSE.** Study 19
had the threat-PSQT row (random 32 B from 1.94 MB) written into its own
remainder as the stall cause, then measured it: **`at_full` owns just 4.69% of
misses; 65.75% are in the three ACCUMULATOR-FOLD kernels.** It withdrew its own
hypothesis in place rather than shipping it. This corroborates
`i16_deltas_into` + `finny_refresh_into` as the real stall site — study 20's
files, not study 19's.

**CORRECTION 3 — my per-symbol profile shares are FRAME-DEPENDENT.** Study 19's
self-profile agrees with mine on the AGGREGATE (accumulator maintenance 45.4% vs
my 44.1%; head 7.7% vs 8.8%) but disagrees **3.5x on `at_full`** and **7.0x on
`finny_refresh_into`**, because `wl50.fens` is endgame-skewed and a fixed-depth
bench set is not. **Rank on the aggregate, or name the frame.** Amendment sent to
study 20, which had been given my numbers.

**A call-count law worth keeping:** g1/g1b removed provable work and still cost
instructions, because `ripple` runs 0.481/node against `record_square_touch`'s
1.0865 — **0.443 ripples per touch, so anything hoisted out of the rarer callee
is paid 2.26x more often.** Check the call-count ratio before hoisting.

**Its honesty, unprompted:** the paired cycles median read −2.06% against a
−0.74% instruction change and **it refused to claim it**, because its own null
came back at 1.0163 with a **19.17pp spread**, and going 8→20 rounds moved the
null AWAY from unity (drift slower than the measurement, so rounds don't help).
It also volunteered that it ran that null in the wrong order and destroyed its
first attempt. That is exactly the standard this project needs.

**Handed to study 20 as its item 1:** SF prefetches inside
`append_changed_indices` the instant each index is computed
(`eca43a97:full_threats.cpp:295-299`); our phase split leaves the latency
exposed. `compute`'s comment rejects this at "−0.4% NPS" — **study-4 era, inside
today's measured noise floor, therefore not evidence.** This arm EXPECTS
