### ★★ sb170 SCALE CHECK — the calibrated target landed us IN STOCKFISH'S FRAME

Packed `d48y2-1280-sb170-ema.cenn` (138,283,640 B, checksum `0x51906e4e0aae20e2`)
and ran `net_scale_check.sh` against D48X:

```
n=520  median(cand/ref)=1.788  IQR=(1.48,2.03)  slope=1.807  sign-agree=99.8%
  -> FAIL the RELATIVE band (0.85-1.18)          [expected: the target changed]
ABSOLUTE vs SF INTERNAL: n=518  median(ours/sf)=1.026  slope=1.030
  -> ABSOLUTE PASS (band 0.80-1.25)
```

**D48Y2's eval is 1.79x D48X's and sits at 1.026x STOCKFISH'S INTERNAL FRAME.**
The sb80 observation is confirmed at sb170 with n=518. Consequence worth stating
plainly: our search margins were transcribed from SF, so against D48Y2 they are
only **0.97x off**, where against D48X they are ~1.74x off. **The new net is
better matched to our own pruning constants than the incumbent is.**
`sign-agree 99.8%` says the two nets almost never disagree about WHO is better —
this is a scale change, not a behaviour change.

**RAN AT NATIVE SCALE, deliberately, and the runbook's step 3 was NOT followed.**
The runbook calls calibration mandatory. Two measured reasons to override it
here, both recorded earlier in this doc: (1) **the frame effect is ASYMMETRIC** —
enlarging eval measured +7.7 ± 17.7 (free), shrinking it cost **80 Elo**
(−111 → −191), because pruning margins are fixed cp subtractions so a smaller
eval over-prunes; D48Y2 native is the LARGER direction. (2) The absolute check
PASSES, so native is the frame our constants were written for. Calibrating down
to D48X's frame would deliberately move us away from SF's.

**Match launched at the IDENTICAL protocol to the sb90 one** (120 games, 40k
nodes, Hash 16, UHO, native scale) so the trend is readable. Note the binary
changed (s10pgo → s19) but every study 12–19 change was **node-identical**, so at
FIXED NODES both matches search the same trees and remain comparable.

Trend so far:

| checkpoint | result |
|---|---|
| D48Y sb120 (broken 1.80x LR) | −111.4 ± 26.3 (300 games, 60k) |
| D48Y2 sb90 (LR fixed) | −43.7 ± 33.4 |
| **D48Y2 sb170 (deep in anneal)** | **running** |

Heldout R² alongside: sb150 **0.8866**, sb160 **0.8877**, sb170 **0.8889**, vs
D48X's fully-annealed **0.8815**. The loss metric says D48Y2 passed the incumbent
around sb130. **Games arbitrate — this match is the test of whether that
converts.**

### ★ RETRACTION — "no teacher" is NOT a confound (owner, 2026-07-29)

Every tick of this doc has carried "D48Y differs from D48X in TWO ways
(calibrated per-corpus target AND no teacher)". **Owner challenged the teacher
half. They are right; it is withdrawn.** Read from the configs:

```
D48X   distill_alpha_ramp = [[0, 0.7], [30, 0.7], [60, 0.0]]   superbatches = 520
D48X   warm = data/nnue/experiments/d48x-warm-a3.bin
D48Y   warm = data/nnue/experiments/d48x-warm-a3.bin          <- IDENTICAL trunk
D48Y2  warm = d48y-1280-sb160-ema-raw.bin                     <- same lineage
```

Three reasons it is not a confound:

1. **The teacher was OFF for 88.5% of D48X's training.** It ramped to zero at
   sb60 of 520 — inactive through all of D48X's convergence and its entire
   anneal.
2. **Both lineages share the identical warm trunk** (`d48x-warm-a3.bin`), so
   whatever the teacher contributed in sb0–60 is baked into weights D48Y2
   INHERITS. D48Y2 did not "lose" it.
3. **The direction is wrong for a confound.** If the teacher contributed
   something D48Y2 lacks, that biases AGAINST D48Y2 — it cannot manufacture the
   −111 → −11.6 improvement. It was only ever a confound for the opposite
   conclusion.

**WHAT REMAINS A REAL DIFFERENCE (and why `d48z` still matters):** D48Y2 trained
**190 sb from a D48Y checkpoint** on its own LR ramp; D48X trained **520 sb from
the raw trunk** on a different one. Those are schedule/budget differences, not
teacher differences. `d48z_old`/`d48z_new` hold warm, seed, mix and budget fixed
with **`per_corpus_target` as the single variable**, and remain the clean test of
the WDL question.

**Method note for future ticks:** this caveat survived many ticks unexamined
because it was written into the handoff header early and then copied forward. A
confound asserted once and inherited is not a measured confound. **Two minutes of
`json.load` on the two run configs would have killed it at any point.**

### ★★★ THE QUANTISATION THEORY — 73% of the net carries ~2 bits of weight precision (2026-07-29)

Owner eliminated, with reasons, every theory on the table: not architecture
(Reckless's **768-wide** net gives +30.0 in OUR search), not data source or labels
(we train on SF's own binpacks), not training volume (nets plateau ~100sb until
anneal), not co-evolution (a Reckless-trained net still beats ours in our
search), not features (proven identical to SF index-by-index). **What is left has
to be something every metric we own is blind to.**

**Measured on `d48y2-1280-sb170-ema-quant.bin`:**

| channel | container | share of inputs | max abs | levels used | typical weight | eff. precision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| HalfKA | i16 @ 255 | 27.1% | 1057 / 32767 | 1,516 / 65,536 (2.3%) | **54.9 steps** | ~5.8 bits |
| **threats** | **i8 @ 64** | **72.9%** | **127 / 127 — AT THE CEILING** | **250 / 256 (97.7%)** | **4.6 steps** | **~2.2 bits** |

**A typical threat weight is 4.6 quantisation levels.** The i8 container is
COMPLETELY FULL (97.7% of levels used, max exactly at the ceiling), so the scale
is being set by OUTLIERS — a few weights need ±127, forcing scale 64, leaving the
bulk of the distribution with ~2 bits. HalfKA has the same tail but 16 bits
absorbs it (uses only 3.2% of its range and does not care).

**Why SF does not pay this** — and the line was in our own port spec all along
(`searchopt/sf-net-port-spec.md` §3): their threat weights are i8 added
**directly** into the 255-frame accumulator, no shift, because **"SF's trainer
bakes the scale in instead."** That IS quantisation-aware training: their weight
distribution adapts during training to fill the container. We train in float,
let the distribution go where it likes, and quantise post hoc.

**WHY EVERY METRIC WE HAVE IS BLIND TO IT:** heldout MSE, R², the loss curves and
the target-transform work are ALL computed on the FLOAT net. **We play with the
QUANTISED net.** Nothing in the training loop, and nothing in any screen we run
before packing, can see this damage. It also explains the Reckless result
directly — a smaller net trained with quantisation in the loop beating a larger
one quantised afterwards.

**STATUS: HYPOTHESIS, NOT A MEASUREMENT.** This measures weight PRECISION, not
Elo. The link to strength is an inference and must not be quoted as a finding.

**Two tests, cheap one first:**
1. **Float-vs-quantised eval divergence** on heldout positions — prices the
   damage in cp before anyone commits to a retrain. Needs the raw checkpoint
   format resolved (`-raw.bin` is NOT a clean f4 array; it has a header — size
   433,755,450 is not a multiple of 4).
2. **QAT** (straight-through estimator), which the training-gap study already
   lists as one of four current SF mechanisms with NO ARCHON counterpart
   (`nnue-training-gap-study-2026-07-28.md` §2.5, "real QAT, default on").

Related open item from the same study: **G5 — our threat FT is quantised 4x
coarser than BOTH comparison engines and nothing in training knows about it.**
That gap was written down on 2026-07-28 and not acted on. This is the same
finding, now with the weight distribution measured.

### ★★ QUANTISATION ERROR MEASURED PER CHANNEL — threats carry 86% of the noise

Owner's theory, now with the premise measured on `d48y2-1280-sb170`
(raw vs quant, factoriser folded):

| channel | share of inputs | weight RMS | quant-err RMS | rel err | SNR | eff. bits |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| HalfKA | 27.1% | 0.21517 | 0.001099 | **0.51%** | **45.8 dB** | ~7.6 |
| **threats** | **72.9%** | 0.07208 | 0.004025 | **5.58%** | **25.1 dB** | **~4.2** |

**Threats carry 11x the relative quantisation error and 20.7 dB less SNR.** At
the accumulator (~32 HalfKA + ~90 threat features active) threats contribute
**86% of all quantisation noise, 6.14x HalfKA's share**.

**TWO causes compound**, which is why it is worse than the 4x scale ratio alone:
(1) threats are quantised 4x coarser (scale 64 vs 255); (2) threat weights are
**3x SMALLER in magnitude** (RMS 0.072 vs 0.215), so the same absolute step is a
proportionally larger error. 3x smaller x 4x coarser ~= 12x; measured **11x**.

**Robustness:** the accumulator split uses ESTIMATED active-feature counts
(~32/~90, not measured). The raw error ratio is already **3.66x** before any
count weighting, so threats dominate even if the counts are equal or reversed.
The conclusion does not rest on the estimate.

**CORRECTION to my own earlier figure:** I first said "~2.2 bits" for threats
from a crude step-count. The SNR-based number is **~4.2 bits** (vs HalfKA's
~7.6). Still a 3.4-bit deficit over 73% of the network, but 4.2 is the right
number and 2.2 was wrong.

**TWO MEASUREMENT TRAPS PAID FOR HERE, both mine:**
1. The raw checkpoint is a **NAMED TENSOR STREAM** with per-tensor headers
   (`<Q` count + name), NOT a flat f4 array. Offset arithmetic on it returns
   NaN/inf. Use `archon_jax.checkpoint.open_raw(path, None, mmap=True)`.
2. **HalfKA has a FACTORISER** (`l0_factw`, 704x1280) that is folded into the
   main weights at export. Comparing raw `l0_pstw` against quantised HalfKA
   without folding gives a nonsense **208% error / −6.4 dB**. Fold with
   `pst + tile(fact, (22528/704, 1))` first. Threats have no factoriser, so that
   channel was unaffected.

**STILL AN INFERENCE: that any of this costs Elo.** Shown is that the threat
channel is far noisier under quantisation and dominates accumulator noise.
The cp cost is NOT measured. Next test (needs the GPU, free at sb190): eval
divergence float-vs-quantised on heldout positions, to price it before anyone
commits to a QAT retrain.

### Tick @ sb185 — training nearly done, study 20 landing real finds

Trainer alive, **sb185/190**, GPU 95%. Heldout EMA 0.011069 (sb170) →
**0.010971 (sb180)**, R² **0.8899** vs D48X's fully-annealed 0.8815.

**Study 20 (ARCHON-only, no SF comparison) has landed arm 2: "the NNUE weight
tables were 16 B off a cache line."** Exactly the class of defect the
SF-comparative method cannot see — there is no Stockfish counterpart to diff
against for our own table alignment. Owner's blind-spot hypothesis continues to
pay out.

### ★★ THE FIX: scale 128, not Stockfish's 255 — and SF's would make us WORSE

Owner asked to try Stockfish's scale. **Measured, and it is the wrong move for
our weights.** Exact sweep on sb170 using the real quantiser rounding
(`_round_half_away`, confirmed in `checkpoint.py:92`):

| scale | clipped | rel err | SNR | vs today |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **64 (today)** | 0 | 5.58% | 25.1 dB | 1.00x |
| 112 | 735 | 3.49% | 29.1 dB | 1.60x |
| **128 — OPTIMUM** | **1,549 (0.0020%)** | **3.39%** | **29.4 dB** | **1.65x** |
| 160 | 4,613 | 3.77% | 28.5 dB | 1.48x |
| **255 (Stockfish's)** | 37,791 | 6.80% | 23.3 dB | **0.82x — WORSE** |

**Why SF's 255 loses:** an i8 at 255 tops out at ±0.498; our threat weights span
**±1.9778**. Their trainer PRODUCES a distribution that fits that container —
that is precisely what QAT buys them — while ours does not, so 255 just clips our
tail and the clipping costs more than the resolution gains. **We cannot adopt
their scale without adopting their training.** 128 is the optimum for the
distribution we actually have. The curve is FLAT 112–144 (1.59–1.65x), so this is
robust, not a knife-edge.

**WHY 64 WAS CHOSEN — it was never tuned.** `checkpoint.quantise` **RAISES on
overflow rather than clipping** (`checkpoint.py:104-108`). Max |threat w| =
1.9778, and 127/1.9778 = **64.2**. So 64 is simply the largest scale that does
not throw. **One outlier out of 77.7 M weights is setting the precision for all
of them.**

**CHANGE SET (small, but it spans two layers):**
1. Export: clip threats to ±127/scale, set `QUANT_THREAT = 128`
   (`spec.py:82`). Tool written: **`tools/nnue/requant_threats.py`** — clips,
   patches the constant at call time, and never mutates `spec.py` on disk.
2. Engine: the 255/64 ratio is a literal `<< 2` at **`sota_head.rs:248`** and
   **`sota_simd.rs:423`** → `<< 1` (255/128 = 1.992, the same 0.39% gain
   approximation as today's 3.984→4).
3. Loader validates quant constants (`sota_loader.rs:361-375`), so both sides
   must move together.
4. **The NNUE gate pins MOVE** — intentionally, same rule as any behavioural
   change.

**SCOPE CONFLICT, deliberately deferred:** `sota_head.rs` and `sota_simd.rs` are
**study 20's files**. The engine half waits until study 20 lands rather than
racing it.

**THE A/B THIS ENABLES — and it is the clean Elo measurement we lacked.** Same
trained weights, two quantisations, fixed nodes:
`d48y2-sb190 @ scale 64` vs `d48y2-sb190 @ scale 128`. No confound from
training, data, architecture or search. It prices quantisation damage **in Elo**
rather than dB, and it is the test that decides whether QAT is worth a retrain.

## ★★★ TRAINING PROPOSAL — D49, the "do it properly" net (2026-07-29)

Owner's requested batch, implemented and verified this session. **All knobs
default OFF => every change is a bit-exact no-op until deliberately enabled.**

### IMPLEMENTED AND VERIFIED

| # | mechanism | where | verified |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **QAT, full integer pipeline** (not weight-only): FT weights, the pairwise `>>9` -> 127-level activation grid, L1 weights | `model.py: fake_quant / fake_quant_act` | STE grads flow through unmoved weights; floor gives 0.5 -> 0.4961 |
| 2 | **QAT clipping callback** — SF's missing half | `model.py: clip_qat_weights` | reproduces SF's ranges exactly (threat +/-0.4961@256, l1 +/-1.9844@64) |
| 3 | **Double sigmoid on the NETWORK side** — the half of SF's transform D48Y2 lacked | `losses.py: expected_score` | `expected_score(0)=0.5`; off => bit-exact |
| 4 | **`qp_asymmetry`** (SF ships 0.2287) | `losses.py: value_loss` | off => bit-exact |
| 5 | **Piece-count rebalancing**, SF's 5-point spline 0/8/16/24/32 -> −0.20/0.45/1.0/0.95/0.75 | `features.py` | weight emitted only when enabled |
| 6 | **WDL filtering** (down-weight eval/result disagreement) | `features.py` | OUR formulation of SF's shape, NOT a transcription |

**Activations FLOOR, weights ROUND** — the engine's `>>` is a floor and SF floors
for the same reason. I had written `round`; caught by reading their quantiser.

### BLOCKED, WITH THE REASON

**7. Soft early-ply spline — CANNOT BE DONE AT LOAD TIME.** `RECORD_DTYPE` is
32 bytes (`occ, pcs[16], score, result, ksq, opp_ksq, extra[3]`) and `extra` is
spent on policy. **Ply is not stored.** Needs a corpus re-convert.
**The gap this leaves is not small:** our convert filter drops ply < 16, and SF's
spline accepts only ~5-20% of ply 16-29, so we OVER-WEIGHT early middlegame
badly relative to SF.

### THE OPEN QUESTION THAT SHOULD BE SETTLED FIRST

**What does `band` mean?** Training corpora are `band75-100` / `band75-88` /
`band75-95` / `band75-83`; the heldout is `band0-5`. I could NOT find a
definition in `data-recipe-contract.md`, the study, or the tooling. The study's
percentages are MIX WEIGHTS, not retention rates. If bands are percentile slices
with heldout disjoint, **band 5-75 is unused entirely** — potentially the largest
lever available and consistent with the owner's "we only use a band". **Do not
build a run plan on a guess: resolve this first.** It is cheap.

### PROPOSED RUN — D49

**Do NOT enable everything at once.** D48Y2 is the cautionary case: it bundled a
target change with a schedule change and landed unattributable (−40.7 +/- 35.9
final, R² 0.8906 vs D48X's 0.8815 — metric moved, strength did not).

**Arm A (the mechanism most likely to explain D48Y2's null):** network-side
double sigmoid ONLY, warm from d48y2-sb190, same schedule. If D48Y2's R²-without-
strength was the uncorrected output frame, this is where it shows.

**Arm B:** QAT (all four knobs) at threat scale **256** with the clipping
callback, warm from d48y2-sb190. Scale 128 was the right answer to the wrong
question — it is optimal for POST-HOC quantisation of unconstrained weights;
with the callback holding weights in range, 256 is correct and matches SF.

**Arm C:** data shaping (piece-count + WDL weighting) ONLY.

**Then combine what wins.** Each arm is ~100sb warm (the owner's own finding:
nets plateau ~100sb until anneal) + a 70sb anneal.

### MEASUREMENT RULES FOR D49 — non-negotiable

1. **GAMES ARBITRATE.** D48Y2 improved R² monotonically for 190sb and gained
   nothing. **No arm is accepted on heldout, R², or dB.**
2. **The screen is ~100x underpowered** (study G0): we run 120-400 games at
   +/-35 Elo; Reckless screens every net at 25,000 fixed nodes over ~40,000
   games at +/-2 Elo, and the study says that is affordable here. **Fix the
   instrument before running the arms**, or none of them will resolve.
3. **Self-null is clean** (0.00 +/- 0.00, W29/L29/D62) — the harness is symmetric
   and the engine deterministic at fixed nodes. That is a symmetry check, NOT a
   noise floor: paired openings force cancellation.
4. **QAT parity check before any strength claim:** all knobs off must reproduce a
   QAT-off run bit-exactly.

## ★★★ G0 CLOSED — the screening instrument was the binding constraint all along

`tools/screen.sh`. **Every net conclusion this session was read off 120-game
screens returning +/-33..53 Elo.** D48Y2's entire trend — sb90 −43.7, sb170
−11.6, sb190 −40.7 — fits inside ONE error bar. The training-gap study filed this
as **G0: "our net-screening instrument is ~100x underpowered, and the fix is
cheap"**, and it was right.

**The bar, CALIBRATED against our own observed screens rather than a textbook
rule.** Our 120-game runs returned ~+/-35, so `k = 35*sqrt(120) = 383` and
`bar ~= 383/sqrt(n)`. **Cross-check: that predicts +/-1.9 at n=40,000 and
Reckless report +/-2 at exactly that n. It matches.** (My first attempt used a
generic `1600/sqrt(n)`, which was wrong by **4.2x**; the observed data corrected
it.)

**MEASURED throughput: 104 games/min** at concurrency 14, 25k nodes, PGO:

| target | games | wall time |
|---|---|---|
| +/-10 Elo | 1,466 | **14 min** |
| **+/-5 Elo** | 5,867 | **56 min** |
| +/-3 Elo | 16,298 | 2.6 h |
| +/-2 Elo (Reckless's bar) | 36,672 | 5.9 h |

**A 100x power gain for one hour of wall clock.** This should have been built
before any of tonight's net decisions.

**THE PROBE ALREADY SHOWS THE DAMAGE.** 200 games @25k: D48Y2-sb190 vs D48X
= **+6.95 +/- 29.34** — POSITIVE, where the 120-game @40k screen said
**−40.7 +/- 35.9**. Not a contradiction: the same effect inside one bar, sign
flipping on noise. **We do not currently know whether D48Y2 is better or worse
than D48X.** The 5,880-game screen is running to settle it.

**TWO IMPLEMENTATION TRAPS PAID FOR:**
1. **CONCURRENCY IS MEMORY-BOUND, NOT CPU-BOUND.** Each engine process loads the
   net into PRIVATE heap, so the cost is `2 * CONC * 138 MB` — conc=14 wants
   ~3.9 GB of nets before hash. First run was **oom-killed** under
   `MemoryMax=4G`. The script now derives the cap from free memory and the actual
   net size, and takes the min with cores-2.
2. `EvalFile` must be passed EXPLICITLY for both sides — it defaults to
   `<empty>`, which silently evaluates PSQT (~2.13 Mnps vs ~1.0) and would
   measure nothing.

**STANDING RULE: no net verdict from a screen under ~1,500 games.** At 120 games
the bar is +/-35 and every effect we care about is smaller than that.

## ★★★ D49 LAUNCHED — from scratch, 400sb, all six mechanisms ON (2026-07-29)

`tools/nnue/runs/d49_1280.json`, unit `archon-d49.service`, log via
`journalctl --user -u archon-d49`. **Owner ruling: everything on, no ablation —
"these are all things that are proven by other engines".**

| | setting |
|---|---|
| init | **FROM SCRATCH** (`init_params` seed 0), no warm |
| budget | **400 sb** |
| LR | peak 5.5625e-4, flat sb0–10, **cosine to 1.10e-4 at sb300 (5.1x decay)**, linear anneal 300→400. D48Y2 managed only **1.24x** before its anneal — the owner's "we didn't decay enough" |
| loss | net_offset **0.43775** / net_scale **0.179** (network-side double sigmoid), qp_asymmetry **0.2287**, pow_exp **2.5** |
| QAT | ft 255, threat **256**, act **127 (FLOOR)**, l1 64, **+ clipping callback** |
| data | piece_rebalance ON, wdl_filter ON (floor 0.10) |

**First superbatch: `value 0.025927`, 172,219 pos/s** — against D48Y2's ~178k,
so **the six mechanisms cost ~3% throughput**. QAT's fake-quant, the activation
grid and the per-record weights are all essentially free.

### The four pre-flight items, and what each caught

1. **Config surface** threaded through `TrainConfig`, `ArchSpec` AND
   `pump_train`. This is the exact bug class that sank D48Y2: `per_corpus_target`
   was dropped by an explicit field list and the run trained the OLD target while
   the config claimed the new one.
2. **Clipping callback** fires after `apply_updates`, matching SF's per-batch
   cadence. This is what makes threat scale **256** usable instead of the 64 our
   unconstrained weights force.
3. **Net-side constants DERIVED, not guessed.** The export path assumes
   `logit ≈ score/400`, so scaling the label frame by /400 makes `qf` and `pf`
   **the same function** — verified at **max abs diff 0.00e+00**.
4. **Parity check** — all knobs off reproduces the historical MSE **BIT-EXACT**.
   It also caught a real bug: `.view(np.uint8)` on a non-contiguous structured
   field, which would have thrown on the first batch of a 400sb run.

### THREE LAUNCH BUGS, each of which would have wasted the run

1. **`pump_train` had NO from-scratch path.** Every recent run was warm-started,
   so `spec["warm"]` was unconditional → `KeyError`. Added the branch.
2. **`train.py` did not import `model`** — my clipping-callback edit referenced
   it and died on the first step.
3. **Buffer double-donation.** `init_params` returns JAX arrays, so
   `ema = dict(params)` SHARED device buffers and the step fn donates both
   (`donate_argnums=(0,1)`); JAX rejects donating one buffer twice. The warm path
   is safe only because `load_warm` returns numpy. Fixed with `np.asarray` +
   explicit `.copy()`.

All three surfaced within 2 minutes because the launch was watched. **Watch the
first superbatches of any run with new mechanisms.**

### Operational

**Launched as a systemd transient SERVICE, not `--scope`.** A `--scope` dies
with the session — that is what killed the 5,880-game screen at 1043/5880.
Anything long-running is a service from now on.

### ★ D49 RESTARTED at sb16 — the heldout scorer was measuring the WRONG FUNCTION

**Found before it could mislead a 13.6-hour run.** `heldout_value_loss`
(`pump_train.py`) computed `sigmoid(out)`, while D49's training loss uses
`expected_score(out, 0.43775, 0.179)` — the network-side double sigmoid.

**This is the SAME TRAP the function's own docstring warns about, on the other
side.** That docstring covers the LABEL frame ("scoring a calibrated net against
the old target reads as a REGRESSION BY CONSTRUCTION") and nobody extended the
thought to the network output when we added the network-side transform. D49
would have logged a heldout for its whole run that measured a different function
than it was optimising.

Fixed: `heldout_value_loss` now takes `net_offset` / `net_scale` and applies
`expected_score` when set; both call sites (params and EMA) pass `cfg.*`.
`net_scale=0` keeps the historical path bit-exact.

**Restarted at sb16 — 33 min lost out of 13.6 h (4%).** Cheap against flying
blind on the primary health metric for the whole run. Training itself was always
correct; only the telemetry was wrong, but telemetry is how a divergence gets
caught early.

**Generalisation worth keeping: when a transform is added to one side of the
loss, grep for EVERY place that recomputes the loss outside the training step.**
Heldout scorers, probes and parity tools all silently keep the old shape.

### Tick — D49 restarted clean, study 21 lands its first arm

**D49** back at sb6 after the heldout-scorer restart, **198k pos/s** (up from
192k pre-restart; from-scratch keeps getting faster as the cache warms).

**The restart doubles as a determinism check on the fix:**

| sb | pre-restart | post-restart |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 0.025927 | 0.025924 |
| 6 | 0.013312 | 0.013331 |

Reproduces to ~0.1% ⇒ **the heldout change was telemetry-only, as intended.**
Not bit-identical, which is expected — the Pallas FT scatter uses atomics.

**Study 21 arm B1: "the 20 MiB of history tables all started 16 B off a cache
line."** Same defect class as study 20's NNUE weight tables, and again a defect

| symbol | I briefed (inclusive) | study 21 (exclusive) |
|---|---|---|
| `finny_refresh_into` | 9.15% | **1.17%** |
| `search::<NonPv>` | 19.90% | **6.81%** |
| **`i8_deltas_into`** | **not on my list** | **14.88% — LARGEST IN THE ENGINE** |

**I verified the mechanism myself:** `apply_i8_deltas_into` is real at
`sota_simd.rs:185` but does **NOT appear in `nm`** on the shipped binary — it is
inlined, so its cycles land on its caller. That is exactly what inflates a
symbol-level table. (I could not re-run the exclusive profile to confirm the
percentages — `perf` returns 129/255 under contention from the sibling studies —
so the MECHANISM is mine and confirmed; the NUMBERS are study 21's and
unverified by me.)

Two more from the same study: **symbol shares are frame-dependent**
(`i8_deltas_into` reads 10.86% on wl50 vs 14.88% on d16, same binary), and the
**resolution floor is ~1% cycles**, set by the running trainer — so two of the
six targets I briefed sit at or below it.

**ACTION TAKEN: study 22 redirected.** I had sent it after `finny_refresh_into`
at a briefed 9.15%; at ~1.17% exclusive it is AT the floor and no honest arm
there can resolve. Its new target is study 21's top remainder, which happens to
be in its own files:

> **ARCHON maintains TWO 1280-wide accumulators where Stockfish maintains ONE**
> — ~20 KB/node of copy traffic, **65% of all L1 misses**, 135 MB of weight

### ★ CORRECTION TO MY OWN CORRECTION — the profile table was NOT wrong

Last tick I relayed study 21's claim that my briefing shares were wrong, and
told the owner so. **I re-measured and my numbers reproduce.** Fresh
`perf record -e cycles:u`, d16 bench on wl50.fens, `--no-children`, PGO
`archon-s20`:

| symbol | I briefed | RE-MEASURED | study 21 claimed |
|---|---|---|---|
| `search::<NonPv>` | 19.90% | **21.29%** | 6.81% |
| `at_full` | 18.56% | **17.76%** | — |
| `i16_deltas_into` | 13.14% | **12.54%** | — |
| `forward_into` | 8.77% | **8.66%** | — |
| `finny_refresh_into` | 9.15% | **8.49%** | 1.17% |
| `init_quiets` | 6.35% | **6.77%** | — |

**Both measurements are right; they answer different questions.**
`perf --no-children` gives cycles in a SYMBOL'S CODE REGION, INCLUDING callees
the compiler inlined into it. Study 21's figures are cycles in a SOURCE
FUNCTION'S OWN BODY, excluding inlined callees. Mine answers "where are the
cycles"; theirs answers "which function body do I edit". Neither is the error I
called it.

**My failure was process, not arithmetic: I relayed a subagent's reframing to
the owner without re-measuring first** — the exact thing the standing rule says
to do ("verify load-bearing claims against the code/data yourself before acting
on them", and check the FRAMING not just the numbers).

**Consequence for study 22 is smaller than I told it.** 8.49% of cycles really
do execute inside `finny_refresh_into`'s region; much of it is inlined callee
code owned by another file. **Not churning study 22 again** — the
dual-accumulator redirect stands on its own merits (65% of L1 misses, ~20 KB/node
of copy traffic), independent of the share dispute.

**Also note `--call-graph=none` is INVALID on this perf build** when combined
with `-e` — it silently worked in earlier invocations without `-e` and then
failed with exit 129. Omit it; no call graph is the default.

## ★★★ STUDY 22 — refuted my redirect's premise, MIS-CITED the reference, and was right anyway

### The citation error — the exact trap CLAUDE.md documents

Study 22 reported: *"'ARCHON maintains TWO 1280-wide accumulators where Stockfish
maintains ONE' is wrong — **I can prove it from the pinned reference**"*, and
quoted `eca43a97:src/nnue/nnue_accumulator.h` declaring
`psq_accumulators` + `threat_accumulators`.

**I checked. That code is NOT at `eca43a97`:**

```
eca43a97 (PINNED reference):  std::array<AccumulatorState, MaxSize> accumulators;   ONE
sf_18 / HEAD (the CHECKOUT):  threat_accumulators ... psq_accumulators              TWO
```

`git grep threat_accumulators eca43a97 -- src/` returns **nothing**; the same grep
at `sf_18` and `HEAD` hits. **It read the checkout and labelled it the pinned
revision** — precisely what CLAUDE.md warns about ("the reference is `eca43a97`
— never the checkout, and never a release tag"). Interesting corollary:
**Stockfish SPLIT its accumulator into two stacks AFTER `eca43a97`**, so ARCHON's
two-context structure resembles current SF more than it resembles our own pinned
constants revision.

### The conclusion survives, because it never rested on the citation

Study 22 also **priced the merge empirically**, and that is what actually closes
it:

| | i/n |
|---|---|
| max saving from deleting one accumulator (`black_box`'d extra copy in `current_full`) | **≤157.4** (+55.4 L1d misses/node) |
| cost of the refresh-trigger union (extra from-scratch threat rebuilds) | **+872** |
| | **loses ~6:1** |

HalfKA refreshes on every own-king move (0.1611/node); threats only on d/e-mirror
crossings (0.0214/node, 6,240 instructions each). The one escape — weakening
HalfKA's trigger to "king BUCKET changed", which `halfka_king_key`'s own doc
comment promises — is unreachable: that key is a **bijection on all 64 squares**.
**Dual-accumulator merge: CLOSED on measurement, not on the mis-cited quote.**

### ★★ THE METHODOLOGY FINDING — bigger than the arms

**Four nulls on the SAME BINARY vs its own byte-identical copy, one session:**

| | |
|---|---|
| null 1 | **−3.40%** |
| null 2 | −0.07% |
| null 3 | −1.51% |
| null 4 | **+1.42%** |

**A 4.8-point spread on a change that does not exist.** Study 22 first committed
a **−1.16% cycles** headline against a null taken 40 minutes earlier, then ran a
three-way round-robin (base / byte-identical copy / arm, cycled together), found
**the null itself moving +1.42%**, and **RETRACTED its own headline**.

**"NULL FIRST" AS WRITTEN IS INSUFFICIENT. It must be "NULL INTERLEAVED WITH THE
ARM".** A null taken before the arm measures a different machine state than the
arm does. `scripts/panel3-22.sh` implements the round-robin.

This also explains my own 35.8% cycles spread this tick, and it retroactively
puts a band on every cycles number quoted today that was not interleaved —
including study 20's −5.99% and study 21's −1.35%. **Neither is refuted; both are
now un-reverified.**

### What survived, and what it landed

`sota_accumulator.rs` (`7237f6d` + `cd852f3`), node-identical 5,001,562:
- **Hot/cold split** — `dirty`+`computed` were the last 12 B of a 5248 B `Entry`
  (`imul rax,r9,0x1480`): four guaranteed-distinct 5 KB-strided line touches per
  node. Now a dense 12 B/ply `Meta` array.
- **`Box<[T; N]>` not `Box<[T]>`** — the split ALONE cost **+58.7 i/n** in
  fat-pointer indexing; converting all three arrays turned it into **−9.3**.
- **Finny slot probe-first** — `Option<FinnyEntry>` burned a cache line on the
  discriminant (2752 B/slot) with `by_piece` at byte 2656: three lines 2.6 KB
  apart per probe. Now `#[repr(C)]`, two adjacent leading lines, 2688 B,
  validity in a `[u64; 2]` bitmap.
- Arm 3: **measured null (+0.8 i/n)**, landed as documentation repair only and
  labelled as such.

Surviving claim: **−8.08 i/n against an exactly-zero null in the same
round-robin** (three measurements: −9.3, −13.59, −8.08), L1d misses 2.1x and L2
2.9x the null in the arm's direction, plus cache-line reductions that are
structural facts off the disassembly. **Cycles: UNRESOLVED, and it says so.**

Rejects: prefetch (pre-refuted twice on larger tables), the 512 dead memset bytes
(~−2 i/n, and would cost the file its first `unsafe`), outlining `compute`'s king
branch (shrank `current_full` 2461→1440 B then two panels **disagreed on sign**;
reverted), min-of-N cycles (read −2.07% on two identical binaries — median only).

### Top remainder
1. `ThreatEntry` needs the identical hot/cold split — same defect, walked more often.
2. `ThreatContext.stack` → `Box<[ThreatEntry; STACK_LEN]>` — the +58.7 i/n fat-pointer tax, unfixed there.
3. Outline the `Fold::Refresh` arm of `at_full` (7855 B, largest eval symbol).

### ★★ CYCLES ARE UNMEASURABLE ON THIS BOX — THREE INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATIONS

| study | nulls on byte-identical binaries |
|---|---|
| 22 | −3.40% / −0.07% / −1.51% / **+1.42%** |
| 24 | six spanning +1.38% to −6.17%; live null median **−6.167%, 44-point spread — LARGER than the arm, same direction** |
| 25 | ABAB +0.97% / symmetric RR +2.06% / randomised RR **−1.93%** (10/12 sign-consistent, so sign counts are no safer) |

**Studies 24 and 25 each RETRACTED a cycles headline already written into a
commit** (24's "−2.25%", 25's "−2.33%/−1.70%"). Every number above is
`instructions:u` / `branches:u`, whose live nulls read **+0.0001**.

**Standing gate for the next quiet window** (study 25):
`panel3-25.sh <base> <copy> <copy>` with both slots byte-identical, and require
both medians within **±0.3% of zero** before trusting any arm. Tonight that gate
fails 3/3.

### Rejects worth keeping, each killed on a number

- **25**: the same const-generic trick on the FullThreats fold. Histogrammed
  FIRST — `{0,1,2}²` covers only **52%** of shapes (mean 1.98/2.66), so the 48%
  falling through pay dispatch for nothing: **+0.14%/+0.24% instructions**, hot
  code 3.3→11.7 KB. **Coverage is why arm 1 works and this does not** — arm 1's
  own histogram over 4.19 M real folds showed **99.99%** hit rate.
- **24**: `p1` killing `Square::try_offset` **+41.31 i/n — refutes study 19
  remainder item 4**. `b1` precomputed `by_type` only −2.92 i/n because **LLVM
  already folds six bitboard unions into one AVX-512 `vorps`** — answers study 19
  item 6.
- **25**: sized-array views of the head weight blocks read **−0.5 i/n, a null**.
  Root cause: **`Aligned<T>` is not a `Box<[T]>` — it is a `Vec` + offset whose
  `Deref` is a bounds-checked range slice on EVERY access.** Crate-wide finding.

### Two operational hazards found

- **`refs/stash` IS SHARED ACROSS GIT WORKTREES.** Study 24 popped a sibling's
  stash by accident and pulled their `movepick.rs`/`search/mod.rs` into its tree.
  It caught it and restored the entry. **With siblings live: never `git stash`;
  use `git diff > file`.** Warned the others; study 23's work verified intact.
- `for t in 0..TILES` (compile-time trip count) cost **+9.6 i/n** vs the `while`
  form — LLVM declines to unroll counted loops here.

### Top remainder
1. Thin sized head blocks (needs `aligned.rs`) — 25 predicts −35..−60 i/n, **killed if** a raw-pointer probe reads better than −20.
2. `Aligned::deref`'s bounds check — one `get_unchecked`, affects every weight table.
3. **47% of the HalfKA fold's vector work is pure parent→child copy** (80 of ~171 ops for 2.27 rows).
4. `make_move_with` spills the whole 168 B `StateInfo` and reloads across a never-taken realloc — **72% of the symbol's sampled instructions on those two reloads**; mechanism proven, 24's fix failed, next spelling named (preallocated slab + cursor).

### ★★ THE CGU SPLIT — probe-frame instruction numbers carry a caveat, PGO ones do not

Study 23 found that **at `CODEGEN_UNITS=4`, `instructions:u` is ALSO unusable**:
its arm 4 read **+48 i/n while its own loop body shrank 189 → 102 static
instructions**, and the per-symbol diff blamed `unmake_move` for **+382 i/n on
untouched code**. At CGU=1 the inert-source-change null is **275 instructions in
30.0 G** (~0.001%).

**I verified which frame is which.** `Cargo.toml`'s release profile is
`lto = "fat"`, `codegen-units = 1`. But the probe scripts override it —
`probe20.sh`, `probe21.sh`, `probe25.sh`, `build22.sh`, `perf24/build24.sh` all
carry:

```
CARGO_PROFILE_RELEASE_LTO=thin CARGO_PROFILE_RELEASE_CODEGEN_UNITS=4
```

| frame | CGU / LTO | `instructions:u` trustworthy? |
|---|---|---|
| study PROBE frames | 4 / thin | **NO** — study 23's evidence |
| shipped, PGO, and MY panels | **1 / fat** | **yes**, ~0.001% null |

**So the studies' probe-frame instruction figures inherit this caveat, and my
PGO verification panels do not.** That is a concrete argument for continuing to
re-measure every claim on a PGO binary rather than relaying the study's own
number — which is what caught study 21's scope confusion earlier tonight.

Studies 18's and 19's headline deltas survive regardless: I independently
confirmed both on PGO binaries (−120.1 and −113.3 i/n).

### Study 25's follow-ups to my three warnings — all answered with measurement

- **`git stash`**: it had used one (adjacent push/pop for a clippy count) and
  proved no harm two ways — `git diff cf7cf46..HEAD -- src/` touches
  `sota_simd.rs` and nothing else, and the eval hash is identical across every
  probe from baseline to final, which foreign code would have moved.
- **`#[repr(align)]`**: swept every struct in its four files —
  `ThreatTables` 6280, `ThreatResolver` 24, `ThreatPosition` 80, `SkipLane` 48,
  `HeadScratch` 1920 (already 64-aligned), `SotaHead` 296. **All multiples of 8,
  so they address via `lea`+`shl`, never the `imul` that made a 7-byte
  `ThreatDelta` cost 37.73 i/n. Clean negative — the defect does not recur.**
- **"read the disassembly"**: three of its five negatives were exactly that
  failure mode — `for t in 0..TILES` cost **+9.6 i/n** because LLVM keeps a real
  induction variable and won't unroll; `as_chunks` bought −0.5 because
  `Aligned::deref` is itself a bounds-checked slice; sized arrays flipped
  `skip_dot_i8` out of line for +7 i/n until `#[inline(always)]`. **All caught by
  `objdump`/`nm`, none by thinking harder.**

### One more operational hazard

**The sota test suite OOM-kills itself in a 3 GB cgroup at 16 test threads and
truncates SILENTLY — no `test result` line, which reads exactly like a hang.**
`RUST_TEST_THREADS=3` + 4 GB gives 799 passed / 0 failed. Worth knowing before
diagnosing a "hung" gate.

### ★ THE NPS RATIO GAIN IS MOSTLY BOX DRIFT — do not quote 13.3%

Three paired ARCHON-vs-SF measurements today, same tool, same protocol:

| when | ARCHON | SF | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|
| morning | 0.951 | **1.916** | 0.496x |
| after 18–20 | 0.985 | **1.809** | 0.545x |
| after 21–25 | 0.969 | **1.724** | 0.5619x |

Read naively that is **+13.3% relative**. **It is not our gain.** Look at SF's
column: it fell **10%** across the session while never changing a byte. ARCHON's
own absolute moved **0.951 → 0.969, +1.9%**.

Within a run the alternating design controls for load. But the two engines have
very different memory footprints and need not respond identically to
contention, so **comparing ratios across runs hours apart is NOT clean either**.
This tick's spreads were 8.3%/6.9% with a `rustc` live, and the 0.545 → 0.5619
step is SMALLER than that spread — not separately resolvable at all.

**WHAT TO QUOTE: −7.97% instructions/node** (7358.4 → 6771.7, node-identical,
PGO, CGU=1, against a **0.000%** null). At constant IPC that implies ~+8.7% nps,
which is consistent with ARCHON's own absolutes once the busier box is allowed
for.

**The "how far from Stockfish" question needs a quiet box with the trainer
PAUSED.** Any cross-engine number taken now is contaminated. Recording this
because "13.3% faster vs Stockfish" is exactly the sort of figure that becomes a
headline and then a premise.

Owner: *"It isn't width. Our net is only barely larger than SF."* Correct:

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ARCHON | 1280 | 0.851 | — | — | — |
| SF `eca43a97` | 1024 | 1.572 | 1.25x faster | **1.85x** | **1.48x** |
| Reckless | 768 | 1.120 | 1.67x faster | 1.32x | 0.79x |

**Width buys SF 1.25x of a 1.85x gap. ~1.48x is NOT width.** I had commissioned
study 28 partly to normalise for width; that would have spent it on the smallest
term. Brief corrected mid-flight.

### ★★ THE RECKLESS PGO QUESTION — I got this wrong TWICE, record it

Owner asked whether `engines/bin/reckless` was PGO-built. My answers, in order:

1. **"Almost certainly not PGO"** — based on their default `make` target being a
   plain `cargo rustc --release`, and `cargo-pgo` not being installed here.
   **Overstated.**
2. Owner: *"A release build could be PGO."* **Correct, and my logic was wrong** —
   PGO builds ARE release builds, just with `-Cprofile-use`. "It is a release
   build" proves nothing. And `cargo-pgo` being absent only rules out building it
   HERE; it says nothing about a downloaded release artifact.
3. Then the binary evidence came back leaning the OTHER way:

| | embedded source paths |
|---|---|
| our `archon-s25` (built here) | `/rustc/8bab26f4…/library/alloc/src` — full rustc hash |
| `reckless` | `/alloc/src`, `/board`, `/core/src` — **remapped/trimmed** |

Trimmed paths indicate a DISTRIBUTION build, and engine projects normally ship
PGO'd release artifacts. **So the evidence now leans toward it BEING PGO'd.**

**CONCLUSION: undeterminable from the binary — exactly where the Stockfish PGO
question landed earlier tonight, where the empirical race proved my suspicion
WRONG and the shipped SF binary already was profile-built.** The Reckless column
is unreliable **in an unknown direction** until study 28's `target-native28` /
`target-pgo28` builds race the shipped binary.

**Method note on my own failure:** twice I asserted a direction from indirect
evidence when the direct test (build it, race it) was available and is the only
thing that has ever settled this class of question here.

**Unaffected by any of it:** both SF and ARCHON are confirmed PGO'd and were
measured in the same run on the same box, so **the ~1.48x non-width gap to
Stockfish stands.** That is what study 28 is for.

## ★★★ D49'S EVAL FRAME IS 0.35x SF — STRUCTURAL, AND A GAME TEST NOW WOULD MEASURE THE FRAME, NOT THE NET

Packed `d49-1280-sb80-ema.cenn` and scale-checked:

```
vs D48X:  median(cand/ref) = 0.589   IQR (0.49,0.73)  sign-agree 99.2%  -> FAIL (band 0.85-1.18)
vs SF:    median(ours/sf)  = 0.350                                      -> ABSOLUTE FAIL (band 0.80-1.25)
```

**It is NOT converging** — checked three checkpoints:

| checkpoint | median(ours/sf) |
|---|---|
| sb40 | 0.343 |
| sb60 | 0.350 |
| sb80 | 0.350 |

Flat. **Structural property of D49's training, not an early-run transient.**

**This is the OPPOSITE direction from D48Y2** (1.026x, absolute PASS) and it is
the EXPENSIVE direction: our pruning margins are fixed cp subtractions, so a
too-small eval OVER-PRUNES, and the measured asymmetry finding prices that at
**−80 Elo** (D48Y2 calibrated down went −111 → −191).

**LIKELY MECHANISM — `qp_asymmetry = 0.2287`.** It penalises overestimation ~23%
harder than underestimation (`err *= (sig > target) * qp_asymmetry + 1`), so the
network is trained to systematically UNDER-predict; in expected-score space that
pulls output toward 0.5, which in cp space is a smaller magnitude. SF ships this
constant, but with their own co-tuned output scale. **We adopted the constant
without re-deriving the scale that goes with it.** Hypothesis, not yet isolated —
`net_offset`/`net_scale` and `pow_exp 2.5` are also in the same run.

**CONSEQUENCES:**

1. **NO GAME TEST OF D49 UNTIL THE SCALE IS CALIBRATED.** A screen right now
   would measure an −80-Elo-class frame error and read as "D49 is terrible".
   That is exactly the trap that made D48Y2's numbers unreadable for hours.
2. **`calibrate_network_scale.sh` is MANDATORY for D49** — runbook step 3, which
   I deliberately SKIPPED for D48Y2 because its frame was already right at 1.026x.
   For D49 it is not optional. Scale check reports an EVAL_SCALE parity point of
   **3158** against the current export scale of 400.
3. **The heldout R² is unaffected** — it is computed in the training frame, on
   the raw net, and does not care about the export scale. R² 0.8735 at sb80
   stands.

**Deferred the sb80 screen deliberately.** Packing and scale-checking cost
minutes; a 1500-game screen would have cost 15 and produced a number about the
wrong thing.

## ★★★ STUDIES 26 & 27 — and the SF accumulator contradiction is RESOLVED

### ★ THE ACCUMULATOR QUESTION: study 22 read the OLDER revision, study 26 is right

| revision | date | accumulator |
|---|---|---|
| `sf_18` | 2026-01-31 | **TWO** separate stacks (`psq_accumulators` + `threat_accumulators`) |
| **`eca43a97`** | **2026-07-06** | **ONE MERGED** — `acc[k] = vec_add_16(acc[k], vec_convert_8_16(column[k]))` |

`eca43a97` is **223 commits NEWER**. **So Stockfish moved FROM two stacks TO one
merged accumulator.** Study 22 read `sf_18`, concluded "SF has two, our design is
fine", and closed the merge. **Study 26 cited `eca43a97:nnue_accumulator.cpp`
correctly and I verified it.** The direction of travel is TOWARD merging.

**But study 22's blocker was a MEASUREMENT, not a citation, and it stands:**
merge saves ≤157 i/n, costs **+872 i/n** in refresh-trigger union. Study 26 hits
the SAME wall from the other side — "blocked on the Finny cache key, which cannot
cache a threat-contaminated accumulator". Two studies, opposite approaches, one
obstacle. **What changed: SF evidently solved it, so it is solvable.** Study 26
estimates 60–120 i/n for the merge.

### Study 26 — −55.61 i/n (−0.959%), −6.31 br/n

**Ran the raw-pointer probe FIRST as instructed: −21.93 i/n.** Study 25's kill
line was "−20 or better kills it" — survives by 1.93, but study 25's own
prediction of **−35 to −60 is REFUTED by its own probe**.

| arm | Δ i/n | Δ br/n |
|---|---|---|
| a1 `Aligned::deref` → `get_unchecked` (invariant argued + debug-checked) | −8.30 | −2.98 |
| a2 head tables → `Blocks<T, N, BUCKETS>` | −17.94 | −3.33 |
| a3 `l3_dot` → dispatched SIMD kernel | **−22.35** | 0.00 |
| a4 two never-loaded heads `#[cold] #[inline(never)]` | −7.03 | 0.00 |

`forward_into`: **6114 → 3655 bytes, 1274 → 774 instructions, 69 → 19 branches.**

**a2 BEAT THE UNSAFE PROBE BY 4.31 i/n WITH NO `unsafe`.** Against a runtime
length LLVM could not fold the bounds check; against the CONSTANT `BUCKETS` it
discharges it statically, because `OUTPUT_BUCKETS_LAYOUT` is a const table whose
entries are all < 8. **Stating the shape in the type let the compiler prove a
bound it had been re-testing five times per eval.**

**a3 was INVISIBLE to symbol profiling** — one source line (`propagate_l3`'s FMA)
was 4.56% of `forward_into` for a *32-element* dot, compiled to sixteen 2-lane
`vfmadd213ps` fed by sixteen `vmovsd` pair-loads. **Found only by joining
`objdump -d -l` to `perf annotate`.**

**It closed a hole in OUR GATE:** `tools/perf26/nnuepins26.sh` reproduces both
NNUE pins from an **`x86-64-v3`** build. The gate builds v3, so it exercises the
**AVX2 backend that a `target-cpu=native`-only study never runs** — a gap open
through every prior study. Its `probe26.sh` also deletes the thin-LTO/CGU=4
override: at CGU=1 the inert-source null is **0.000 i/n / 0.000 br/n**.

### Study 27 — −1.087% instructions, −0.972% branches, and a USEFUL NEGATIVE

I aimed it at branch misses. **They did not improve** — +0.747% inside a null of
[−0.770, +0.892]. It says so plainly. **Two attributions I handed it are
RETRACTED with measurements:**

- **`do_move`'s capture test is NOT a mispredict source.** Arm 1b genuinely
  removes the branch (verified `cmove`/unconditional load/`sbb` in the
  disassembly) and misses do not move. The picker emits all captures then all
  quiets, so the test is a run of 1s then a run of 0s. **And `do_move` is 5.49%
  of engine misses, not the 7.22% I passed on.**
- **In the sort it is the WALK, not the filter** (~41% vs ~11% of `init_quiets`'
  misses). That inverted its plan and produced arm 2 instead.

| arm | Δ |
|---|---|
| a5 `#[inline(always)]` on `next_move` | **−37.61 i/n, −6.20 br/n** |
| a2 `ExtMove` padding hole closed, `value` to offset 0 | −21.11 i/n, −8.16 L1d miss/n |
| a3 stage transitions as literals | −2.97 i/n |
| a4 state stack slab + cursor (study 24's named spelling) | −1.50 i/n |

**a5 unparked study 23's top remainder by measuring the DENOMINATOR:** total L1d
*loads* fall 4.1/node, so the "+7.5 misses" was misses without loads — real
figure ~+2.4.

**a2 found a DOC that was wrong:** design/02 §3.8 claimed 8-byte `ExtMove`s "move
as single u64 loads/stores"; an implicit padding hole made that illegal, so every
sort shift step was **2 loads + 2 stores**. Now 1 + 1. Doc amended.

**A constraint I inherited and passed on was DISSOLVED:** the two-pass split of
`partial_insertion_sort` **IS** provably tail-permutation-exact (insertions only
write `head[q..=se]`; the next swap targets `se+1`). Study 23's "no reordering
survives node identity" was wrong. What actually kills it is +25 i/n to buy the
wrong 11%.

## ★★★ THE NPS GAP IS 1.37x QUIET, NOT 1.85x — my tool was the fault, twice

Study 28 and I disagreed (my ~1.67-1.85x vs their 1.37x). **They localised it in
one move: their SF figure matches mine to 0.2% (1.821 vs 1.825). Both harnesses
agree on Stockfish — so it was entirely about how ARCHON is COUNTED.**

**Proof, by hardware breakpoint on `Board::make_move_with`** (fires once per node,
cannot be fooled by printed output), at my exact protocol:

```
BUDGET      6,000,000
TRUE NODES  6,039,808   100.66%   <- ARCHON HONOURS THE BUDGET
REPORTED    4,483,696    74.73%   <- it just never PRINTS the last partial iteration
MY RULE     4,532,735    75.55%   <- my "fix" recovered almost nothing
```

**MY FIX WAS ALSO WRONG.** I guarded with `last >= nodes * 0.90`, believing it
detected early stops. It actually tests whether the last ITERATION finished near
the budget — **which ARCHON fails by construction on most positions** — so the
fallback took the under-reported count and discarded the correction. That is why
I measured +4.6% from a fix worth ~27%. Replaced with mate-detection.

| currency | ARCHON | SF | ratio | SF faster |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| my 0.90 guard | 0.885 | 1.594 | 0.5553 | **1.801x** |
| budget (breakpoint-verified) | 1.119 | 1.594 | 0.7020 | **1.425x** |

They reproduced my tool's number to 0.3% (0.5553 vs 0.5568) before correcting it
— which is what proves the fault is currency, not either engine's speed.

**Residual 1.41x (my protocol) vs 1.37x (theirs) is BOX LOAD, and it is
ASYMMETRIC** — a real finding:

| | ARCHON cyc/n | SF cyc/n | ratio |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| quiet | 4169.3 | 3032.4 | **1.375x** |
| loaded | 5086.4 | 3441.3 | **1.478x** |

We are 59% load-stalled against SF's 50%, so L3 contention lengthens OUR stalls
more. **The mechanism this study is about shows up in its own error bars.**

**JOINT STATEMENT: the gap is 1.37x quiet, up to 1.48x loaded. 1.67-1.85x is not
reproducible with a correct node currency.** Every figure I quoted tonight above
1.5x was my instrument.

**Study 28 also corrected ITSELF** — its §5.9 estimated the under-report at 28%
from reported-vs-budget rather than from truth; measured, ARCHON reports
74.7-77.9% of true, i.e. 1.28-1.34x. Retracted in its doc.

**THE REAL FIX IS IN THE ENGINE, NOT THE TOOL:** ARCHON should emit a final
`info` line when the search stops, as SF does (100.06% of budget). UCI-only,
bench-neutral, no `Bench:` movement — and it retires the whole class. **Every
future tool reading our node count over UCI has this bug waiting for it.**

Two suspects of mine, killed: TT semantics (both benches issue exactly one
`ucinewgame` — `bench.rs:252`, `eca43a97:benchmark.cpp:430`) and Hash 64 vs 16
(64 is faster for BOTH and NARROWS the ratio).

## ★★★ THE D49 QAT/EXPORT MISMATCH — my config error, and it invalidates every D49 game so far

**D49 trains with `qat_threat_scale = 256`** (weights clipped to ±127/256 =
±0.496) **but every deployment path is hardcoded `THREAT_QUANT = 64`**
(`netpack.py:208`, `spec.py:82`, `sota_head.rs:129`). So the exporter writes
those clipped weights at scale 64 and they land in ±32 of ±127.

```
D48Y2-sb190 (no QAT):   threat i8  max|w| 127/127   levels 247/256
D49-sb100   (QAT @256): threat i8  max|w|  32/127   levels  65/256   <- 25% of range
```

**Two bits thrown away on 73% of the input space.** D49 is damaged twice: trained
for a 1/256 grid, deployed on 1/64; and range-starved because the QAT clip was
sized for the finer scale.

**I set `qat_threat_scale = 256` in the training config and never changed the
deployment side.** Nothing in the system objected for 100+ superbatches — the
trainer and the exporter each had a constant and they were allowed to disagree.

**Invisible to everything except games**, because R² is computed on the FLOAT
net: heldout R² 0.8757, within 0.008 of D48X, while the net played 129 Elo down.

### The two matches (owner asked for calibrated AND uncalibrated)

| | n | W/L/D | Elo vs D48X |
|---|---|---|---|
| **calibrated** (scale 400→666, frame 0.601→0.974) | 1600 | 241/808/551 | **−128.7 ± 9.6** |
| **native** (uncalibrated) | 1164 | 168/626/370 | **−144.5 ± 11.2** |
| | | | **FRAME COST +15.8** |

**Calibration is worth +15.8 Elo** — real, outside the bars, and the first direct
measurement of frame cost at a KNOWN mis-scaling. It corroborates the earlier
asymmetry finding (−80 Elo when D48Y2 was calibrated DOWN) in the same direction:
under-scaled eval over-prunes.

**But the frame was 15.8 of a 144 Elo deficit.** Both matches were played by a net
missing two bits on its threat channel, so **NEITHER number is a verdict on the
six mechanisms.**

### The fix: CEN3 version guard (owner's call), study 30 commissioned

A global `THREAT_QUANT = 256` would **wreck existing nets** — D48X trained without
QAT, spans ±1.98, and measures **6.80% relative error at 255 vs 5.58% at 64**.
**The scale is a property of the NET, not the engine.**

`threat_quant` is NOT in the CENN header (`ft_quant`32 / `l1_quant`36 /
`ft_shift`40 / `network_scale`44 / `payload_len`48 — fully packed). So: **CEN3
adds the field; loader accepts CEN2 ⇒ 64 implicitly.**

> **LANDED as `CEN5`, not `CEN3` — study 30, `docs/research/perf-study-30-cenn-v5-threat-quant-2026-07-30.md`.**
> `CEN3` and `CEN4` are already the simple/protected HalfKA containers
> (`simple_halfka.rs`, `protected_halfka.rs`) and `uci.rs` routes on both, so
> `CEN3` would have misrouted a SOTA net into the HalfKA loader. Everything else
> is as specified: v5 appends `threat_quant: i32` @64 + zero `reserved: u32` @68
> to an otherwise byte-identical header, CEN2 implies 64, and all three pins
> reproduce on both backends. `netpack.py` now also REFUSES an under-range
> threat table, and `ArchSpec.threat_quant()` derives the export scale from
> `qat_threat_scale`. Cost of the net-dependent merge: **+2.77 instructions and
> +0.85 branches per node** (a runtime shift count cost +34.48 i/n — `vpsllw`'s
> register-count form cannot fold the load — so the kernels are const-generic
> over the shift).

Acceptance test given to study 30 is a hard bit-identity property: **a CEN2 net
must reproduce all three pins unchanged on BOTH backends**, including the
`x86-64-v3` AVX2 path study 26 found no `target-cpu=native` test exercises.

**D49 does NOT need restarting.** Its QAT clip (±0.496) is correct FOR scale 256,
so once CEN3 lands every checkpoint re-exports at full range. Nothing is lost by
letting it run.

**Root cause to fix structurally:** derive `QUANT_THREAT` from the trainer's
`qat_threat_scale`, or assert they agree. A silent disagreement between two
constants in different languages is what cost 100 superbatches.

### Tick — both matches FINAL at n=1600, D49 at sb122

|---|---|---|
| **calibrated** (scale 666, frame 0.974) | 1600 | **−128.7 ± 9.6** |
| **native** (scale 400, frame 0.601) | 1600 | **−142.6 ± 9.6** |
| | | **frame cost +13.9** |

Both at full n now (the earlier +15.8 was native at n=1164). **Frame correction is
worth +13.9 ± ~14 Elo** — the first direct measurement at a KNOWN mis-scaling,
same direction as the −80 Elo asymmetry finding.

**Both nets were missing two bits on the threat channel** (65/256 levels), so

lands** — it would measure the same bug again at the cost of ~30 min a run.

**DISK was the unwatched resource.** `.claude/worktrees/` had grown to **88 GB
across 47 worktrees** (~5 GB of cargo `target/` per finished study), with the
root filesystem at 89% and only 101 GB free. Reclaimed build dirs from all
NON-ACTIVE worktrees (68 dirs): **88 GB -> 9 GB, 101 GB -> 179 GB free.**
Source, git history and the three running studies were untouched — cargo
artifacts are regenerable by definition, which is what makes this safe.
*Standing habit: check `df -h /` on the tick, not just `free -m`.* A first pass
that matched only `target-*` reclaimed 111 MB; most worktrees use plain
`target/`.

**RETRACTED INSTRUMENT: file-mtime stall detection.** I have been calling a
study "stalled" when `find -newermt '-15 min'` returns 0 files. This tick it
fired on **all three studies at once** — and a `/proc/*/cwd` scan showed every
one of them mid-`cargo test` with live child processes (load average 12.04).
The heuristic cannot see work that happens inside a build. **Detect with the
cwd scan instead:**

```bash
for p in $(ls /proc | grep -E '^[0-9]+$'); do
  cw=$(readlink "/proc/$p/cwd" 2>/dev/null) || continue
  case "$cw" in *worktrees/agent-*)
    tr '\0' ' ' < "/proc/$p/cmdline" | grep -q shell-snapshots && continue
    echo "$p $(tr '\0' ' ' < /proc/$p/cmdline | head -c 80)";; esac
done
```

Note this means **the study-23 "stall" I nudged earlier may never have been
stalled** — the nudge was cheap, but the diagnosis was not evidence-based.

**Also checked and CLEARED:** all three transcripts report
`gitBranch: worktree-agent-af852c45b5086e424`, which looked like three agents
sharing one branch. Direct `git -C <worktree> rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD`
shows each is on its OWN branch; the shared value is session-level transcript
metadata, not the real checkout. No merge-integrity problem.

### FUSION: both studies now say DO NOT BUILD

Study 29 and 31 agree on misses-per-line to 8% (0.477 vs 0.516) but differ
**5.2x on stall cycles per line** (2.36 additive vs 0.45 subtractive).
Reconciliation offered: an *additive* redundant-copy probe measures marginal
cost at a capacity cliff; a *subtractive* probe measures average value below it
— both can be true. Fusion therefore spans **net +48 (a LOSS) to −136 (+4.7
Elo)**. Recommendation: **do not build; close the 5.2x gap first** (both probes
are committed, ~half-hour job). This compounds the verified point that SF
*pays* the refresh blocker, so our split accumulator is an advantage fusion
trades away — study 29 prices that too (HalfKA refreshes 0.1610/node vs
threats' 0.0214).

### A hardware trap worth more than the census

**Zen 4 double-pumps 512-bit loads: 2.0015 `L1-dcache-loads` events per
`vmovdqu64`, 1.0016 per `vmovdqu`** (10^8 issued loads each). Getting this
wrong halves the census — it is why its first draft disagreed 2x with the
sampled profile. **Relayed to study 32 immediately**, since that study is
actively counting loads on these same kernels.

- **Retraction (b):** "2a2s is a dead fold shape" (zero in 4.88M folds) — it is

- Its first probe pinned only READS and concluded the stream was free; wrong,

**No strength change landed and none claimed.**

The probes deliberately compute wrong evals (`dstpin` fails 29 tests on
purpose — a green suite would prove it inert), so a leak into a release build
would be serious. Checked: `Cargo.toml` has **no `default` key**, so the default
feature set is EMPTY; `foldcount`/`probe-accpin`/`probe-wpin`/`probe-dstpin`
are all explicit opt-ins over 35 cfg sites. Cannot leak.

**main = `ac21f34`** — studies 29 and 31 fast-forwarded after `gate2931`

### Study 30 — CENN-v5. MY SPEC WAS WRONG AND THE STUDY WAS RIGHT.

I briefed "CEN3". **`CEN3` and `CEN4` are already taken** — verified by me:
`src/eval/nnue/simple_halfka.rs:8` is `b"CEN3"`, `protected_halfka.rs:14` is
`b"CEN4"`, and `src/uci.rs:295,307` routes on both. Reusing `CEN3` would have
misrouted a SOTA net into the HalfKA loader — the exact collision a magic
exists to prevent. It shipped **`CEN5`**. This is the deviation-with-a-reason
that a study is supposed to produce instead of silent compliance.

**Layout:** the v2 header is full (all 64 bytes assigned), so v5 keeps `0..64`
byte-for-byte identical in meaning and **appends** `threat_quant: i32` @64,
zero `reserved: u32` @68, payload @72. `CEN2` implies 64. The serializer emits
the OLDEST container that can represent the net, so existing round-trips stay
byte-identical.

**The fix works on D49:** `d49-1280-sb100-ema` re-exported at 256 and packed
CEN5 goes `max|w|` **32/127 -> 127/127**, levels **65/256 -> 255/256** (healthy
reference 127/247), **3.99x less quantisation error (4.66% -> 1.17%)**. AVX2 and
AVX-512 agree on its evals. Artifact
`data/nnue/tmp/s30/d49-sb100-ema-q256.cenn`.

**The runtime shift is NOT free — measured, null first (0.000% i/n, twice):**

| arm | i/n | delta |
|---|---:|---:|
| baseline | 5654.23 | — |
| runtime vector shift count | 5688.71 | **+34.48 (+0.610%)** |
| plumbing only, immediate restored | 5654.23 | **+0.00** |
| **const-generic (landed)** | 5657.00 | **+2.77 (+0.049%)** |

The diagnostic arm is decisive: keeping ALL the new plumbing but pinning the
shift back to an immediate returns exactly to baseline ⇒ **the plumbing costs
zero; the entire cost is one instruction form.** `VPSLLW`'s memory operand is
the *count*, not the data, so the immediate form folds the load
(`vpsllw $0x2,(%rcx,%r8,2),%zmm3`) while a register count needs a separate
`vmovdqu64` per merge (80/eval at ACC=1280) and loses the unroll. Const-generic
over `{0,1,2}` recovers 31.7 of 34.5, exhaustive by construction because
`threat_shift_for` rejects anything outside `{64,128,256}` — no panic arm.

**Three latent bugs found and fixed**, plus a detector: `netpack.py` now refuses
to pack a threat table that doesn't reach half the i8 range (verified against
the live D49 `quant.bin` — it dies and writes nothing). *That is the check that
would have caught the original mismatch at hour 0.*

**It re-derived the Elo cost rather than quoting my brief:** the -125.1 +/- 20.9
(n=336) I supplied was superseded by main's `ce42c6d` mid-study; final is
**-128.7 +/- 9.6 calibrated / -142.6 +/- 9.6 native, both n=1600**.

## Study 30 LANDED — and the CEN5 pipeline is validated END TO END

**main = `c0f01ed`.** `gate30` green: **Bench 587683, All checks passed,

-> exit 0). That is what confirms the `sota_simd.rs` conflict resolution

### The export path works on a live checkpoint

Ran the full recipe against **D49 sb140-ema** (not study 30's sb100):

```
requant_threats.py --scale 256 --run-spec tools/nnue/runs/d49_1280.json
D40_THREAT_QUANT=256 netpack.py ... d40
```

- threat weights n=77,721,600, RMS 0.08380, max|w| 0.4953
- **scale 64: err 4.89% (26.2 dB) -> scale 256: err 1.23% (38.2 dB), 3.98x
  better, ZERO weights clipped**
- container magic is **`CEN5`** (`4345 4e35`), threat_quant=256, merge shift 0,
  138,283,648 bytes -> `data/nnue/tmp/s32/d49-sb140-ema-q256.cenn`
- **the canonical engine cannot load it** (0 `CEN5` strings — it predates the
  format), so a fresh `sota,arch-d48x` v3 build was needed and made.

### A measurement trap I walked into and corrected

My first load check drove UCI over a pipe and got `bestmove a2a3` with no
`info` lines from **all three** of D49, D48X **and no-net-at-all**. That looked
like "EvalFile is not taking effect" — the PSQT-fallback trap `tools/screen.sh`
warns about. It was not: stdin closed and aborted the search before it started.
**My driving was broken, not the engine.** Re-tested via the `bench` subcommand
(net as the 6th arg, cannot be raced):

| net | nodes to depth 8 | nps |
|---|---:|---:|
| D49 sb140-ema (CEN5@256) | 261,567 | 740,983 |
| D48X canonical | 196,861 | 903,032 |
| PSQT, no net | 1,225,982 | — |

Three distinct results ⇒ EvalFile is applied. **D49 needs 33% MORE nodes to
reach depth 8**, consistent with an undertrained net at 36% of schedule giving
less decisive evals. (The nps gap is NOT a kernel regression — study 30
measured the const-generic shift at +0.049% i/n. Different nets give different
trees, and bench nps is confounded by tree shape. Not a finding.)

Study 32 is **already running its own `tools/screen.sh`** (~13 engine
processes, no `perf` active — it is in its games phase). Launching a second
screen would have:

   available with a 3 GB trainer to protect, and the standing order is DON'T
   OOM THE BOX;
2. **bought almost nothing** — D49 at sb140/400 is 36% through a from-scratch
   schedule. The 33%-more-nodes result already indicates an undertrained net;
   spending ~30 min of contended box to confirm "it is weaker than a fully
   trained net" is not information.

**The informative screen is post-cosine (sb250+), and best at sb400.** The
"test it" half of the user's sequence IS now discharged: the CEN5 path is
proven end to end on a live checkpoint — export, container, engine load, and
distinct search behaviour.

**D49 sb157/400**, active. Heldout 0.012593 @ sb150 (ema 0.011987).

### The scare, and the correction

The logged `pos/s` declines monotonically and looked like progressive
starvation:

| sb | reported pos/s |
|---|---|
| 10 | 198,756 |
| 60 | 192,625 |
| 110 | 181,787 |
| 135 | 177,210 |
| 157 | 174,675 |

That is **~12% off peak** and perfectly smooth — which is the signature of a
*cumulative* average, not an instantaneous rate. Checked it against journal
timestamps instead of trusting the printed field:

- window **sb78 -> sb157: 153.6 s/sb**
- **last 10 sb only: 154.7 s/sb**

Two windows, same answer ⇒ **the rate is STABLE and the trainer is not being
progressively starved.** The declining figure is a cumulative average
converging down toward the true instantaneous rate. **ETA to sb400: 10.4 h.**

*Method note, consistent with the standing rules: a smooth monotonic trend in a
derived statistic is a reason to re-derive it from raw timestamps, not to
diagnose a cause.*

currently 0.00. So it has bounced off the 9 GB limit tens of thousands of times
at some point in the run, without ever being killed. The counter is cumulative
since unit start, so I **cannot** attribute those events to any particular
period — flagging as a watch item, not diagnosing it. `cpu.pressure` some

- **The pre-registered offline fidelity bar FAILED** (p95 4.35 vs 3.0, p99 7.25
  vs 5.0) and **no post-training scale scheme reaches it**. Written up as a
  failure, not a footnote: the bar was miscalibrated, and *the games are what

- **The weight-MSE proxy MISRANKS.** An MSE-optimal scale is 11.5% better on L2
  and **11% worse at eval p95**. Optimising the proxy actively hurts the thing

  successor.

Verified by me on a throwaway branch: **25 hunks across 6 files**
(`.gitignore`, `sota_accumulator.rs`, `sota_head.rs`, `sota_simd.rs`,

## Perf study 33 COMPLETE and MERGED (`265de05`) — the 19.90% symbol is not what we thought

6 commits, tree clean. **`src/` is byte-identical to main — verified by me
(zero diff) — so `Bench: 587683` is unchanged by construction** and the merge
carries only docs + `tools/perf33/`.

### It reframed the target rather than optimising it

1. **The site reproduces.** 410.5 stall cycles/node in the probe frame, on a
   statistic whose byte-identical-copy null is **+0.80%**. Study 28 did not
   measure a build artifact.
2. **"19.90% of the profile" is roughly HALF AN INLINING BOUNDARY.** Same
   source, PGO on: 410.5 -> **759.7** cycles/node while `Worker::do_move`
   collapses 116.6 -> 18.0. By DWARF inline frame, only **32.87% of the PGO
   symbol is search's own code**: `do_move` 26.12%, `next_move` 6.38%, `legal`
   4.96%, `all_threats` 4.55%, `undo_move` 3.56%, TT `probe` 3.17%,
   `correction_value` 2.91%, `see_ge` 2.79%. *Study 28 wrote exactly this
   caveat about Reckless and Stockfish and never applied it to ARCHON's own PGO
   build.*
3. **14.5% is NNUE eviction, measured causally** via study 29's `probe-wpin`
   (~59 cycles/node) — but 85.5% survives with every weight miss deleted.
4. **THE MECHANISM: cost tracks LOAD COUNT, not miss count.** Its share of
   engine stall cycles (8.80%) equals its share of retired load ops (**8.84%**)
   to two decimals, while it holds **1.69%** of misses and **91.4% of its loads
   hit L1**. Calibrated against `forward_into` 0.28 (overlapped streaming) and
   `apply_at` 6.60 (serial). **This is study 31's null with a reason attached,
   and it FORBIDS a third locality proposal here.**
5. **`search::<NonPv>` is the engine's #1 branch-mispredict symbol** — 17.47%
   of engine mispredicts, 4.25/node vs engine mean 24.1. Nobody had ever
   looked. (This was one of the six angles I named in the brief.)

### Its retraction, caught by its own null

Commit `0e0d217`'s engine-wide load table was **wrong**: the IP->symbol map used
the mapping's **file offset** where `nm` reports **ELF virtual addresses**,
which differ by 0x1000 here. It resolved every sample, looked plausible, and
summed to 100%. **Its own null caught it** (`i16_deltas_into` 0.30% where perf
said 13.80%); after the fix the worst disagreement over 14 symbols is **0.00
points**. The `perf`-symbolized headlines were unaffected.

### No speedup landed — an honest failure, not a quit

It designed one arm (skip `correction_value` in check) and **killed it before
writing code** — an exhaustive grep showed the value is live at `mod.rs:3459`
and `3590` inside the move loop. Two more rejected with reasons.

**Its caveats, recorded:** the stall/load index of 1.00 is a ratio of two
profiles and **the ratio has no null of its own** ("tidier than it has any
right to be" — it only defends it because of the 0.28/6.60 calibration pair);
the PGO comparison is a single build; and **the box was NOT quiet** (trainer at
143% plus study 32 recording on CPU 6) so **absolute numbers are inflated
~1.9x — it defends the ratios, not 410.5.** *That vindicates the coordination
protocol and argues for tightening it.*

## Study 35 commissioned — the do_move fusion, re-priced in the right currency

Study 33's parting lead, **verified by me before commissioning**: `do_move` is
198.4 cycles/node, and its two hottest lines are `DirtyPiece::from_move`
re-deriving mover/victim/ep/castling geometry that `make_move_with` derives
again immediately after. **Perf study 13 rejected fusing them — at `+41.5`
instructions/node** (`perf-study-13-panic-edges-2026-07-29.md:179`, line
`a2-dirty-via-hookc 6573.8 +41.5 rejected`).

**That currency is now proven backwards twice on this engine:** study 20
(−5.99% cycles, instructions **+0.19%**) and tonight's study 32 (−6.835%
cycles, instructions **+5.519%**, 8/8 rounds, shipped). A +41.5 i/n rejection
is not evidence.

**And the mechanism agrees with the lead:** study 33 proved cost here tracks
**load count**, not miss count — so locality proposals are forbidden and
load-count reduction is mandated. Removing a duplicate geometry derivation is
precisely a load-count reduction.

Brief includes the **mandatory D24 perft ritual** (`make_move_with` is
board-mutation code: `cargo test` AND `cargo test --release -- --ignored
perft_full_suite` before landing, extended tier if make/unmake is substantially
rewritten), the expectation that this is **node-identical so Bench 587683 must
reproduce bit-for-bit**, a warning that study 32's merge will move the two NNUE
pins but not Bench, and the contention protocol with study 33's ~1.9x inflation
cited as the reason it exists.

## Study 32 MERGED to main (`fbabe54`) — the i8 HalfKA transformer is in

Fast-forward, 33 files, +8097/-505. `check.sh` now declares **Bench 587683 /
eval `eb981b119bd6…c84f4f37` / d13 1985398**. My own `gate32` run is in flight
(full `CHECK_NNUE_GATE=1` **plus** a `cargo check` of all four probe features,
which the default gate cannot see).

### The control converted the guess into a measurement — exactly as intended

Built `--features foldcount,arch-d48x` and ran `tools/perf29/census29.sh`:

| statistic | measured | established | delta |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| HalfKA refresh rate/node | 0.16020 | 0.16102 | −0.51% |
| HalfKA refresh rows/node | 0.91017 | 0.91320 | −0.33% |
| HalfKA rows/fold | 2.27725 | 2.27500 | +0.10% |
| HalfKA fold rate/node | 0.97818 | 0.97490 | +0.34% |
| evals/node | 0.42716 | 0.43070 | −0.82% |

All five within 0.9%, and the residual is *expected*: the eval changed so the
tree is not node-identical (5,001,624 vs 5,001,562). Counters are exact
integers, so a mis-wiring would appear as a **missing or doubled site, not
0.3%**. `FOLDREGION hka_weights bytes=28835840` — exactly half of 57,671,680,
correctly attributed.

**MY BRIEF CONTAINED AN ERROR AND THE STUDY CAUGHT IT.** I told it to verify
`pushes/node == 1.0000` as one of the four controls. `foldcount` has **no
pushes-per-node counter** — that was study 31's throwaway build — so the study
correctly declined to claim it rather than inventing a number that matched.
Recording this because it is the failure mode I keep warning subagents about,
and here it ran in the other direction.

### The channel-separation decision

Both transformers now fold i8 rows, so `i8` no longer distinguishes them, yet
study 29 counts them at *separate* sites (`HKA_INTO`/`note_hka_shape` vs
`THR_INTO`/`note_thr_shape`). Collapsing to one entry would have **silently
conflated the census**. Resolved as **two entry points over one kernel, named
by channel** (`apply_halfka_deltas{,_into,_dual}`, `apply_threat_deltas_into`),
every counter and `pin_rows!`/`pin_src!`/`pin_dst!` site kept on the channel it
measures. The composition is
`clamp(pst[j]*ft_scale[j] + (threat[j] << threat_shift), 0, 255)` with
study 30's const-generic `{0,1,2}` dispatch and exhaustive arm preserved.
