`data/nnue/tb/` holds `d47b`, `d47c`, `d47c-stage2`, `d47d-stage3`,
`d47e-stage4` … and **no `d49`**. The run has been dark since launch. The
standing rule from the 2026-07-22 twin is *every training run gets tb telemetry
armed AT LAUNCH*; I launched D49 without checking. **Nothing is lost** — the shim
backfills from the log, which had everything — but it should not have needed
backfilling.

Armed: `d49shim-extend.service` → `data/nnue/tb/d49-1280/extend`, and
`tbserver.service` on **http://127.0.0.1:6006/** (serving all of `data/nnue/tb`,
so D49 charts against the d47/d48 history). Both transient **services**, so they

run's JAX format including the `ema` column.

### Two bugs found arming it, one of them general

1. **`systemd-run --user` starts in `$HOME`, not the repo.** The shim failed with
   `can't open file '/home/nick/tools/nnue/tb_shim.py'` because I passed relative
   paths. **Every service launched this session could have failed the same way** —
   audited: `d49x`, `oomguard`, `tbserver`, `d49read` all pass ABSOLUTE script
   paths and are unaffected. Fix is `--working-directory=`.
2. **`tensorboard` is not on PATH** — it runs via
   `uv run --with tensorboard python -m tensorboard.main`.

### Orphan from the cancelled study, and a THIRD oomguard leak

Killing the representation agent left `reprgap/kpk.py probe` alive holding
**6417 MB**, which had driven available memory to **1577 MB — below the floor**.
Killed by PID; **7.5 GB reclaimed**. **`oomguard` v3 missed it**: it discovers
candidates with `pgrep -f "$REPO"` (absolute path) and that process ran with a
RELATIVE path. Third leak, same root cause every time — **matching on how a
process is NAMED rather than what it IS.**

## D49 EQUAL-NODE READ: −25.88 ± 11.60 Elo — and the silent-failure trap that nearly ate the verdict

**Result (1466 games, fixed 25k nodes, D49 sb580-EMA @756 vs canonical D48X @600,
each net at its OWN optimum, same binary both sides):**

| | |
|---|---|
| **Elo** | **−25.88 ± 11.60** |
| score | 46.28% (W 404 / L 513 / D 549) |
| Ptnml(0–2) | [37, 232, 295, 141, 28] |

Behind, as expected mid-run, with 110 sb and the whole anneal left.

### The first run of this screen was MEANINGLESS and looked fine

It returned **−1106.98 ± 234.39: 0 wins, 1461 losses, 5 draws.** Cause:
`tools/screen.sh` defaults to `ENGINE=engines/canonical/archon`, **which predates
CENN-v5**, and `netpack` with `D40_THREAT_QUANT` emits CEN5:

```
ERROR EvalFile: CENN bad magic: [67, 69, 78, 53]   ([C,E,N,5])
```

**The EvalFile load failed, NNUE switched off, and CAND played 1466 games on
PSQT** — while `screen.sh` reported a well-formed result with tight error bars.
Confirmed: the same binary loads the canonical net (`info string eval:
NNUE(sota-v2)`), and benching the D49 net through it gives the PSQT node count.

**The giveaway was the DRAW COUNT, not the Elo — five draws in 1466.** A net that
is merely weak still draws. That is not a signal anyone should have to notice.

**This was aimed at the sb700 verdict**: `d49_verdict.sh` prints screen
invocations that inherit the same default engine, so the real verdict would have
compared PSQT against canonical and declared D49 destroyed.

### Fix: a LOAD GUARD in `screen.sh`, before a single game is played

Both nets must load in the chosen `ENGINE` **and** report `info string eval:
NNUE` — otherwise it exits 1 with the engine's own error. Verified both
directions: it rejects `engines/canonical/archon` + the CEN5 net with the exact
message above, and passes `target-sota` + both nets through to the match phase.

## ★ RETRACTION — the pre-registered sb700 range was ill-formed. LOSS IS NOT ELO.

**Owner, verbatim: "Loss is not Elo. It never has been."**

I pre-registered a −15 to −1 Elo range for sb700 by taking a heldout delta and
multiplying it by a 12–28e-6-per-Elo "exchange rate". **That range is retracted as
ill-formed — not re-estimated.** There is no such rate.

**Why it is wrong in KIND:** heldout loss is a **mean over a position
distribution**; Elo is determined by **decisions at decision points**. A net can
shave average error on quiet positions it already scored adequately and gain
nothing, or sharpen exactly where games turn and gain a lot with flat loss.
Nothing makes those proportional.

**The project already had this rule one level up**, from the blunder work:
*"Centipawns are not additive into outcomes… small inaccuracies are mostly
absorbed, while one 126 cp error at a decision point converts a result."* **Loss →
Elo is that identical error applied to the training metric**, and I imported it
while believing I was respecting the games-arbitrate rule.

**The receipt, from today's own runs:**

| | heldout | games (25k nodes) |
|---|---:|---:|
| sb580 | — | **−25.88 ± 11.60** (n=1466) |
| sb620 | **−44e-6** vs sb580 | **+5.3 ± 21.2** (n=327, running) |
| implied by the fake rate | "1.6–3.7 Elo" | **~30 Elo observed** |

**Off by an order of magnitude.** That is not a mis-calibrated constant; it is a
quantity that does not exist.

**What loss is for from here:** divergence, NaN and gross-regression detection —
the abort conditions — and ranking arms *within* a panel where everything else is
held fixed. **It is never a strength estimate and is never converted to Elo.**
Strength comes from games; the 5880-game verdict is the only thing that answers
sb700.

Also running: sb620 @ 5k finished at **−9.0 ± 12.1** (1000 games); sb620 @ 25k in
flight to 2000 games (bar ±8.6).

## ★ THE WARM RESTART IS WORTH +17.1 ELO — and I nearly re-measured what was already known

**sb620 @25k COMPLETE: −4.2 ± 8.6 over 2000 games.**

| checkpoint | Elo vs canonical @600 | n | interval |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| **sb400** — where the run would have STOPPED | **−21.24 ± 5.67** | **5880** | [−26.9, −15.6] |
| sb580 | −25.9 ± 10.0 | 1466 | [−35.9, −15.9] |
| **sb620** | **−4.2 ± 8.6** | 2000 | [−12.7, +4.4] |
| sb620 @ **5k** nodes | −9.0 ± 12.1 | 1000 | — |

**⇒ the extension is +17.1 Elo over sb400, intervals DISJOINT**, with 65
superbatches of anneal still to run.

**And the shape matters as much as the number: it went DOWN before it went up.**
sb580 is no better than sb400 and possibly worse — matching the loss curve, where
the restart cost **476e-6** and took ~130 superbatches to repay. **A warm restart
is a debt before it is a dividend**, which constrains any cyclical schedule: the
period must be long enough to clear the recovery, or you pay the cost repeatedly
and never collect.

### My error: I asserted sb400 was unmeasured

I proposed spending 20 minutes measuring sb400 "because we have never measured
it". **It was measured at 5880 games — the most precise number in this entire
run** — and the owner corrected me. I made a **negative existence claim without
grepping**, which my own standing note records as the worst kind. The record was
in this very document at lines 7096–7464.

### What the literature says (checked, not recalled)

**Stockfish's `nnue-pytorch` uses `StepLR`, gamma 0.992 per epoch — a monotone
exponential decay. No cycling, no restarts.** Their docs add that nets "mature"
around 400 epochs but are "very competitive after only 100" — i.e. a long flat
tail, exactly the regime where a loss-based stop misleads. Cosine-with-warm-
restarts (SGDR) and cyclical LR are textbook in general ML; **I could find no
NNUE-specific application.** That is a weak negative — the community publishes in
Discord and commits, not papers — but combined with SF shipping a plain `StepLR`,
the obvious upgrade appears untried in this domain.

**Caveat kept:** gamma 0.992 compounded over hundreds of epochs is itself a very
long smooth anneal, so SF's schedule may already capture much of what a restart
provides, spread out.

## ★ THE GAP IS DEPTH-INDEPENDENT — a flat evaluator deficit, not a search interaction

**D49 sb640-EMA @756 vs canonical D48X @600, 500 games at each node budget:**

| nodes | n | score | Elo |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| 2,000 | 500 | 49.5% | −3.5 ± 17.1 |
| 5,000 | 500 | 47.5% | −17.4 ± 17.1 |
| 10,000 | 500 | 48.5% | −10.4 ± 17.1 |
| 25,000 | 334 | 47.6% | −16.7 ± 21.0 |
| **POOLED** | **1834** | **48.3%** | **−11.6 ± 8.9** |

**Across a 12.5× range of node budget the gap does not move.** Every point sits
inside a ±17 bar that overlaps every other; the spread is entirely consistent
with sampling noise around a single constant near **−12 Elo**, which the pooled
1834-game estimate gives at **±8.9**.

**What this rules out:** the deficit is NOT something the search amplifies at
depth, and NOT something a shallow search exposes. **It is a flat
evaluator-quality difference.** So the fix must come from the net — training
budget, schedule, or data — and nothing on the search side will touch it.

**It also retires two earlier readings as noise:** sb620 measured −9.0 ± 12.1 at
5k against −4.2 ± 8.6 at 25k, which looked depth-dependent and is not.

**Method note:** pooling is legitimate here *because* the flatness test passed
first — had the points trended, pooling would have averaged away the finding.
Per-point and pooled together, never a lone aggregate.

### Where this leaves the weights gap

Net-swap put Stockfish's weights in our search at **+90.7 ± 16.9**. D49 sb640 is
**−11.6 ± 8.9** against our own canonical, and that gap is depth-flat. Both point
the same way: **the remaining deficit lives in the weights**, and the live
hypothesis for its mechanism is **presentation budget** — we train 10 G positions
against Stockfish's 40 G. The queued sb700→1700 cyclical continuation takes D49 to
**42.3 G = 106% of their budget**, which is the first direct test.

**Owner stepping away.** Everything below runs without intervention.

### D49, queued in order

| unit | what | trigger |
|---|---|---|
| `d49x` | current phase, sb600–700 anneal | running, ~sb650 |
| `d49y` | **cyclical continuation sb700 → 1700** — 35 cycles × 20sb, 1e-4 → 5e-6, peaks decaying **0.985**/cycle to 5.98e-5; sb1400 jump to 5e-5, anneal to zero over 300sb | waits on `d49x` + 5500 MiB GPU |
| `d49v700` | **5880-game sb700 verdict** vs canonical, `CONC=6` so it runs beside the trainer | waits on `d49x`, +120s |
| `d49yshim` | TensorBoard on `cyc.log` — **armed BEFORE launch this time** | live |
| **`d49wd`** | **WATCHDOG — restarts `d49y` if it dies, exits at sb1700** | live |

**The watchdog is new and it closes a real hole:** the directive says *restart if
it stops*, and until now nothing did. It polls every 100 s for ~55 h, relaunches
`d49y` if the unit is inactive before sb1699, and stands down on completion.

**On completion D49 reaches 1700 sb = 42.3 G positions = 4.16 dataset passes =
106% of Stockfish's 40 G budget** — the first time this project crosses it, and
the direct test of the presentation-budget hypothesis for the +90.7 weights gap.

### Study 50 commissioned — `forward_into`'s four allocations

Study 49's IBS work corrected study 48's ranking: **`forward_into` is the
third-largest TRUE stall source at 17.5%**, not the cheapest loads in the engine,
and **40.5% of its loads miss L1** because **`activate_inner` streams 12.8 KB per
eval from FOUR SEPARATE ALLOCATIONS** — four stride streams, four TLB sets, no
adjacency guarantee, ~5.5 KB/node of streaming nobody has tried to make
contiguous.

Told to re-derive the 12.8 KB rather than inherit it, to consolidate into one
aligned sequential allocation, and to measure `L1-dcache-load-misses` on the
symbol directly since that is the thing being changed. Given the full current
instrument state, including that **`load_not_complete` is not adjudicating**
(byte-identical null +3.478%) and that **study 42 retracted an IBS claim** when
the L1-hit statistic proved blind to prefetch.

**Anti-escape-hatch receipts attached:** a lever study 44 declared dead was the
largest on the board, and **study 48 landed in `activate` immediately after study
47 priced it at its floor** — because study 47 assumed `vpmullw`'s memory operand
had to be the scale, and it is commutative. Also told plainly that studies 47 and
49 both produced excellent analysis and no speedup, and two in a row is enough.

| 25,000 | 500 | 49.1% | −6.3 ± 17.1 |
| 50,000 | 500 | 49.5% | −3.5 ± 17.1 |
| **POOLED** | **2500** | **48.8%** | **−8.2 ± 7.7** |

**The spread across five points is 13.9 Elo against a single-point bar of ±17.1**
— i.e. the points are *closer together than one point's own noise*. There is no
depth trend to find. **The gap is a constant ≈ −8 Elo across a 25× range of node
budget.**

**Both endpoints reading −3.5** (2k and 50k) while the middle dips is the
signature of noise around a flat line, not a curve.

**What it settles:** the deficit is **not** a search interaction in either
direction — depth neither amplifies nor masks it. It is **flat evaluator
quality**, so no search-side work will touch it and the fix must come from the
net. It also retires the apparent depth-dependence in the earlier sb620 readings
(−9.0 ± 12.1 @5k vs −4.2 ± 8.6 @25k) as noise.

**And the number itself is better than the headline figure has been.** At sb640
D49 sits **−8.2 ± 7.7** against canonical over 2500 games — against **−21.24 ±
5.67** at sb400. The extension continues to pay.

**Method note kept:** pooling was legitimate only *because* the flatness test
passed first. Per-point and pooled together, never a lone aggregate.

**D49x: sb655, EMA 0.010623** — flattening as the sb600–700 anneal exhausts. The
whole chain (`d49y` continuation, `d49wd` watchdog, `d49v700` verdict,
`d49yshim`) is queued and verified waiting.

## ⚠ CAUGHT BEFORE IT FIRED — the sb700 handoff would have failed on contact

**D49x: sb662.** Stress-testing the handoff before it triggers found a bug I
introduced.

`jax/tools/pump_train.py` takes the resume point as its **second positional
argument**:

```
python tools/pump_train.py <config.json> [resume_sb]
```

`start_sb = resume_sb + 1`, and it loads `{net_id}-sb{resume_sb}-raw.bin` **and**
`-ema-raw.bin`. The `400` in d49x's working invocation was therefore
**`resume_sb=400`**, with `superbatches=700` in the config supplying the total.

**I wrote d49y's launcher passing `1700`** — the total — which would have tried to
resume from `d49-1280-sb1700-raw.bin`, **a file that does not exist**. The
continuation would have died immediately at the handoff, and with the owner away
the watchdog would have restarted it into the same failure every 100 seconds for
55 hours.

**Fixed: the launcher now passes `700`.** Verified arithmetic —
`remaining = 3040 × (1700 − 700) = 3,040,000 batches = 1000 superbatches`,
`skip_batches = 3040 × 700` so the loader skips data already seen, and it loads
the sb700 raw **and** EMA state. `d49y` relaunched with the corrected line and is
waiting correctly.

**Why this was worth the check rather than trusting the queue:** the failure
would have been silent from the outside — the unit would flap, `cyc.log` would
fill with load errors, and every status check that only asks `is-active` would
have said "active". **A queue that is verified only by its own status flag is not
verified.** The mistake came from copying d49x's invocation shape while
mis-reading which number meant what — the same units-at-the-boundary error the
project has now paid for three times today (SF "epochs", study 36's denominator,
this).

## Watchdog v2 — progress-gated, because v1 would have amplified the bug I just caught

**D49x: sb669**, ~31 superbatches to the handoff.

**v1 had a flaw the sb700 resume bug made concrete.** It restarted `d49y`
whenever the unit went inactive, with no check that the run had actually
advanced. Had the `resume_sb=1700` bug not been caught, the sequence would have
been: continuation dies instantly → watchdog restarts it → dies instantly →
**every 100 seconds for 55 hours**, filling the log and burning the box while
every `is-active` check reported healthy.

**v2 is progress-gated.** A restart is permitted only if the superbatch counter
has advanced since the previous one. **Three restarts with no progress and it
HALTS**, logging that the fault is a resume/checkpoint/config problem rather than
something transient, and explicitly declining to restart again.

That is the right failure mode for unattended running: **a watchdog that cannot
tell "crashed" from "crashing" is an amplifier, not a safety net.**

**Audit of the other queued unit** (`d49v700`, the 5880-game verdict): it waits on
`d49x`, polls up to 30 min for `sb700-ema-raw.bin` before touching it, requants
and packs at 756, and screens with `ENGINE=target-sota` — CEN5-capable, so
`screen.sh`'s new load guard passes rather than fires. No positional-argument
hazard: `requant_threats.py` and `netpack.py` take explicit flags, and
`screen.sh` takes `CAND REF GAMES NODES` in that order. Clean.

## Study 51 commissioned — stop the fold evicting the head. Gate GREEN on study 50.

**Gate on merged main: `Bench: 587683`, all checks passed.** Study 50 verified.
**D49x: sb676, EMA 0.010599** — still falling in the anneal, ~24 sb to the handoff.

Study 50's closing line is the commission: **"Bytes are the lever, not layout."**
Its per-region IBS census found the mechanism:

- **The evictor is the fold — 21.3 KB/eval of COLD weight rows through a 32 KB L1.**
- **58.3% of `forward_into`'s misses are the head's five weight tables:**
  `l1_weights` **89.0%**, `l2_weights` **98.95%**, `skip_q` **96.9%**,
  `l3_weights` **100%**, `l1_biases` **100%**.
- **A 2048-byte array cannot miss 98.9% of the time except by being evicted
  between every use.**
- Confirmed in the winning direction: removing 20 lines/eval of `ft_scale`
  traffic improved `hka_stack` 52.4%→43.8% and `thr_stack` 24.7%→21.7%
  **without touching either array.**

**The question: how do you stop single-use streaming data from evicting a small
hot working set?** Four mechanisms given, with the effort explicitly weighted:

1. **NT loads — ⚠ VERIFY THE HARDWARE FIRST.** `VMOVNTDQA` only bypasses cache on
   **WC** memory; on **WB** it behaves as a normal load on most x86. **Told not to
   build an arm whose premise it has not tested**, and that "NT is blocked on WB,
   therefore closed" obliges it to move to 2/3/4 rather than stop.
2. `PREFETCHNTA` — a different hint from the seven prefetches already killed, but
   the prior stands and the burden is theirs.
3. Reordering to shrink the head tables' reuse distance.
4. **Shrinking the streamed bytes** — *the one with a proven track record here*,
   since study 50's win was exactly this, found by **dumping a value histogram**
   and discovering 15 of 16 bits were padding. That technique now ships.

**Ceiling stated up front:** `forward_into` converts only 2.0% of its miss latency
to stall, so that symbol alone is ≤~2% of frame — **but the fold is the top TWO
symbols in the engine (13.77% + 12.38%)**, and the prize is its own miss cost plus
the collateral damage, not `forward_into`'s share.

**Prior art that bounds it:** study 43's non-temporal STORES lost 30/30 at +5.82%
cycles; prefetch is 0-for-7. **Anti-escape-hatch receipts attached:** a lever
study 44 declared dead was the largest on the board; study 48 landed in `activate`
right after study 47 priced it at its floor; **study 50 landed in a path study 47
had already closed.**

## The perf line has a MEASUREMENT problem: four landed changes, two unpriced

**D49x: sb696**, four superbatches to the handoff. All units waiting correctly.

**Landed since the canonical binary `archon-s25`:**

| study | what | cycles claim |
|---|---|---|
| 40 | outlined accumulator copy | **−0.156%** |
| 44 | fold tile 20 → 5 `zmm` | **−1.37%** |
| 48 | `activate` reused operand into a register | **none — declined** |
| 50 | `ft_scale` i16 → u8 | **none — declined** |

**Studies 48 and 50 both landed real, exactly-measured reductions and both
correctly refused to claim cycles**, because this box's `cycles:u` cannot resolve
what they moved — study 45's two *inert* arms disagreed by **1.164 pp**, and study
50's own panel null was −1.026% against an arm of −1.215%. That refusal is the
right call and I am not asking them to reach.

**But it leaves the cumulative figure unmeasurable one change at a time.** Our
running total (**−1.53% cycles ⇒ +2.66 Elo**) counts only the two changes big
enough to price, so it *understates* what has landed by an unknown amount.

**The fix is an AGGREGATE panel, not more per-study panels:** one binary with all
four changes against one without, on a quiet box, median of ≥6 blocked-Latin
rounds. Four sub-resolution changes summed may clear the ~1% the instrument can
see, where none of them can alone. **That is also the only way the games-threshold
rule (≈5.61% cycles before a 1466-game screen can resolve it) can ever be
applied** — it needs a cumulative number, and right now we do not have one.

**Blocked on a quiet box**, which this is not about to be: the handoff fires in
minutes, then the 5880-game verdict runs beside the continuation. Queued for the
next genuinely idle window.

## ★ HANDOFF FIRED CLEANLY — D49 is on the cyclical schedule

**`[pump d49-1280] RESUME from sb700 (fresh Adam transient)`** — the corrected
`resume_sb` worked, all four sb700 checkpoints written, continuation running at
**sb710, 195k pos/s**. This is the transition that would have failed silently and
been restarted into the same failure every 100 s for 55 h had the argument bug not
been caught.

**D49 is now in cycle 1 of 35, heading for sb1700 = 42.3 G positions = 106% of
Stockfish's budget.**

### First cycle: the debt is visible, as predicted

| point | heldout value | EMA |
|---|---:|---:|
| sb700 (end of anneal) | **0.010570** | 0.010592 |
| sb710 (mid cycle 1, LR ≈ 5e-5) | 0.010846 | 0.010725 |

**The restart cost +276e-6 on the raw value** — the same debt-before-dividend
shape as the sb400 warm restart, which cost 476e-6 and took ~130 sb to repay.

**But sb710 is mid-cycle at high LR, which is the wrong comparison.**
Trough-to-trough is the right one, and **sb720 — taken just after cycle 1's
trough at sb719 — is the decisive early reading**:

- recovers toward ~0.010600 ⇒ the 20-sb period is long enough, cycling can pay
- stays at 0.010725+ ⇒ **the period is too short**, every cycle pays the debt and
  never collects, which is the risk flagged when the schedule was designed

**Nothing changes on one mid-cycle sample.** The experiment is running as
specified.

**2377 / 5880 games**, running at `CONC=6` beside the trainer. Trajectory so far,

## ★★★ sb700 VERDICT FINAL — the extension is worth +17.1 Elo

**D49 sb700-EMA @756 vs canonical D48X @600, 5880 games at 25k fixed nodes:**

```
Elo: -4.14 +/- 5.56   nElo: -6.61 +/- 8.88
Games: 5880  W 1733  L 1803  D 2344   49.40%
Ptnml(0-2): [112, 741, 1301, 677, 109]   WL/DD 1.81
```

| checkpoint | Elo | n | interval |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| **sb400** — where the run would have STOPPED | **−21.24 ± 5.67** | 5880 | [−26.9, −15.6] |
| **sb700** | **−4.14 ± 5.56** | 5880 | [−9.7, +1.4] |

**+17.1 Elo, both ends at full 5880-game precision, intervals DISJOINT.**

### What this establishes

**The stopping criterion was wrong.** At sb400 D49 looked finished — loss had
flattened and the anneal had run. Training it **75% longer bought 17.1 Elo that
the loss curve never showed.** Every net this project has shelved on a flat loss
curve is now suspect.

**And sb700 is still only 44% of Stockfish's presentation budget** (17.4 G
positions against 40 G). The running continuation takes D49 to **42.3 G = 106%**,
which is the first time the question gets asked at parity.

**Trajectory, all vs canonical @25k:** sb400 −21.24 ± 5.67 (5880) → sb580 −25.90 ±
10.00 (1466) → sb620 −4.20 ± 8.60 (2000) → sb640 −8.20 ± 7.70 (2500, pooled over
a 25× node range) → **sb700 −4.14 ± 5.56 (5880)**.

### Cycle experiment: NOT readable yet, and the instrument has a blind spot

**Heldout is sampled every 10 sb; the cycle period is 20.** Troughs fall at sb719,
739, 759 — **always 9 mod 20, never on a multiple of 10** — so every reading lands
at a peak or mid-decay and **the trough state is systematically invisible.** The
metric grid is aliased against the schedule period. Fixing it costs a trainer
restart and another fresh-Adam transient, which is worth more than the reading.

What is readable is the EMA, which smooths across cycles and is the deployed
artifact: **0.010694 after one full cycle against sb700's 0.010592.** One cycle
cannot beat a completed anneal; the experiment is only readable at **sb1400** (end
of cycling) and **sb1700** (after the final anneal).

## ★ AGGREGATE CYCLES PANEL — the whole batch is −1.475%, and the NULL is the story

The measurement flagged as owed: **`06e05970` (last commit before study 44) vs
current `main` (studies 44+48+50+51)**, both built from source with identical
flags, plus **a byte-identical copy of the base as a null in the rotation**,
blocked-Latin ordering, 10 rounds, pinned to one core, trainer live throughout.

| arm | median | range | sign |
|---|---:|---|---|
| **NULL** (byte-identical) | **+0.517%** | [−2.71, +1.78] | 3/10 negative |
| **HEAD** (44+48+50+51) | **−1.475%** | [−3.68, −0.54] | **10/10 negative** |

**10/10 against a null that went 3/10 — sign test p ≈ 0.001.**

**But the null's median is +0.517%, not zero.** Even under blocked-Latin ordering
this harness carries a systematic ~0.5% bias, so the null-corrected cumulative is
about **−2.0% cycles ⇒ +2.6 to +3.5 Elo**.

**Instructions went +2.90%** — head does MORE work in FEWER cycles, study 44's
tile pattern surviving to the aggregate, and further proof `instructions:u` would
have rejected this entire batch.

### What it says about the unpriced studies

Studies 40+44 alone predicted **−1.53%**; the measured aggregate is **−1.475%**.
So **studies 48, 50 and 51's landed changes contribute ≲0.5% and cannot be
separated from noise even in aggregate.** They were real on exact counters —
study 48's −0.286% instructions at 15/15 against a 0.000% null, study 50's
−0.563% L1d with instructions at exactly 0.000% — and they remain **unpriced in
cycles**. That is the honest position, not a claim that they did nothing.

**Against the games threshold (~5.61% cycles before a 1466-game screen can
resolve it), ~2% is still well short.** The cycles gate remains the only
instrument for this work, and it is at the edge of its own resolution.

### The fold's byte volume is bounded by TRAINING, not by code

Study 51's forward pointer, now the perf line's binding constraint: weight-row
bytes are exactly **rows/node (7.1754) × width (1280 B) = 9,184 B/node**, that
streaming is **25.3% of all L1 fills, 55.05% of engine miss latency and ≥13.19%
of cycles**, and it scales **linearly in width**. Both factors are training
decisions. Study 51 also killed the one code-side lever (nibble-packing) with the
**fold-weighted** entropy measurement — static entropy said 3.55 bits in 8, the
touched population said 97.3% of folded HalfKA rows need all 8.

**So the largest remaining perf lever cannot be pulled from the code side.** It
reopens the width trade — the standing finding is that width is ~Elo-neutral
across 768–1536 at equal time, and this says the speed half of that trade was
under-credited.

## Two unattended-run hazards closed, one of them expensive

**D49x: sb780**, cycling. Disk 92% → **83%**, 158 GB free.

### 1. The watchdog would have thrown away 11 hours of GPU

`d49y_launch.sh` passed a **hardcoded `700`** as `resume_sb`. The watchdog restarts
that launcher on a crash — so **a failure at sb1000 would have resumed from sb700
and re-run 300 superbatches.** The launcher now **discovers** the resume point by
scanning for the highest superbatch with **both** a `-raw.bin` and an
`-ema-raw.bin` (the pair `pump_train` actually loads). Verified: it would now
resume from **sb770, not 700**.

**Same class as the original `resume_sb` bug — a literal where a lookup belonged —
and it only mattered on the failure path, which is where nobody looks.** The
watchdog was written to make a crash survivable and would instead have made it
expensive.

### 2. Checkpoints were on track to fill the disk

D49 wrote **154 raw checkpoints at 433 MB** = **83 GB**, one pair every 10 sb, with
**930 superbatches still to run** (~79 GB more against 91 GB free). It fit only
just, with **no margin for the screens, study builds and PGNs that share the
disk.**

Pruned to **12 GB** — kept **sb400 and sb700** (the two measured baselines), every
**100 sb** for later analysis, and the **newest four pairs** for resume safety.
44 files kept, 264 deleted, **72 GB freed.**

### A peer session's ranked list for `search::<NonPv>` — routed, not landed

Another session produced seven ranked items and it reached me. **Analysis only —
explicitly unbuilt and unmeasured.** I verified its load-bearing premises against
`main`:

| claim | result |
|---|---|
| `RFP_THREAT_MARGIN = 55 ≥ 0` ⇒ `relaxed ≤ base` (the biggest item's node-identity) | ✅ `search/params.rs:349` |
| magic table is a runtime-length `Box<[Bitboard]>` | ✅ `attacks.rs:535` |
| `CONT_NB = 3328`, not a power of two | ✅ `history.rs:128` |
| "additive on top of your `.max(1)` guard removal" | ❌ **`main` has no such edit** |

It is addressed to a peer ("your allocator-fragility finding", "your `.max(1)`
edit"), so **its line numbers are for a tree I do not have** and one item's
dependency is unmet. **Forwarded to study 52**, which owns the area and can
measure it, with my verification attached and the same bar as everything else:
node identity plus eval hash, its own byte-identical null published, and arms
clearing ~1.5% on cycles — since tonight's aggregate panel had a byte-identical
null at **+0.517%, range [−2.71, +1.78]**.

**Not landed.** Seven unmeasured proposals is exactly the input that needs a
measurement gate: study 47 priced `activate` at its floor and study 48 landed
there anyway; study 44 declared port pressure dead and it was the largest lever on
the board.

## Study 53 GATED; cycling debt measured STABLE; the run is now fully unattended

**Gate on merged main: `Bench: 587683`, all checks passed.** Study 53 verified —

### Study 53 corrected two premises that came from ME

**(a) "Study 39 reported that fusion is dead" — WRONG, and I verified the
correction.** Study 39 killed **accumulator-channel** fusion. Study 35 §4 line 13
says of `do_move` fusion: *"that arm measures −11.36 i/n — a win, not a loss"*,
left unlanded only because a better arm dominated it at the time. **A measured win
sat unlanded for a day because of a sentence I got wrong**, and it is now study
54's lead item.

**(b) The "461% stall" I passed on from study 49's caller table is a counter
artifact** — `do_move`'s own miss latency is 16.7 c/node, so there was never 461%
of recoverable stall to aim at. Study 53 attacked op count instead, which is where
the win came from.

Four things it surfaced rather than buried: its **inert control was not inert**
(−6.9 i/n at 18/18, failing to compile byte-identical, cause unpinned);
**`magic53` alone beats the combination on loads** (−22.3 at 10/10 vs −10.6 at
7/10 stacked) so it flagged a counter disagreement it could not settle;
**`cont53` works at −13.4 i/n and it declined to land it** because +1.125 MiB per
worker thread breaches design/09 §8 — **a design-doc decision, correctly escalated
rather than taken**; and **`ls_stlf` fails its byte-identical null** (+0.540%,
p=0.0215), closing store-forwarding for a measured reason.

### D49 cycling: the debt is STABLE, not compounding

| | Elo | n |
|---|---:|---:|
| sb700 (anneal end) | **−4.14 ± 5.6** | 5880 |
| sb750 | −17.7 ± 12.1 | 1000 |
| sb800 | −12.5 ± 12.1 | 1000 |

**The two points differ by 5.2 Elo against ±12.1 bars — one constant, not a

was the decision-relevant question and it means **no schedule change**: a
compounding cost would have argued for intervening with 900 sb left; a fixed cost
is what the design assumed.

**Recovery precedent has margin:** D49's own warm restart sat at −25.9 and a

The reaper's newest-4 rule is load-bearing: **the launcher discovers its resume

## ★ THE CYCLES NOISE FLOOR IS σ = 1.57% — measured, and it retires a standing rule

**D49x: sb871.** All five services healthy; checkpoint dir held at 14 GB by the
reaper; 156 GB disk.

Every cycles panel tonight had a bad null — **+0.517%** (aggregate), **−1.847%**
(PGO), **−1.026%** (study 50), **−1.488%…+0.024%** (study 53), **+0.553% at
p=0.041** (study 54). Four studies declined cycles claims because of it. I went
looking for the mechanism.

### My frequency hypothesis was REFUTED, and the n=1 test that "confirmed" it was noise

The governor is `powersave` + boost + `amd_pstate active`, clocks spanning
**2989–5575 MHz**. Since `cycles:u` counts core cycles and memory latency is fixed
in wall time, a memory-bound workload (this engine is **83% memory-bound**) should
show cycles/node rising with clock. An n=1 paired test appeared to confirm it:
+2.16% clock, +1.75% cycles.

**At n=12 it collapses:**

| | |
|---|---|
| clock range | 4092–5310 MHz (**+29.8% spread**) |
| cycles/node range | 3740.3–3914.4 (**+4.7% spread**) |
| **Pearson r** | **−0.144** |

**A 30% frequency swing produces no correlation.** The mechanism is not frequency,
and my n=1 "confirmation" was a 1.75% draw from a 1.57% distribution — I went
looking for confirmation and found it.

### The real answer is simpler and more useful

**A single run's cycles/node carries σ = 1.57% on this box, intrinsically.**

That one number explains **every** bad null of the night without any mechanism —
all of them sit within ~1σ of zero. And it retroactively justifies what the
studies did on instinct.

**It also retires the standing gate rule.** "GATE ARMS ON CYCLES (median ≥6
rounds)" is under-powered: to resolve a 1% effect you need σ/√n < 0.33%, i.e.
**n ≈ 23 paired rounds, not 6.** At n=6 the residual noise is 0.64% — larger than
most arms landed tonight.

**⇒ `instructions:u`, with a measured null of 0.000, carried every landed decision
tonight, and that was correct rather than conservative.** Studies 50, 52, 53 and
54 all refused cycles claims for effects of 0.3–1.5%; at 3σ ≈ 4.7% single-run
resolution, none of those effects was ever visible.

## Tick — 2026-07-31, study 55 merged (−83.8 i/n, largest of the series)

**Gate green on the merge:** `All checks passed.`, `Bench: 587683`, exit 0. Three
pins unmoved (eval hash `2e1ede95…79c4`, d13 `1739588`), `perft_full_suite`
PASSED, all 20 arm binaries node-identical at 5,001,562.

**Series to date on `instructions:u`:** 52 −36.5, 53 −39.3, 54 −46.7, **55 −83.8**.
Each study has found more than the one before it.

### The three findings that change how the next study is built

1. **Mechanism A (single-call-site inlining) INVERTS on large bodies.** Winners
   were nine small helpers (−19.3, −12.6 i/n). Losers, priced rather than
   guessed: `do_move` **+25.7**, medium batch **+25.0**, `see_ge` **+4.5**.
   Three arms each cost more than study 54's entire gain — **reading study 54 as
   "inline more things" would have shipped a regression.**
2. **Mechanism B is bigger than the study that found it.** `bcbuf55` — masking
   MovePicker's buffer funnel — is **−84.6 i/n alone**, from three characters on
   three lines, and makes the binary **3,576 bytes smaller**. Highest
   yield-per-character in the series.
3. **Arms are violently non-additive in BOTH directions.** `bcall55` misses the
   sum of its parts by **+2.63 pp (159 i/n)**; `landa55` misses by **−44 i/n
   favourably**. Study 54's "instructions add to the third decimal place" does
   **not** generalise. **Build the combination you intend to ship; never sum arms.**

### Three corrections study 55 made to my own commissioning framing

- **"One call site ⇒ LLVM inlines it" is FALSE.** `make_move_with::<TtPrefetch>`
  has exactly one call site and is still out of line at 2466 bytes. The condition
  is a single **hot** site.
- **`search::<Root>` has one call site and no symbol in the binary** — LLVM had
  already folded its ~16 KB body into `iterative_deepening` (29,459 B vs
  `search::<NonPv>`'s 16,264). **Study 54's mechanism was already running at the
  largest scale in the engine, unnoticed.**
- Negatives in full: `bccont55` **+5.8 i/n** — the arm that looked most like
  study 54's on paper, and lost.

### Left unlanded, and it is the next study's target

`bcmisc55` (**−44.7**) and `bcsort55` (**−34.6**) are large wins alone, blocked
only by interaction and retained behind feature flags. **~79 i/n of measured work
is sitting on the table.**

**Correctness item, with study 55's framing corrected.** `Worker::reduction()`
indexes `self.reductions[move_number as usize]` where `reductions: [i32; 256]`,
no clamp. Study 55 called this a "latent panic in a 256-move position". **I
checked it: the maximum legal move count in any chess position is 218, so it is a
missing bound, not a reachable panic.** `bcmisc55` closes it — but `bcmisc55` was
not landed, so **the bound is still absent.**

**Instrument discipline held:** inert control `ctl55` byte-identical to base
(`.text` md5 `1ce4526f`) *and* measured 0.000%. **No cycles claim anywhere** —
correct, given σ = 1.57%.

### The D49 trajectory vs canonical @25k

| point | Elo | games |
|---|---|---|
| sb400 | −21.24 ± 5.67 | 5880 |
| sb620 | −4.2 ± 8.6 | 2000 |
| sb640 | −8.2 ± 7.7 | 2500 |
| **sb700** (end of extend) | **−4.14 ± 5.56** | 5880 |
| mid-cycling prior pooled | −15.1 ± 8.6 | — |
| **sb900** (mid-cycle) | **−11.59 ± 11.51** | 1500 |
| **mid-cycling pooled** | **−13.84 ± 6.89** | — |

### CORRECTION — "stable ~11 Elo debt" was over-stated

I have written across several ticks that mid-cycling carries a **"stable ~11 Elo
debt, not compounding."** The second half survives; **the first half does not,
and I should not have asserted it.** Testing it properly:

- **Mid-cycling vs canonical:** −13.84 ± 6.89 → **2.0σ**. Behind, marginally.
- **Mid-cycling vs sb700:** difference **−9.70 ± 8.85 = 1.10σ**. **NOT
  significant.** There is no established evidence that cycling has cost anything
  relative to where the extend run ended.

So the correct statement is: *mid-cycling measures modestly behind canonical, and
is statistically indistinguishable from sb700.* Calling it a "debt" implied a
measured loss caused by the cycling; that loss is not in the data. This is the
[[quote-levels-not-trends]] failure — reading a direction off overlapping bars.

**What it does establish:** nothing is diverging. The abort condition (a large,
growing deficit) is not firing, which is the only thing a mid-cycle datapoint is
entitled to say. **The experiment remains readable only at sb1400 and sb1700** —
by construction, since intermediate nets sit at high-LR points of the sawtooth.

**Both causes are our own landed work.** Study 32 quantised the 57.7 MB i16

### The current top block

**The i8 delta family is 24.10% — the largest single block in the engine, larger
than search, and untouched by studies 52–56 (all search-side):**
`i8_deltas_into_inner` **10.36%** + `i8_deltas_into_generic` **8.74%** +

**The lead:** both an `_inner` and a `_generic` variant of the same operation are
hot, 19.1% combined. A generic fallback burning 8.74% next to a specialised inner
suggests **the specialised path is not covering the cases it should** — but that
is a question to measure (call-site split, dispatch shape, monomorphisation
boundary), not a diagnosis.

**Separately: ~5.12% is STARTUP, not search** — `load_v2_bytes` 2.70% +
`quantise_halfka` 2.42%, the i8 quantisation of the weight table done at load.
Amortised over a 5M-node bench, but **match play spawns short-lived processes by
the thousand**, so it is real cost there and an inflated denominator here.

**Study 57 commissioned** on both, with the instrument problem stated explicitly
in the brief: **a cache/layout win in a SIMD kernel may be invisible to
`instructions:u`**, the only counter with a 0.000 null. So instructions is the
*guard*, cycles needs n≥23 (σ = 1.57%), and any cache counter must ship its own
published null — `ls_stlf` fails its null outright and
`ex_no_retire.load_not_complete` reads +3.478% on byte-identical binaries.

## Tick — 2026-07-31, THE PERF PROGRAMME'S ELO IS UNMEASURED, AND OUR INSTRUMENT CANNOT MEASURE IT

**Measured base:** 30,794,610,363 instructions over 5,001,562 nodes =
**6157.0 instructions/node**. The session's landed ≈ **−240 i/n is 3.90% of
base.**

**But that does not convert to Elo, and I should not have implied it might.**
If instructions mapped 1:1 to time, 3.90% would be 1.0406× → **6.9 Elo** at the
project's ~120 Elo/doubling. **The directive's own PERF LOOP note contains the
counterexample:** study 20 was **−5.99% cycles with instructions UP 0.19%.**
Instructions and time demonstrably dissociate in this codebase, so 6.9 Elo is an
arithmetic exercise, not an estimate. Recorded as unmeasured.

### The structural gap

**No instrument we are currently running can price the perf programme:**

- **Screens are FIXED-NODE (25k) — speed-blind by construction.** A faster engine
  searches the same 25,000 nodes and plays the identical move. This is a feature
  for net comparisons (it removes speed as a confound) and it means the perf work
  is *invisible* to every Elo number in this document.
- **`cycles:u` has σ = 1.57%** — a 3.90% effect needs n≥23 paired rounds to
  resolve cleanly.
- **Match NPS has ~24% noise** — the directive already calls it unusable.

So the perf series (52–56, ≈−240 i/n) has **zero measured strength value**. Not
"small" — *unmeasured*. This is not a defect in the studies, which were gated
correctly on the one counter with a 0.000 null; it is a gap in what we have ever
asked games to arbitrate.

**Also confirmed:** `target-sota/release/chessengine`, the screen binary, was
built **2026-07-30 11:47** — *predating* the study-52 merge (07-31 05:56) and
study-55 merge (07-31 09:04). **This does not invalidate any D49 result**: both
CAND and REF run the same binary under `ENGINE=$BIN`, and the matches are
fixed-node. The net-quality measurements stand exactly as recorded.

### Queued for the first quiet box

1. **A fixed-TIME match, current main vs the pre-52 build, both PGO** — the only
   thing that can price the perf series. Per rule 5 (GAMES ARBITRATE) this is
   overdue: the series has been landing for two days with no game ever run on it.
2. **An n≥23 cycles panel on the aggregate** — the cheaper, weaker fallback.

Both need the box; two studies plus the trainer are on it now. **Not started, and
deliberately so** — starting a time-sensitive match on a contended box is how the
`nodestime` error happened (it gave TM +52.6 where the real clock gave −31.4).

**D49:** sb926, heldout sb920 ema **0.010666** (sawtooth, no trend), 193k pos/s,
four services active, 8.1 GB free.
**Studies 56 and 57 both alive**, one `rustc` between them — the shared
`flock` is serialising builds as designed.

## Tick — 2026-07-31, THE PERF CHAIN IS REAL: −193.1 i/n measured end-to-end, 93.6% of claims

Last tick I recorded that the perf programme's value was unmeasured. This tick
measured the instruction half of it properly, **and caught myself running a
confounded comparison first.**

### The controlled result

Built the pre-52 parent `f289433c` from source with **flags identical** to a
fresh `main` build (`sota,arch-d48x`, `-C target-cpu=x86-64-v3`, same box, same
pinned 5M-node frame):

| | i/n |
|---|---|
| pre-52 (`f289433c`) | 6321.2 |
| main | 6128.0 |
| **delta** | **−193.1 (−3.056%)** |
| claimed by studies 52–55 (36.5+39.3+46.7+83.8) | −206.3 |
| **realised** | **93.6%** |

**The chain composes.** Study 55 proved arms are violently non-additive *within*
a study; across merged studies the loss is only 6.4%. Those are different
questions and it is worth not confusing them: non-additivity bites between arms
sharing code paths, not between independently-gated merges.

### The confounded comparison I ran first, and why it was wrong

I first compared `main` against the existing `target-sota` binary and got
**−0.473%** — which would have meant the whole series was worth almost nothing. I
did not report it as a finding, because its provenance was a *guess*: I inferred
that binary's commit from its **mtime** via `git log --until`, and I never knew
its `RUSTFLAGS`. Both are load-bearing for an instruction comparison. Rebuilding
the real parent with matched flags moved the answer from −0.473% to −3.056%.

**The lesson is narrow and reusable: a binary on disk is not a known commit.**
Provenance by timestamp is not provenance. Build both sides of a perf comparison
yourself, or you are measuring your build flags.

### Wall-clock, designed null-first

An earlier paired timing panel on the same box **disqualified its own primary
statistic**, which is exactly what rule 1 is for:

| statistic | NULL (byte-identical) | ARM |
|---|---|---|
| paired **mean** | **+3.504% ± 3.372** (sd 9.54) | −2.858% ± 0.699 |
| **median** | **−0.132%** | −2.103% |

One contention round of **+27.07%** wrecked the mean; its null noise exceeds the
effect, so **the mean cannot answer under load**. The median null is −0.132% and
is licensed. A controlled median panel (main vs the rebuilt pre-52, null first)
is running now; its ratio will say whether −3.056% of instructions buys
proportional time. **Until it reports, no time claim is made** — and the
directive's own study-20 receipt (−5.99% cycles, instructions **up** 0.19%) is
the standing reason not to assume one.

**D49:** sb933, heldout ema 0.010666/0.010714 (sawtooth), 193k pos/s, four
services active. Studies 56 and 57 alive, builds serialised on the shared lock.

### Wall-clock panel result: FAILED ITS NULL. No time claim.

Controlled median panel, main vs the rebuilt pre-52, run null-first:

| | value |
|---|---|
| **NULL** (pre-52 vs byte-identical copy, n=8) | **+0.972%** |
| **ARM** (main vs pre-52, n=14) | **−0.809%** |

**The null is LARGER than the arm.** By rule 1 the instrument cannot answer this
question under the current load, and it says so rather than reporting −0.809% as
a result.

**This also supersedes the −2.103% I recorded earlier in this tick.** That
reading came from the *confounded* binary and used a null taken under lighter
load (−0.132%). With controlled binaries and a contemporaneous null, it does not
reproduce. **Retracted, not re-estimated.**

Note how far the wall-clock null itself moved with box load: **−0.132% → +0.972%**
within one tick. Wall-clock on a contended box is not a stable instrument at the
3% scale, and no amount of rounds fixes a null that drifts with a third party's
build.

### Where this leaves the perf programme

- **Instruction half: MEASURED and real.** −193.1 i/n, −3.056%, 93.6% of claims
  realised. `instructions:u` is load-robust (0.000 null), which is exactly why it
  survives on a busy box while wall-clock does not.
- **Time/Elo half: STILL UNMEASURED**, and now with a *known* blocker rather than
  a vague one — it needs a quiet box, not more rounds.

**Queued for the first quiet box, in order:** (1) the median wall-clock panel
re-run, null first; (2) a fixed-TIME PGO match, main vs pre-52. The pre-52 binary
is preserved at `$SCRATCH/pre52_bin` so neither needs a rebuild; its build tree
was deleted to return disk.

## Tick — 2026-07-31, study 56 merged (−65.8 i/n) and it RETRACTS my commission's premise

**Merged at `46588c4b`. Gate re-running from source on my side; not yet green as
of writing — no pass claimed until it prints.**

### I verified the number myself, before/after, same flags

| | i/n |
|---|---|
| main pre-56 | 6128.0 |
| main post-56 | 6058.1 |
| **delta** | **−69.9 (−1.141%)** |
| study 56 claimed | −65.8 (−1.107%) |

**Verified, and marginally better than claimed.**

### The premise I sent it after did not exist

I commissioned study 56 to recover "**~79 i/n sitting behind feature flags**"
(`bcmisc55` −44.7, `bcsort55` −34.6). **That work does not exist.** Study 55
measured those arms against `base55` — a substrate it then **destroyed by landing
on top of it**. Re-measured against the binary actually shipped:

| arm | study 55 | vs shipped |
|---|---:|---:|
| `bcmisc55` | −44.7 | **+7.7** |
| `bcsort55` | −34.6 | **+14.7** |
| `bccont55` | +5.8 | **+55.6** |

**The interaction did not BLOCK those wins, it CONSUMED them.** A retained arm's
number expires the moment the study lands anything.

### Bundles hide their members — the mechanism behind both studies

`attackers_to_exist` is **−19.2 i/n** on its own. Study 55 measured it inside the
`inld55` bundle at **+25.0** and rejected the whole group, because the bundle also
held `correction_value` at **+28.1**. Study 56 priced members individually via an
`nm -S` × `perf report` join and recovered the win. **Two of study 55's headline
rules fall with it:** its size rule for mechanism A is wrong (885 B wins at −6.9;
504 B is the worst loser at +28.1), and edge count predicts nothing.

### The missing bound: my correction held

No 256-move position exists — 218 is the maximum, `move_number` counts only
post-legality-filter moves, and depth is clamped at node entry. A path to ≥256 was
looked for and not found. Landed as free correctness. **But the spelling costs
15.2 i/n:** `clamp` is +11.3, the same bound as a mask is −3.9.

### A gate gap worth more than the study

`HISTORIES_BYTES` counted the *logical* slab count, so design/09 §8's heap guard
would have under-reported this study's padding by **288 KiB/worker**. Fixed. The
general point stands and is ours to own: **every gate in this repo is a BEHAVIOUR
gate; none weighs the heap.**

### Deviation RATIFIED — and the fault was mine

The study built **unlocked** (under a tighter 4G cgroup at `nice 19`, floor raised
2500→3500 MB) because **my `flock` was held by a 1500-game MATCH, not a build**.
That was my design error: `screen.sh`'s own header says "Fixed NODES, so the
result is contention-immune", so a match never needed the build lock and holding
it stalled a study for 15 minutes. **Ratified.** Standing fix: **the build lock is
for builds only — never wrap a match in it.**

### Still owed, and now most acute

**The PGO pair, overdue across studies 52–56.** This study lands six
`inline(always)` attributes — **exactly what PGO re-litigates**, since PGO makes
its own inlining decisions from profile data. The instruction win is measured on
non-PGO builds; whether it survives PGO is unmeasured.

### Study 56 gate: GREEN

`All checks passed.`, `Bench: 587683`, all three pins unmoved. Merged at
`46588c4b`, verified independently at **−69.9 i/n** against its claimed −65.8.

**Session total on `instructions:u`, now measured end-to-end rather than summed:**
pre-52 `f289433c` **6321.2** → main **6058.1** = **−263.1 i/n, −4.163%.**

### Quiet window used for the PGO pair (the overdue item)

Both studies idle and zero `rustc` running, so the box was finally quiet enough
to start the thing that has been owed since study 52. Building **both** PGO
binaries — current `main` and pre-52 `f289433c` — with `tools/build_pgo.sh
sota,arch-d48x <out> <net>`, sequentially, flocked, 6G-capped, memory floor 3500 MB.

**The eval-file argument is mandatory and is why this is done properly:** the
script's own header records that a bench-only profile leaves the NNUE kernels
unprofiled and **PGO inert** (measured 2026-07-18, `pgo == native` on the sota
path). Passing the canonical net makes the profile exercise the real inference
path.

**Why this pair specifically.** Study 56 lands **six `inline(always)` attributes**,
and PGO makes its own inlining decisions from profile data — so PGO is not a
formality here, it is the thing most likely to *re-litigate* the win. The
−263.1 i/n is measured on non-PGO builds; whether it survives PGO is unknown, and
the match binaries are PGO by standing rule.

## Tick — 2026-07-31, study 57 complete; merge DEFERRED on a semantic conflict

Study 57 landed two arms on `perf-study-57-i8deltas`, gate green, eval-bit-exact,
node-identical. **Not merged yet** — see the conflict below.

**Arm A — `RowOffset` (pre-scaled fold row offsets):** the runtime-length i8 fold
kernels recomputed `feature * ACC` once per tile, and at the shipped `REGS = 5` an
`ACC = 1280` accumulator is **eight tiles** — 2 of 6 scalar instructions per row,
paid 8×. **instructions:u −0.694% (24/24, se 0.000, p<0.0001) = −41.27/node.**
Carried as a **newtype, not a bare `usize`**, so nine producer/kernel sites cannot
silently disagree on the unit.

> **Provenance worth keeping:** perf study 37 §5 named this "the best-priced
> remaining candidate" and priced it at −8 i/n **when the fold was 2 tiles wide**.
> Study 44 then made the walk 4× wider and **nobody re-priced it.** The arm exists
> only because study 57 read the catalogue of prior work first.

**Reported regression, not buried:** `L1-dcache-loads:u` **+0.283%** (3/24,
p=0.0003), priced at ≈+2.4 cycles/node in the cheapest load class; the alternative
fix cost +14 i/n and was declined.

**Arm B — `quantise_halfka` without `idiv`:** 28,835,840 non-pipelined divisions
per engine start replaced by an exact round-up reciprocal (both domain bounds
structural, 128× margin, two new tests sweep **every** input pair against the real
division). Startup frame **cycles −22.496%** (39/39, p<0.0001), branches −31.4%,
loads −5.9%, **instructions +4.5%**. ≈**−269 M cycles per process.**

**That arm is the counterexample to our own gate.** `instructions:u` rises while
cycles fall 22% — the idiv trade. It is the one place the licensed counter would
have returned the wrong verdict, and it is exactly the shape the directive's
study-20 note warns about.

**Negative arm with its number:** deleting the `i % ACC` in the quantiser
row-outer loses on **all five counters, 0/40 rounds** (+10.7% instructions,
+19.0% branches, +36.7% loads, +5.3% cycles) — `Aligned::from_iter_len` ends in
`Vec::extend` and a `FlatMap` loses the bulk specialisation. Shipping the combined
version would have landed a **5.3% startup regression while believing a division
had been removed.**

### THE MERGE IS DEFERRED — a semantic conflict, resolved by its owner

Study 56 and study 57 both rewrote `resolve_into`'s buffers in
`sota_threats.rs`, from different directions, and **both changes are wanted**:

- **56:** `&mut [usize]` → `&mut [usize; DELTA_CAP]`, writing `adds[add_len & M]`
  — the mask is what deletes the `cmp`/`ja panic` per event.
- **57:** `&mut [usize]` → `&mut [RowOffset]`, writing `RowOffset::of(idx)`.

Correct resolution is **`&mut [RowOffset; DELTA_CAP]` with
`adds[add_len & M] = RowOffset::of(idx)`** — both wins, neither dropped.

**I did not hand-merge it.** The point of making `RowOffset` a newtype was that
nine producer/kernel sites cannot silently disagree on the unit; that invariant
belongs to the study that created it, not to my guess under time pressure. Sent
back with instructions to rebase, re-gate, and — **critically — re-measure against
post-56 main**, because study 56 itself proved a retained arm's number expires the
moment another study lands on its substrate (`bcmisc55`: −44.7 → +7.7).

### Study 57's corrections to my brief, all measured

- **My profile table came from a differently-inlined build.**
  `i8_deltas_dual_inner` does not exist in theirs; `finny_refresh_into` is 6.34%,
  not 1.56%. **The cause is mine: I profiled `./target-sota`, which I later
  established was a PRE-52 binary.** I re-profiled to avoid a stale *list* and
  then handed over a stale *build*. The shape holds — i8 family #1 at 21.95%.
- **"This block has not been touched at all" is FALSE.** Studies
  21/25/32/36/37/43/44/46/47/48/51 all worked it. Checking that catalogue is
  precisely why arm A exists.
- **The `_inner`/`_generic` split I flagged as a likely coverage failure is
  CORRECT.** Measured: `hka_shape` sums to `hka_into.calls` exactly ⇒ 100% of
  HalfKA folds are shaped; the generic path is the **threat channel**, 4.594
  rows/call, 54.7% of all folded rows. My headline lead was wrong.

### Study 57's own retractions

1. It "found" mid-study that `instructions:u` is **bimodal** (0.694% on
   byte-identical binaries). **Not real** — a `nohup`'d panel outlived its tool
   call and appended to the same file as its replacement, scrambling label
   attribution. Hazard inherited from `tools/perf55/exact55.sh`'s shape;
   `panel57.sh` now `flock`s its output.
2. Its own §6 re-pricing of the threat shape dispatch (−1.5%) was wrong; it is
   **0.63%**. Folds of ≤4 rows are 59.9% of **calls** but only 29.6% of **rows**.
   **Studies 25 §3, 36 §6 and study 57 have each over-priced this arm ~3× by
   spending a per-call statistic on a per-row cost.** Arm stays closed.

### FOR A HUMAN RULING — escalated, not actioned

**Startup is now measured for the first time**: 5.42% of frame instructions,
6.58% of frame cycles (two-budget difference) — the figure study 36 §4 listed as
unmeasured. **The largest remaining item is the FNV-1a payload checksum at ~542 M
cycles, ~45% of startup.** It is irreducible without changing the CENN container
or the shipped net. Reported for a decision rather than changed quietly.

|---|---|---|---|
| **non-PGO** | 6321.2 | 6058.1 | **−263.1 i/n (−4.163%)** |
| **PGO** | 6046.7 | 5884.2 | **−162.4 i/n (−2.686%)** |

**Only 61.7% of the series' hand-optimised win survives PGO.**

**The same fact from the other direction:** PGO is worth **−274.5 i/n** on pre-52
code but only **−173.9** on main. **Our manual work and PGO overlap by ~100 i/n** —
we have partly been doing PGO's job by hand, and PGO then finds less left to do.

**This was predicted in advance and is why it got checked.** Study 56 flagged that
it lands **six `inline(always)` attributes** and that "PGO re-litigates exactly
that". It does. Expect absorption to be largest for mechanism-A-style arms
(inlining, call-site specialisation) and smallest for arms PGO cannot express —
data layout, algorithmic removal, study 57's `idiv` elimination.

### The consequence for how we report

**Match and release binaries are PGO by standing rule**, so **every non-PGO study
number overstates shipped value by ~1.6×.** Study 55's headline "−83.8 i/n" is
about −52 i/n of shipped effect; study 56's −69.9 is about −43.

Not a reason to change the gate: `instructions:u` non-PGO remains the only counter
with a 0.000 null, and PGO builds are far too slow to gate every arm on. **The
rule is to add a PGO pair before quoting a shipped number** — two `build_pgo.sh`
runs and two `perf stat` calls, which is what this tick cost.

Every profile in this document until now was taken on a **non-PGO** binary — and

both axes. Profiled `archon-main-pgo`, the binary we actually ship:

| function | my non-PGO re-profile | **PGO (shipped)** | directive's list |
|---|---|---|---|
| `search::<NonPv>` | 11.19% | **23.53%** | 19.90% |
| `HalfkaContext::current_full` | 3.72% | **12.40%** | 3.28% |
| `i8_deltas_into_generic` | 8.74% | **10.21%** | — |
| `SotaHead::forward_into` | *not in top 18* | **9.30%** | **8.77%** |
| `init_quiets` | 6.35% | 7.64% | 6.35% |
| `at_full` | 5.61% | 7.19% | — |
| `finny_refresh_into` | 1.56% | **6.38%** | **9.15%** |
| `qsearch::<NonPv>` | — | 4.07% | — |
| `i8_deltas_into_inner` | 10.36% | **ABSENT — PGO inlines it** | — |

**The directive's percentages were closer to the shipped truth than my
"correction" was.** It was evidently measured on a PGO build. I saw one genuinely
dead name (`i16_deltas_into`, killed by study 32's i8 change), generalised from it
to "the whole list is stale", and re-derived numbers from the wrong binary. Perf
study 57 caught this independently — "your profile table came from a
differently-inlined build" — and it was right.

a symbol **that does not exist in the shipped binary.**

**What survives from the earlier finding:** the `i16_deltas_*` family really is

frame really is startup. What does *not* survive is my claim that

profile. **Both are alive and large in the shipped binary — 6.38% and 9.30% — and
both were on the directive's list all along.**

`search::<NonPv>` **23.53%** · `current_full` **12.40%** ·

`forward_into` and `current_full` are the two largest genuinely-untouched blocks,

**Standing rule going forward:** profile `engines/bin/*-pgo`, built with

## Tick — 2026-07-31, study 57 merged; its PGO challenge CHECKED, and I had a confound

**Study 57 merged at `05ff213f`**, integrated with study 56, gate green on the
merged tree, byte-reproducible (`.text` md5 `8876f37d`).

**The conflict was resolved as specified, keeping both wins:** `resolve_into` is
`&mut [RowOffset; DELTA_CAP]` written `adds[add_len & M] = RowOffset::of(idx)` —
study 56's fixed size discharges the bounds check on the **write**, study 57's
pre-scaling stops the kernel recomputing `feature * ACC` per tile on the **read**.

**Re-measured against post-56 main, the arm got BIGGER, not smaller:**
−0.694% → **−0.772%** (−41.27 → −45.40 i/n, +10%), with a branch **sign flip**
(−0.014% 24/24 → +0.015% 0/24). Both left **unattributed** — correctly, since the
plausible mechanisms are hypotheses. A dedicated arm-A-only binary was built on
the new base rather than subtracting arm B from the combined number, which would
have been two unmeasured inferences deep.

Combined post-56, 39 rounds: instructions **−0.523%** (39/39), branches −2.344%,
`ls_dispatch` −0.667%, cycles **−1.228%** (29/39, p=0.0034), null +0.033%.

**Method finding worth more than the arm:** the same cycles contrast read
**−0.790% at 24 rounds and FAILED its own sign test** (17/24, p=0.0639); at 39
rounds it is −1.228%, p=0.0034. Stopping at 24 would have produced "the cycles win
halved after integration" — **a story, not a measurement.** A ~1% cycles effect on
this box needs **~40 paired rounds for the SIGN TEST**, not the 23 that resolve the
median. Our σ = 1.57% note covers the median only; this extends it.

### It challenged my PGO finding with a control I had not run — and it was right to

Study 57 built the PGO pair, got −1.611% instructions, then **declined to quote
it** because a direct PGO-vs-non-PGO contrast of `base56` (same source, same
`target-cpu`, only `-C profile-use` differing) read **instructions +0.060%** — no
PGO win at all. It escalated instead of resolving, and named a cheap falsifier.

**I checked it. My measurement had a real confound:** my non-PGO builds used
`-C target-cpu=x86-64-v3` while `build_pgo.sh` uses `target-cpu=native`, **so my
"PGO pair" varied two things at once.** `build_pgo.sh` honours `PGO_CPU`, so the
isolation is one build:

| contrast (current main) | delta |
|---|---|
| **PGO alone** (`PGO_CPU=x86-64-v3`, target-cpu held fixed) | **−82.6 i/n (−1.408%)** |
| **target-cpu alone** (v3 → native, no PGO either side) | −20.3 i/n (−0.346%) |

**PGO works.** Study 57's `+0.060%` does **not** reproduce at main — it is a
result about its own `base56`, worth its own look but not an overturning.

**The absorption finding survives, with its label corrected.** target-cpu
contributes only −0.346%, so PGO dominates the config difference. But the honest
statement is **"native+PGO absorbs ~38% relative to v3-non-PGO, with PGO the
dominant term"** — not the clean "PGO absorbs 38%" I wrote. Memory updated with
the caveat and the one-build isolation recipe.

## Tick — 2026-07-31 12:0x, a contention-immune control: is the perf work behaviourally inert?

The fixed-TIME match still needs a quiet box (study 58 is running). But there is a
control that is **contention-immune by construction** and has never been run:

**Fixed-NODE match, `archon-main-pgo` vs `archon-pre52-pgo`, same canonical net,
25k nodes, 1500 games.** Both are PGO, both serve the same net; they differ only
by studies 52–57.

**The prediction is exact, not approximate: 0 Elo.** Every study in the series was
gated node-identical (5,001,562) with the eval hash unmoved, so at fixed nodes the
two binaries should make **identical decisions**. Anything outside noise means
some study changed *search behaviour* in a way the bench frame did not catch —
which the bench cannot rule out, since it is one fixed position set at one depth
and says nothing about time management, hash pressure across a real game, or
repetition state.

This is the null the perf programme has never had. It costs 15 minutes and runs
happily under load.

**Two path bugs of mine on the way in**, both worth naming because they are the
same class: I extracted `BOOK` from `screen.sh` with a grep that dropped the
`$ROOT` prefix, producing `/engines/books/…`, and the heredoc then froze that wrong
value at write time so my first `sed` fix edited a string that was already
expanded. **Reconstructing a script's variable by grepping its source is
guesswork** — the same failure family as inferring a binary's commit from its
mtime, which cost me a phantom −0.473% earlier today.

## Tick — 2026-07-31, THE NODE-NULL IS PERFECT: the perf work is provably behaviourally inert

`archon-main-pgo` vs `archon-pre52-pgo`, same canonical net, fixed 25k nodes,
reported at the 998-game interval:

```
Elo: -0.00 +/- 0.00        DrawRatio (pairs): 100.00%
Games: 998  Wins: 271  Losses: 271  Draws: 456
Ptnml(0-2): [0, 0, 499, 0, 0]
```

**Every one of 499 game pairs split exactly 1–1. Zero deviations.** Two binaries
differing by six merged studies and **263 instructions/node** played *identically*
in every position. The 271/271 win/loss split is pure colour symmetry — the same
game, played from both sides.

**This is stronger than a null result; it is a determinism proof.** A conventional
null says "no effect detected within ±10 Elo". A degenerate pentanomial says the
decisions were **bit-identical in live play**, which no aggregate could establish.

### What it buys, and it is not small

1. **Node-identity holds in real games, not just on the bench.** The bench frame
   is one position set at one depth; it says nothing about time management, hash
   pressure across a game, or repetition state. Those are now covered by
   measurement rather than by assumption. This null had never been run.
2. **The entire perf programme cannot have changed strength at fixed nodes.**
   Therefore any Elo it produces is **100% attributable to speed** — no
   behavioural side-channel, no accidental search change hiding inside a "pure
   speed" claim.
3. **It makes the pending fixed-TIME match clean by construction.** Whatever that
   match reads is speed and nothing else. Very few speed measurements get to say
   that; ours now can, and it cost 15 minutes on a contended box.

It also confirms, from the other direction, why **fixed-node screens can never
price perf work** — here they returned a mathematically exact zero.

**Still owed and now the only remaining question:** the fixed-TIME PGO match. Both
binaries exist, the behavioural confound is eliminated, and it waits solely on a
quiet box. Study 58 is running.

**D49:** sb989, 193k pos/s, four services active.

## Tick — 2026-07-31, THE FIXED-TIME MATCH IS INFEASIBLE. Plan corrected.

I have been carrying "run a fixed-TIME PGO match" as the queued next step for
several ticks. **I costed it and it does not survive the arithmetic.**

| | |
|---|---|
| PGO-measured instruction win | −2.686% |
| if it converts 1:1 to speed | 1.0276× → **~4.71 Elo** |
| games to resolve at 2σ (bar 2.36) | **26,408** |
| games to resolve at 3σ | 59,418 |
| wall-clock at 0.2s+0.002s, conc 5 | **29.3 hours** |
| at 1s+0.01s | 88.0 hours |
| at 10s+0.1s | 440 hours |

**29 hours at the fastest usable TC, for a 2σ read on a single perf series.**
That is not a good use of a box that is also training D49, and at very short TC
the result would additionally be confounded by time-management effects that have
nothing to do with instruction count.

**This is why the perf programme has never been priced in Elo — not negligence,
but a genuine measurement-cost wall.** Worth stating plainly rather than leaving
"fixed-time match" on the queue indefinitely as if it were merely waiting for a
quiet hour.

### The correct route, and it is much cheaper

**Measure SPEED precisely, then convert with our own calibration constant.**

1. **Wall-clock median panel, ~40 paired rounds, quiet box, criterion
   pre-registered as median-only** (already registered, before the run — three
   panels showed the median null stable in [−0.132%, +0.972%] while the mean null
   reached −2.068% with sd 6.3–9.5). Study 57 independently established that a
   ~1% effect needs **~40 rounds for the sign test**, not the 23 that resolve the
   median — so 40 it is. **Cost: ~10 minutes.**
2. **Convert via ~120 Elo per speed doubling** — our own measured constant
   (exp-0154: V beat S-post +38 at equal time, 1.51× speed ≈ +73 Elo). Label the
   result **derived**, never measured.

**The node-null makes this conversion legitimate in a way it would not otherwise
be.** Ptnml [0, 0, 499, 0, 0] proved the two binaries are behaviourally identical
at fixed nodes, so the speed ratio is the *entire* difference between them. There
is no behavioural term to contaminate the conversion — which is exactly the
assumption a speed→Elo constant needs and almost never gets.

**Standing correction:** the queue item is now "high-n wall-clock panel on a quiet
box", not "fixed-time match". The PGO pair remains built and useful for it.

**D49:** heldout sb990 ema 0.010701, 193k pos/s, four services active.
**Study 58** working, no builds in flight.

```
Games: 1500  Wins: 421  Losses: 421  Draws: 658
Ptnml(0-2): [0, 0, 750, 0, 0]
```

**All 750 pairs split exactly 1–1. Zero deviations at full sample.** The 998-game
interim was not a small-sample artifact; determinism holds across the whole run.
The perf series is behaviourally inert in live play, proven rather than assumed.

### The quiet window arrived and was used immediately

`nodenull3` finished, `rustc = 0`, no builds in flight — the first genuinely quiet
box since the plan was corrected. **The 40-round wall-clock panel is running now**
(`wc40`), against the PGO pair.

Design, all of it fixed *before* the run:

- **Interleaved null and arm within each round** — each round times `pre52-pgo`,
  a byte-identical copy of it, and `main-pgo`. Null and arm therefore experience
  the *same* contention. This was the fix for the earlier failure where the null
  drifted −0.132% → +0.972% *between* a null phase and an arm phase as a third
  party's build came and went.
- **Blocked rotation** of which binary runs first, so position in the round cannot
  alias onto a binary.
- **40 rounds**, per study 57's finding that a ~1% effect needs ~40 paired rounds
  for the **sign test** (its own contrast read −0.790% and *failed* its sign test
  at 24 rounds, then −1.228% at p=0.0034 at 39).
- **Median-only criterion, pre-registered** (registered two ticks ago, before any
  of this data existed): licence requires |null median| < |arm median| / 2.
- On licence, convert via **~120 Elo/doubling** (exp-0154) and label the result
  **DERIVED**, never measured.

**Note what the panel measures:** `archon-main-pgo` was built at post-56 main, so
this prices **studies 52–56**. Study 57 merged afterwards and is not in it.

**D49:** sb996 — about to cross **sb1000**. 193k pos/s, four services active,
8.5 GB free, 154 GB disk. **Study 58** working, no builds.

## Tick — 2026-07-31, THE PERF PROGRAMME IS PRICED: −4.163% headline = +1.77 DERIVED Elo

The 40-round interleaved panel **licensed under its pre-registered criterion**,
and the perf series finally has a strength number.

```
NULL (byte-identical PGO) : median +0.109%   sd 5.358   sign 20/40 neg   p = 1.0000
ARM  (main-PGO vs pre52)  : median -1.020%   sd 4.989   sign 29/40 neg   p = 0.0064
LICENSED: |0.109| < |1.020| / 2
```

**A null with a sign test of exactly 20/40 and p = 1.0000 is as clean as this
instrument gets** — and it is the direct payoff of interleaving null and arm
*within* each round. The earlier null-then-arm design had its null drift
**−0.132% → +0.972%** between phases as a third party's build came and went.

### The full honest accounting for studies 52–56

| stage | value |
|---|---|
| non-PGO instructions — **what the studies report** | **−4.163%** |
| PGO instructions | −2.686% — native+PGO absorbs **38%** |
| **PGO wall-clock** | **−1.020%** — instructions overstate time a further **2.63×** |
| **DERIVED Elo** (120/doubling, exp-0154) | **+1.77** |

**Direction is established (p = 0.0064); magnitude is loose.** The median's
precision is ≈±0.99%, so the honest range is **+0.1 to +3.5 Elo**. Stated as
DERIVED, never measured.

**So a −4.163% instruction headline is worth under two Elo.** Not zero, and it
compounds across studies — but **~4× smaller than the headline** and ~2.4× smaller
than the PGO figure. Every study number in this document should be read through
that chain from now on.

**What makes the conversion legitimate rather than hand-waving:** the fixed-node
match of this exact PGO pair returned `Ptnml [0, 0, 750, 0, 0]` over 1500 games.
The binaries are behaviourally identical, so **the speed ratio is the entire
difference between them** — the assumption a speed→Elo constant needs and almost
never gets.

**And it confirms the infeasibility call was right.** Pricing the *true* +1.77
Elo by direct match would need far more than the 26,408 games (29 h) computed for
a hypothetical 4.7 — well over 100,000 games. The panel did it in **eight
minutes**.

### What this does NOT say

It does not say the perf work is worthless. +1.77 Elo for five studies is real,
it accumulates, and studies 57/58 are not in this number (the PGO binary predates
them). It does say that **the headline instruction figure is not the deliverable**
and that expectations for future studies should be set against wall-clock, not
`instructions:u`.

**D49:** sb996→sb1000, 193k pos/s, four services active. **Study 58** working.

the gap more interesting, not less. **I am not inventing a mechanism for it.**

## Tick — 2026-07-31, THE 6× GAP IS EXPLAINED: an arm's value is target-cpu dependent

Last tick I flagged that study 57 self-reported ≈−31 i/n and delivered **−187.6**
on my build, and said I would not invent a mechanism. **Bisected it instead, and
the cause is exact.**

Its own arithmetic implied a base of **5880.8 i/n**. My post-56 gate build reads
**6058.1**. So I rebuilt both endpoints with `target-cpu=native`:

| build | post-56 | post-57 | **study 57 delivers** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **`native`** (what we SHIP) | **5880.9** | 5850.1 | **−30.8 i/n (−0.523%)** |
| **`x86-64-v3`** (what the GATE builds) | 6058.1 | 5870.4 | **−187.6 i/n (−3.097%)** |

**5880.9 against its implied 5880.8 — one decimal.** Study 57 measured on native
and reported `−0.523%`, which reproduces **exactly**. Neither of us was wrong;
**we measured different compilers.**

**Mechanism:** `native` on this box has AVX-512 VBMI2 and already does much of
what arm A does by hand — pre-scaling fold row offsets so the kernel stops
recomputing `feature * ACC` per tile. On v3 it does not, so the hand-written
version wins six times as much.

**The distinction that matters, and it is easy to conflate:** comparing v3 vs
native on the *same* code is a **small** difference (−20.3 i/n, measured earlier).
The value of an **arm** between the two can differ **6×**. Those are different
questions.

### Consequence: our gate build is not our shipped build

| | pre-52 → post-58 |
|---|---|
| gate (v3, non-PGO) | **−486.8 i/n (−7.700%)** |
| **SHIPPED (native+PGO)** | **−317.7 i/n (−5.254%)** |

**Shipped retains 65% of the gate headline.** Both provenance-verified by
rebuilding from source, not by trusting a binary's mtime.

This refines — and partly re-labels — the earlier "PGO absorbs 38%" finding.
Some of that absorption was never PGO; it was `target-cpu`. The three effects are
now separated:

- **PGO alone** (target-cpu held fixed): −82.6 i/n
- **native vs v3, same code**: −20.3 i/n
- **an ARM's value between v3 and native**: up to **6×**

**Standing rule added:** keep gating on v3 `instructions:u` — cheap, portable,
0.000 null. **But rebuild on `native` (+PGO) before quoting a number as
delivered**, and when reconciling against a study's figure, **ask what `RUSTFLAGS`
it used first.** Two correct measurements can disagree 6× and both be right.

**D49:** sb1010, heldout ema 0.010655/0.010691 (sawtooth), 193k pos/s, four
services active, 154 GB disk.
