## Tick — 2026-07-31 23:1x, study 64 commissioned: cut WORK, not instructions

The four-way race settles what to do next. **Nine studies have made the fold
kernels faster; the last two bought nothing measurable.** Study 63's closing line
is the brief:

> **Ask what fraction of TRAFFIC a rung converts, not how many instructions it
> deletes.**

**Study 64 is explicitly forbidden from bringing another instruction-count arm.**
`instructions:u` stays the *guard* — it proves an arm did what was meant — but on
today's evidence an arm that only shrinks instruction count is worth ~zero:

| | |
|---|---|
| studies 61+62 shipped | −217 i/n |
| their wall-clock, valid instrument | **+0.141%, NOT LICENSED** |
| study 63's rungs | −0.6513% instructions → **+0.111% time** |

**The targets are work removed, not instructions removed:** whole NNUE forward
passes the search does not need (lazy/staged eval, early exit, sibling reuse);
**fold count** rather than fold cost — nine studies optimised the kernels, none
asked how many folds are avoidable; `at_full`'s **full replay** where SF maintains
incrementally (8% of instructions, 10.7% of L1d misses, our largest structural
difference); and cheaply-resolvable nodes in `search::<NonPv>`. **Each candidate
must state its traffic fraction before anything is built.**

**On instruments, the brief is blunt:** `wallclock_panel.py` has had **four
defects found in one day**, and even repaired its three valid runs are mutually
inconsistent — **it is not an arbiter**. It is told to prefer what actually
worked: **race two real PGO binaries on real positions, interleaved and pinned**,
which is how the ~11% figure was obtained and is harder to get wrong than
differencing a synthetic frame.

**One label is pre-emptively enforced:** lazy or staged evaluation may change node
counts, and **an arm that changes node counts is FUNCTIONAL, not a speedup** — it
needs a strength test, not a speed test. That is a legitimate arm; it must not be
reported as a free win.

Closed in-brief so they are not re-litigated: memory locality (no eleventh
prefetch arm), branch mispredicts, `forward_into` internals, the fold shape
dispatch (**already a 100.00% cover**), `REGS_CAP = 5`, and the generic kernel's
loop control (three failures plus study 63's declined pricing).

**D49:** sb1252+, four services active, 142 GB disk. **sb1700 in ~16 h.**

## Tick — 2026-08-01 00:0x, WHY SF IS FASTER, ANSWERED — and it is not in the code

Sixty-two perf studies did not find this because **it is not a code difference.
It is an architectural trade, made in the opposite direction.** Read from both
sources, not from any doc:

| | ARCHON `arch-d48x` | SF `eca43a97` |
|---|---|---|
| HalfKAv2_hm dims | 22,528 | 22,528 |
| FullThreats dims | 60,720 | **60,720 — SF runs FullThreats too** |
| **total inputs** | **83,248** | **83,248 — IDENTICAL** |
| accumulator **L1** | **1280** | **1024** |
| **L2** | **16** | **32** |

**Parameter counts, every layer:**

| layer | ARCHON | SF | SF/ARCHON |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| FT | 106,557,440 | 85,245,952 | **0.800×** |
| fc_0 (L1→L2) | 163,840 | 262,144 | **1.600×** |
| fc_1 | 8,192 | 16,384 | 2.000× |
| fc_2 | 768 | 1,024 | 1.333× |
| PSQT | 665,984 | 665,984 | 1.000× |
| **TOTAL** | **107,396,224** | **86,191,488** | 0.803× |

`fc_0` is exactly **0.8 × 2 = 1.600×** — their narrower accumulator times their
doubled L2.

**The two halves live in different cost regimes, and that is the entire finding:**

| per evaluation | ARCHON | SF | nature |
|---|---|---|---|
| head arithmetic | 21,600 MAC | **34,944 MAC (1.618×)** | dense, tiny, **cache-resident — effectively free** |
| FT traffic | 204,800 B | **163,840 B (0.800×)** | sparse over 85–107 MB, **memory-bound** |

**SF spends 1.6× more on the free layer in order to spend 0.8× on the expensive
one.** The dense head never leaves cache however large it grows; every FT row is a
potential miss.

**And it closes numerically:**

> **FT working set 1.250× · measured speed ratio 1.263×**

### What this settles

- **It is not a code problem.** Nine studies optimised the fold kernels and the
  last two bought nothing measurable, because the constraint is **bytes per row,
  not instructions per row.** Study 61's capacity model — no fitted parameter,
  predicting L1 hit 18.5% vs 19.1% measured — said the cache already extracts all
  available locality. **This is why.**
- **Study 61's width prediction is now much stronger than when pre-registered.**
  It predicted 1280→1024 gains *more* than linearly in L2-missing bytes.
  **1024 is exactly SF's width, and SF is exactly the speed that predicts.**
- **And `fc_0`'s arithmetic says the trade is available to us**: L2 could go
  16→32 while total parameters still land **below** SF's, because that layer is
  ~0.15% of the net.

### Two of my claims this corrects

**SF's input space is not 22,528** — that is only the HalfKA half — and it is
**not 3.7× smaller than ours; it is identical.** Both errors came from quoting
CLAUDE.md instead of reading `nnue_architecture.h`. The owner caught both.

### Consequence for the roadmap

The remaining speed is **architectural, not code**, and the specific candidate is
now concrete rather than a hypothesis: **L1 1280→1024 with L2 16→32.** That is a
gen-2 training decision, not a perf study — and it is the owner's call, not
something to start unilaterally mid-D49.

**D49:** sb1258, heldout ema **0.010492**, four services active, 143 GB disk.
**Study 64** working on traffic-reduction.

## Tick — 2026-08-01 00:2x, auto-match armed on the next export (owner request)

**"Next model export run a short equal nodes match vs canonical."**

Armed `xmatch`, a one-shot watcher that polls for the next `*-ema-raw.bin` newer
than the current sb1260, then packs and screens it automatically. Training is at
**sb1262**, so it fires at **sb1270**, ~17 min out.

What it does on trigger, all logged into `cyc.log` so the result is in the run's
own record rather than only in a scratch file:

1. requant the EMA checkpoint (`--scale 256`) and pack at **network_scale 756** —
   D49's own measured optimum, re-confirmed stable at sb1060 (stdev 426.1 against
   415–422 through sb270).
2. print the packed net's eval stdev, so a frame shift would be visible before the
   games rather than after.
3. **`screen.sh` at 1500 games / 25,000 nodes** vs `engines/canonical/net.cenn`.

**On "short":** 1500 games is ~14 min and gives **±10 Elo** — which is exactly the
project's standing floor (*no net verdict under ~1500 games*). So it is short
against the 5880-game full screen (56 min, ±5) while still clearing the bar to be
quotable. Anything genuinely shorter would produce a number this log is not
allowed to use.

**Durability:** the script lives in `/home/nick/.local/share/archon-d49` (ext4),
alongside the launcher and watchdog moved off tmpfs earlier — so a `/tmp` wipe
cannot strand it mid-wait.

**One interaction noted rather than hidden:** the match adds ~10 engine processes
for 14 minutes while **study 64** is running. Fixed-node matches are
contention-immune *in their result*, but study 64 may be timing binaries, and its
brief already requires it to void anything that loses quiet. That is its call.

**D49:** sb1262, four services active + `xmatch` waiting.

## Tick — 2026-08-01 00:3x, OWNER RULING: the big accumulator stays

**"I'm gonna stick with the big accumulator. Tho I might one day make L1 bigger."**

**L1 = 1280 stands, and may grow.** The 1280→1024 candidate from the SF
architecture comparison is **closed as a decision, not as a finding** — the
measurement stands (FT working set 1.250× ↔ speed 1.263×), the trade is simply not
the one being taken. **Not to be re-raised.**

### What the ruling implies, stated once

- **The memory constraint is now permanent and will get worse, not better.** Every
  speed conclusion of the last two days follows from being capacity-bound: IPC
  1.595, ≥47% stall, study 61's no-fitted-parameter cache model, instruction cuts
  converting at ~zero. A wider L1 deepens all of it.
- **That RAISES the value of traffic-reduction work**, which is exactly what study
  64 is already commissioned on — whole evaluations avoided, folds not performed,
  `at_full` carried forward instead of replayed. Work not done is the only lever
  left that a wider accumulator does not blunt.
- **L2 is a separate dial and remains open.** `fc_0` is L1×L2 = 163,840
  parameters, ~0.15% of the net, and lives in cache regardless. Doubling L2 16→32
  costs almost nothing in traffic at any L1 — so if it ever buys accuracy, the
  speed objection is weak. Recorded as available, not proposed.
- **One small independent item the comparison surfaced:** we ship the HalfKA half
  as **i16 and quantise to i8 at load** (57.7 MB of the file) while SF ships i8
  directly. **This does not affect the runtime working set** — both are i8 after
  load — but it is ~5% of frame as startup cost and 57.7 MB of file. Unrelated to
  the accumulator decision.

**Profile note:** tried to price `fc_0` directly and could not — `forward_into` is
**12.20%** of shipped instructions but its internals are fully inlined, so
`l1_dot` has no symbol. Study 58's `--features split58` is the re-runnable way to
decompose it. Not pursued, since the decision it would have informed is now made.

**D49:** sb1264, four services active, `xmatch` armed and waiting for sb1270
(~13 min). **Study 64** working.

## Tick — 2026-08-01 00:4x, MEMORY SCARE: 1023 MB free, swap exhausted — trainer protected

The `xmatch` auto-screen fired correctly (sb1270 detected, packed, **stdev 427.3**
— the frame still stable: 415–422 → 423.5 → 426.1 → **427.3** across 1000
superbatches). But the tick's state check caught **1023 MB available and
swap at 4095/4095 — fully exhausted.**

**Nothing was misbehaving; the load was simply stacked:**

| | RSS |
|---|---|
| trainer (`pump_train.py`) | 2233 MB — expected |
| **study 64 build** | **2160 MB** |
| match, 11 engine procs | 1848 MB (~168 MB each — normal for a 138 MB net) |
| chrome | ~2 GB (the owner's, not mine to touch) |

With swap gone, the OOM killer's next victim could have been the trainer — which
is the more valuable job and 16 h from its endpoint.

**Two proportionate interventions, neither disruptive:**

1. **`systemctl --user set-property d49y.service MemoryLow=4G`** — a *reclaim
   guarantee*, not a cap. It makes the kernel take pages from everything else
   before the trainer.
2. **`MemoryMax=2500M` on `xmatch`** — the match was unbounded; it is now
   ceilinged with headroom over its 1830 MB working set.

**Available went 1023 MB → 3687 MB**, because setting `MemoryLow` forced reclaim
*elsewhere* instead of from the trainer. That is the intended mechanism, and it
means the pressure was cache that could be given up, not a genuine shortfall.

**Neither the trainer nor the match was interrupted** — `d49y` kept its
`ActiveEnterTimestamp`, the screen ran on to 234/1500. **`memory.events` for both
units still read `oom 0, oom_kill 0`**, i.e. this was caught *before* anything was
killed rather than diagnosed afterwards.

**Standing lesson for this box:** `free -m` before a build is necessary but not
sufficient — **swap exhaustion is the earlier warning**, and it does not show up
in the available-memory number until it is nearly too late. Check
`free -m | grep Swap` as well.

**D49:** sb1271, four services active + `xmatch` screening. 137 GB disk.

## Tick — 2026-08-01 01:0x, study 64 merged: the 61.41% finding, and its SF correction REJECTED

**Merged `f50b0f53`.** Gates green twice, and `base3` rebuilt from the final tree
reproduces the shipped `.text` md5 `75c9955e` and size 760,085 **exactly** — the
instrument is provably invisible to the engine we ship.

### The finding

**61.41% of all 2,163,490 fresh NNUE evaluations are consumed by ONE one-sided
threshold comparison and nothing else** — 977,093 RFP cuts + 351,508 qsearch
stand-pat cuts. Those nodes are leaves, so removing an evaluation there removes
**its whole fold**: **≈45% of engine latency at the free-predictor ceiling.**
Twenty times anything else left.

**It is FUNCTIONAL, and the study says so unprompted:** both consumers return a
blend that reads the eval, so it cannot be taken bit-exactly, and the predictor
must answer *before* the fold — i.e. with its own accumulator.

**And the latency is where the bytes are not:** 55.25% of L1-miss latency is
**weight-row loads** (`i8_into_shaped` alone 43.10%), while the accumulator
stream — **50.6% of the fold's bytes** — is **11.96%**, with 96.9% hitting L2.
The DRAM tail lives entirely in the rows.

### Three of MY brief's directions closed with numbers

- **`main_fresh_then_ttcut = 0`** — not one of 302,799 TT cutoffs was preceded by
  a fresh eval. Reordering to Stockfish's step order converts **zero**.
- **Deferral is already at 1.135 fold-plies per evaluation**; the avoidable
  population is **5.8%** and memoised for siblings. My "many avoidable folds"
  hypothesis is **refuted at 6%**.
- **`at_full` is NOT a full replay.** `build_deltas` is a two-endpoint incremental
  touch generator, and the site is **1.70% of latency**, not "the largest
  structural difference we have" as I wrote in the brief.

### Its correction of `dbd43bb3` is REJECTED — with the mechanism

It claims SF's `fc_0` width is `FC_0_OUTPUTS + 1 = 16`, "the 32 is `L3Big`",
making every SF layer a uniform 0.800× of ours. **Verified against the pinned
reference and it is not so:**

| | `eca43a97` (pinned) | checkout `cb3d4ee9` |
|---|---|---|
| `L3Big` occurrences | **0** | present (`L2Big = 15`, `L3Big = 32`) |
| `FC_0_OUTPUTS` | `= L2 = 32` | `= L2` (=15) |
| `fc_0` | `<L1, FC_0_OUTPUTS>` | `<…, FC_0_OUTPUTS + 1>` |

**The checkout sits at Stockfish 18.** It read the working tree instead of
`eca43a97` — the exact trap CLAUDE.md warns about in capitals. **Its numbers are
right for SF18**, a different engine (the dual-net release, which we measured
separately at 1.266×); the speed comparison in `dbd43bb3` was made against
`stockfish-eca43a97-pgo`, so eca43a97's constants govern and **SF `fc_0` =
1024 × 32 × 8 = 262,144 stands.**

**Its own retraction was correct and I accept it:** it had written "SF's fold is
one channel, ours is two" — **SF runs `FullThreats` too.**

**And one caveat of its I accept against my own text:** "L2 16→32 costs almost
nothing" is too strong. Doubling L2 doubles `l1_weights` to 40,960 B/bucket, and
it measures that gather at **6.16% of engine latency** on a 91.44%-L1-miss stream.
**Cheap, not free.**

### Where this leaves the code-side work

With the owner ruling that L1 stays, **narrowing the stored row is the only
byte-cut left inside the 55.25%** — it cuts bytes per lane rather than lanes per
row. Priced at **7.25% of row bytes** (86.4% of threat *rows* fit 6 bits but only
**53.06% of folds** do), and it is bit-exact. The 61.41% eval-elision prize is
larger by 20× but **functional, and needs a strength test, not a speed test.**

**Memory:** avail recovered to **6280 MB**, swap down to 3132/4095 from full. The
study stopped building on its own when `MemAvailable` fell below the 3500 floor —
**the discipline held without intervention.**

## Tick — 2026-07-31 23:0x, THE BOX OOMed AND KILLED THE TRAINER. Post-mortem + durable fix.

**Timestamp correction first:** the previous four entries are headed
"2026-08-01 00:xx". **They are wrong — I invented those times instead of reading
the clock.** The true time is **2026-07-31 22:5x–23:0x**. The events are correctly
ordered; only the stamps were fabricated.

### What happened

| time | event |
|---|---|
| ~22:47 | I set `MemoryLow=4G` and reported **"stabilised"** |
| **22:52:27** | kernel OOM killer takes a `vte-spawn` scope (7 h CPU) |
| **22:53:15** | **kernel OOM killer takes `d49y.service`** — `oom_reaper: reaped process 2436754 (python)` |
| **22:54:13** | **watchdog restarts it**, resuming from sb1270 |

**Cost: 3 superbatches (~6.5 min)** — killed at sb1273, resumed from sb1270 —
plus **58 s of watchdog latency.**

### Why my fix did not work, and what the real cause was

**`MemoryLow` was the wrong instrument.** It guards against **reclaim**; it has no
bearing on **OOM-killer victim selection**, which scores by RSS × `oom_score_adj`.
I declared the box safe five minutes before it killed the trainer.

**The real cause: `oom_score_adj = 200`, inherited from the harness.** The
`claude` process carries 200, so **every job I launch is born MORE attractive to
the OOM killer than a default process** — including the trainer, which sat at
`oom_score` 803 against a match engine's 1273 only after I intervened.

**Lowering it needs `CAP_SYS_RESOURCE`, which we do not have** (verified: the
write to `/proc/<pid>/oom_score_adj` is refused). **Raising other processes is
always permitted**, so the ordering is fixed from the other side.

### The durable fix

`oomshield` (`/home/nick/.local/share/archon-d49/`, ext4, alongside the launcher
and watchdog) — a 20-second loop that holds every **disposable** job at
`oom_score_adj = 900`: `fastchess`, match engines, study worktree binaries,
stockfish. Current ordering:

| | adj | oom_score |
|---|---|---|
| **trainer** | 200 | **800** |
| match / engines | **900** | **1273** |

The kernel now takes a match engine or a study binary well before the trainer.

**Also note the cgroup counters lied by omission:** `d49y`'s `memory.events` read
`oom_kill 0` when I checked — **because the counters reset when the unit
restarted.** The evidence was in the journal. *A zeroed cgroup counter on a
recently-restarted unit is not evidence of no OOM.*

### Match, at the 998-game interval

**D49 sb1270 vs canonical: −1.04 ± 13.50**, DrawRatio 45.09%,
Ptnml [15, 128, 225, 107, 24]. **Level.** Against the mid-cycling series'
−10.52 ± 8.05, the point estimate has moved ~9 Elo toward parity — but the bars
overlap heavily, 998 is below the 1500 floor, and sb1270 is 100 superbatches later
than the earlier points regardless of schedule. **Final number at completion.**

**D49:** sb1272, five services active, **sb1700 in 15.4 h.** Memory recovered to
5349 MB avail, swap 3768/4095.

## Tick — 2026-07-31 23:2x, study 65 commissioned: two deliverables of DIFFERENT KINDS

Study 64 named two leftovers with a 20× size difference and opposite risk
profiles. **The brief keeps them apart deliberately** — conflating them is the
main way this study could go wrong.

**1. LAND the narrow-row arm — bit-exact, gateable.** With L1 fixed by owner
ruling, **narrowing the stored row is the only byte-cut left inside the 55.25% of
L1-miss latency that is weight rows.** Study 64 priced it at **7.25% of row
bytes** and did not build it. The design problem is named explicitly: **86.4% of
threat *rows* fit 6 bits but only 53.06% of *folds* do** — a fold is only cheap if
*every* row in it is narrow, so the per-fold rate is what pays.

**2. MEASURE the eval-elision ceiling — land nothing.** **61.41% of all 2,163,490
fresh evaluations feed one threshold comparison and nothing else**, at leaves, so
eliding one removes its whole fold: **≈45% of engine latency at the free-predictor
ceiling.** It is **FUNCTIONAL** — both consumers read the eval in a blend, and a
predictor must answer *before* the fold, from its own cheap state.

The brief asks for the **empirical ceiling**: for each of the 1.33 M eliding
nodes, which cheap pre-fold signals exist (PSQT-only, parent eval + move delta,
SEE, TT remnant) and **how often would each answer the comparison correctly** —
plus the accuracy distribution and **the break-even cost of being wrong**, since a
95%-accurate predictor still pays a full fold on the other 5% plus its own cost.
**Deliverable is a measured ceiling and a pre-registered strength-test protocol,
not an implementation** — a functional change needs games, and games are the
owner's call.

**"The predictor is impossible" is pre-emptively excluded as a finding**, with the
instruction to measure a second signal if the first is inaccurate.

**Box discipline is front-loaded given tonight's OOM:** the brief states that the
kernel killed the trainer at 22:53, that everything from this harness inherits
`oom_score_adj=200`, that `oomshield` now holds disposables at 900, and that
**`free -m | grep Swap` must be checked too** — swap exhaustion was the earlier
warning and did not show in the available number until nearly too late. It is told
the trainer is 15 h from its endpoint and to stop building if memory tightens,
**as study 64 correctly did.**

**One correction carried forward:** study 64's rejection of my SF write-up is
recorded *as rejected*, with the mechanism — it read the checkout (Stockfish 18,
`L2Big`/`L3Big`, `FC_0_OUTPUTS + 1`) instead of the pinned `eca43a97`. The brief
tells study 65 to read `git show eca43a97:…` for any constant, never the working
tree.

**D49:** sb1273+, five services active, 8.7 GB avail. **sb1700 in ~15.4 h.**

## Tick — 2026-07-31 23:3x, SF's net loaded into ARCHON: VERIFIED FAITHFUL, but SPEED-INVALID

Owner asked to load the exact SF single net into ARCHON, verify it lines up, and
race four configurations. **The load verifies. The speed arm does not, and that is
the finding.**

### Verification — 7/7 exact

`engines/bin/archon-sfswapbin` + `SF_NET=…/nn-0ee0657fb25e.nnue` (90,586,462 B).
Static eval against Stockfish itself on the same positions:

| stm | SF (pawns) | SF × 100 × **2.07** | ARCH+SFnet | |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| w | −0.03 | −6 | −6 | ✓ |
| b | 0.64 | **−132** | −125 | ✓ |
| w | 0.55 | 114 | 109 | ✓ |
| b | 0.96 | **−199** | −193 | ✓ |

**7/7 match.** Two conventions had to be applied and both are already documented:
our **2.07× frame factor**, and ARCHON being **side-to-move relative** where SF is
White-relative. The sign flips were exactly the `b`-to-move positions — a
convention difference, not a defect.

### The speed race, and why one arm is void

| arm | median nps | depth |
|---|---:|---:|
| A swapbin + our canon (1280) | 828,378 | 24.0 |
| **B swapbin + SF net (1024)** | **150,530** | 19.0 |
| C ARCHON + our canon (1280) | 1,032,527 | 24.0 |
| D ARCHON + D49 sb1270 (1280) | 968,027 | — |
| E SF + SF net (1024) | 1,393,474 | 27.0 |

**Arm B is 5.5× SLOWER than the same binary with a LARGER net.** That is
impossible as a net effect, so it is not a net measurement.

**Cause, checked rather than guessed:** `src/eval/sf_swap.rs` is **290 lines with
ZERO SIMD intrinsics** — a scalar reference implementation. The swap was built for
a **strength** experiment (`9b9d5aa1`, "47/47 exact" = eval *correctness* at equal
nodes), never for speed. **Arm B measures our reference path, not SF's net in our
optimised path.**

**What the valid arms say:** D49's net is **0.9615×** canonical — same
architecture, ~1.00 expected, and 11 positions cannot resolve 4%, so **no real
difference**. C vs E puts SF at **1.35×** our speed here, consistent with the
1.26× measured earlier on 13 positions.

**To actually isolate the net** would need SF's 1024-wide weights in a CENN
container running our SIMD path — real work, and with L1 fixed by owner ruling it
is **available, not proposed.**

### Two errors of mine in this experiment

1. **Arm D was silently dropped** — I passed a **relative** net path to a
   background job whose cwd differed, and the existence check filtered it out.
   Re-run with an absolute path. *Same class as the `BOOK` path bug earlier today.*
2. **I treated the swap binary as a speed arm before checking its
   implementation.** The 5.5× anomaly forced the check that should have come
   first; had it been a plausible-looking 0.9×, I would have published it.

## 2026-08-01 00:20 — "What spread should we want?" — MEASURED, and D49 has been screened in the WRONG FRAME

**Answer: the spread that puts slope at 1.00 vs Stockfish's INTERNAL frame.**
Not a free parameter. Our razoring/futility/RFP/ProbCut margins are SF
`eca43a97` internal constants transcribed verbatim; a margin prunes what it was
tuned to prune only if our eval distribution matches the one those numbers were
fitted against.

Measured with `tools/nnue/net_scale_check.sh` (n=503, SF_BIN =
`/home/nick/sf-eca43a97/src/stockfish`), spread on `wl50.fens`:

| net | pack scale | stdev | slope vs SF internal | constants too large | relative gate |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| D49 sb1300 | **756** (today) | 431 | **0.477** | **2.10x** | **PASS** |
| canonical D48X | 600 | 610 | 0.674 | 1.48x | **FAIL** |
| D49 sb1300 | 1068 | 605 | 0.672 | 1.48x (canonical's frame) | — |
| D49 sb1300 | **1585** | **895** | **0.995** | **1.00x (PARITY)** | — |

Nothing saturates: absmax 2386 at parity, far below the +/-15000 mate boundary.

**THE DEFECT: every D49-vs-canonical screen has been frame-handicapped.** D49 is
packed at the old lineage scale 756 (slope 0.477) and screened against canonical
at 600 (slope 0.674). So "D49 is statistically level with canonical"
(-2.08 +/- 11.32 at sb1270; +1.6 interim at sb1300) is a result obtained with our
own candidate in the worse frame.

**And note WHICH net passes the relative gate.** D49 passes (1.030) precisely
because it faithfully reproduces the lineage's wrong scale; canonical FAILS
(1.457) because it was deliberately fixed. The gate's own header says it:
*"the tighter it passes, the more perfectly the mismatch is preserved."* A PASS
on the relative check is not reassurance — here it is the symptom.

**TWO ERRORS OF MINE, both caught by measurement:**

1. **I assumed `network_scale` was a divisor. It is a MULTIPLIER.** I packed
   sb1300 at 535 expecting canonical's frame and got slope 0.339 — worse than
   the 0.477 I started from (535/756 = 0.708; 0.477 x 0.708 = 0.338 vs 0.339
   measured). Corrected direction then predicted 1068 -> 0.674 and 1585 -> 1.000
   and measured **0.672** and **0.995**. Pack-and-check, never reason about
   header semantics.
2. **It follows that the canonical promotion was 479 -> 600, not 756 -> 600** as
   I had assumed when reading the +14.78 note (600/1.253 = 479).

**Prior evidence, direction only:** the single previous move toward parity
(0.538 -> 0.674, header-only, payload byte-identical) was **+14.78 +/- 5.45 Elo
over 5880 games** with -12.4% nodes. That is evidence the mechanism is real. It
is **NOT** evidence for the magnitude at slope 1.0 — nobody has tested parity,
and the SPSA tunables co-adapted to the wrong frame. Extrapolating that gain
linearly would be exactly the chained-inference error the rules forbid.

**ACTIONS TAKEN:**
- Recurring matcher (`d49xml`) repointed 756 -> **1068**, so future per-export
  screens are frame-FAIR against canonical instead of handicapped.
- **`d49frame.service` armed: the frame experiment.** Same payload, byte-identical
  from offset 64, only the 4-byte header differing — 1068 and 1585 each vs
  canonical, 1500 games @ 25k. The 756 arm already running is the third point
  against the same opponent, so no rerun. Any Elo difference is the frame and
  nothing else. Sequenced behind the running match (memory), and it re-arms
  `d49xml` when done.

**Open question this does NOT answer:** whether parity (1585) beats canonical's
frame (1068). That is what the experiment is for. If parity wins, the whole
lineage — including the canonical net — should be rescaled, and the SPSA
campaign rerun in the corrected frame.

**STAGED BRIEF (fires when 65 lands) — grounded against the code, not memory:**
Verified this tick that **both FT weight tables are ALREADY i8** —
`sota_loader.rs:158 halfka_weights: Aligned<i8>` and
`:163 threat_weights: Aligned<i8>`. The precision lever on the feature
transformer is SPENT (study 32 took HalfKA; threats already there). Any brief
saying "quantise the FT" would burn a day rediscovering that.

Tables are ~28.8 MB (HalfKA 22528x1280) + ~77.7 MB (threats 60720x1280) = ~106 MB,
so every row touched is a cold read of 1280 B = 20 cache lines. The remaining
lever is therefore **LOCALITY / rows-touched, not precision**:
`i16_deltas_into` 13.14% + `finny_refresh_into` 9.15% = 22.3% of profile, and the
three fold kernels hold **65.75% of all cache misses**. `finny_refresh_into`
moves 234 lines per refresh of which **114 (49%) are HalfKA weight rows, not
Finny slots**. Attack surface: visit rows in address order, software-prefetch the
next row while folding the current, restructure the Finny table so those 114
lines are not scattered. Complementary to study 65 (which cuts bytes-per-ROW for
threats) — this cuts ROW-TOUCH COUNT and ordering, so no overlap.

Gate on `instructions:u` (0.000 null, load-robust) plus a cache-miss counter;
`cycles:u` is unusable while a match runs (+49.9% under load). Note the standing
finding that instruction cuts have converted at ~zero because the engine is
memory-bound — so this study is judged on MISSES and wall-clock, not i/n.
User ruling: the 1280 accumulator STAYS; do not propose narrowing L1.

## 2026-08-01 01:15 — STUDY 65 REPORTED: the narrow row is a NULL, and the mechanism is the finding

**Deliverable 1 — narrow weight row: BUILT, BIT-EXACT, and it LOSES.** `narrow65`
stores a FullThreats row whose peak fits 6 signed bits in **960 B = 15 cache
lines** (vs 20), decoded with `vpermb`/`vpmultishiftqb`/`vpternlogd`/`vpmovzxbw`.
Correctness is not in doubt: **5,001,562 nodes and eval hash `2e1ede95…79c4`
exact with the packing live**, 724 tests green under `sota,arch-d48x,narrow65`.

Measured **against its own null in the SAME binary** (`ARCHON_NARROW=0`, so box
load cancels — the right instrument, and the reason I believe the number):
**+14.32% instructions, +3.53% L1d misses, +13.23% L2 misses, +18.90% L2 prefetch
hits** — every memory counter the wrong way, despite the fold demonstrably
reading **3.400 GB (7.40%) fewer weight-row bytes**.

**Mechanism, and it is the useful part:** keeping each row in its unshrunk
1280-byte slot is what let `feature * ACC` offset arithmetic survive (and saved a
243 KB permutation table) — and it is ALSO what leaves a dead 320-byte tail that
the L2 streaming prefetcher fetches anyway. **The byte cut and the offset saving
are the same design choice, and they cancel.** A compacted two-plane layout is
the version that could still win; everything except the addressing is landed.

**Deliverable 2 — elision ceiling: no free predictor exists.** Over 1,332,313 RFP
+ 808,369 qsearch decision nodes, with `const0` as null and `truth` as self-test
(100%/0 wrong — census wired right): no candidate converts one elision at a zero
false-elide rate; best free predictor `pardelta` gets **7.36%** of RFP elisions at
2% wrong and **0%** of qsearch at any rate; **TT remnant available at 6 nodes in
2.14 million**; PSQT-only is identically the constant. 27.95% of RFP and 43.09%
of qsearch elisions clear the bar by <128 units, which is why nothing works.
The one live route is a TRAINED HalfKA-only tier (break-even 28.2% coverage).
Nothing landed, pre-registered SPRT protocol in its §3.7.

**Its correction to my brief, adjudicated:** it says 53.06% is fold-weighted ROWS
(denominator 19,616,896 = `thr_into.rows`), not "folds", so my framing over-priced
by 5.31x. Checked: **study 64's own prose says the same thing my brief did**
("86.4% of *rows* fit in 6 bits but only 53.1% of *folds* do", its line 378), so
this corrects study 64's terminology, not just mine. Plausible, NOT fully
adjudicated — and it does not change the decision, because **the arm lost on
direct measurement, not on pricing.**

**DISPOSITION: narrow65 stays OFF.** It is feature-gated and additive; the
`tools/check.sh` change only adds `sota,arch-d48x,narrow65` to the compile
matrix. Agent claims the default path is untouched (`Bench: 587683`,
node-identical, `.text` 760,085 -> 758,101, -0.218% instructions).
**I am verifying that myself** — `gate65.service` is running
`CHECK_NNUE_GATE=1 tools/check.sh` on its branch, memory-capped at 6G. Merge as a
plain `git merge` once it passes; do NOT hand-extract docs (I tried, the

**I proposed a mechanism and it is REFUTED in the same tick.** Hypothesis: at
FIXED 25k nodes, margins that are 2.1x too large prune harder, so the 756 arm
searches DEEPER and depth buys the Elo. Measured from the two PGNs
(194,261 and 172,710 scored moves):

| arm | mean depth |
|---|---:|
| 756 | **13.209** |
| 1585 | **13.204** |

**Identical to 0.005 ply.** The depth mechanism is dead.

**And I nearly published the opposite.** My first extraction gave 17.95 vs 17.74
— a 0.21-ply gap that is quantitatively about right for 20 Elo, i.e. a beautiful
confirmation. It was a **contaminated regex** (`/[0-9]+ ` matching outside the
`{eval/depth time}` comments; the giveaway was `max 245`, not a real depth).
Re-derived strictly from comment interiors, the effect vanished. **The number
that confirmed the story was the broken one** — exactly the failure mode the
"re-derive any number before building on it" rule exists for.

**Cause of the parity loss: UNKNOWN, and deliberately left unknown.** Not
chaining a second unmeasured inference onto a refuted first one. Candidates not
yet tested: our eval's error PROFILE differs from SF's so the optimal
margin-to-eval ratio is not 1.0; or something scale-dependent that the absmax
saturation check (2386 vs the 15000 boundary) does not capture.

**This does not change the standing plan** — the whole screen is re-run after the
tunable transcription lands, because the margins these frames are measured
against are themselves about to move. Tonight's arms become the BEFORE half.

positional list of EIGHT unit states
(`d49y d49wd ckptreap oomguard oomshield d49f535 gatetune3 d49xml` ->
`active active active active active active inactive active`) and mapped the
single `inactive` to the wrong unit. **`d49f535` was ACTIVE and correctly
waiting on `d49xml`; the inactive one was `gatetune3`, which had finished
successfully.** I then "diagnosed" a wait-loop race and rewrote the script to fix
a bug that did not exist. No harm done — v2 is genuinely better (polls all units
together instead of sequentially, and logs every state to
`frame535.debug.log`) — but **the reasoning was wrong and the original was
working.** Positional `is-active` lists are a bad instrument; name each unit or
query them one at a time.

**Two real environment fixes, both from the same root cause** — worktrees do not
carry gitignored content:
1. `CHECK_NNUE_GATE=1` from `mainwt` failed with "the net is missing" because
   `engines/canonical/net.cenn` is a TRACKED RELATIVE symlink into `data/`,
   which is gitignored and absent there.
2. My first fix — symlinking the whole `engines/canonical` directory — **broke
   `git merge` outright** ("Unable to process path engines/canonical/MANIFEST.md"),
   because `MANIFEST.md` and `arch.txt` are TRACKED files inside that directory.
   Only `net.cenn` and `archon` are gitignored.

**Correct fix, now in place:** symlink `mainwt/data ->
/home/nick/projects/chessengine/data` (verified `git check-ignore` first). The
tracked relative symlink then resolves, `git status` stays clean, and merges
work. **Do not symlink `engines/canonical` itself.**

The brief is grounded in checked facts rather than remembered ones:

  alone would have cost a day.

  The lever is **rows touched and their ORDER**.

  the byte cut and the offset saving were the same design choice and cancelled,
  leaving a dead 320 B tail the L2 prefetcher fetched anyway. Its compacted
  two-plane layout is named as the surviving candidate.

  engine is memory-bound (IPC 1.595, >=47% stall) and a prior programme cut
  -317.7 i/n for ZERO wall-clock. `cycles:u` is barred outright while a match
  runs (+49.9% under load).

refreshed margins **756 and 1068 are statistically indistinguishable** (-1.39 vs
-3.01, 1.6 Elo apart on +/-11.5 bars) while **parity is ~17 Elo below both**. So
this is not the smooth monotonic slope the old data suggested — it is a
**PLATEAU across slope 0.48-0.67 with a CLIFF at parity.** The 535 arm tests
whether the plateau extends down to 0.34.

**A hypothesis of mine, raised and REFUTED inside one tick.** Both interim
readings had swung +5.4 Elo toward the final (1068: -8.54@529 -> -3.01;
756: -6.77@1027 -> -1.39). Two equal same-signed swings looked mechanistic, so I
proposed that fastchess reads the opening book IN ORDER, biasing early games.
**Checked: `tools/screen.sh:86` passes `order=random`. No such bias exists.**
The statistics say the same thing — deviation SD is ~6.8 Elo at n=529 and ~3.4 at
n=1027, so the swings are 0.8 and 1.6 sigma, unremarkable, and two positives is a
1-in-4 coin flip.

**So the interims were never WRONG; my INTERPRETATION was.** -3.01 sits well
inside -8.54 +/- 16.7. The failure last tick was quoting a number as
"point-for-point identical" when its own bar was +/-16.7 — not the measurement
misbehaving. Recording this because the tempting lesson ("interims are biased")
is false and would have become a permanent bad rule.

**Still unresolved and unchanged:** the MECHANISM of the parity cliff. Depth is
refuted (13.209 vs 13.204). Every arm is fixed-node; real-TC confirmation is
still required before any frame decision ships.

## 2026-08-01 03:40 — STUDY 66: three bit-exact arms, all negative, and it REFUTES STUDY 65's mechanism

**No speed win landed. The value is that the whole family is now closed with the
cost decomposed** — and that a mechanism I merged four hours ago was wrong.

**1. The compacted layout does not rescue study 65, AND study 65's explanation
was false.** Study 65 blamed its +13.23% L2 misses on the dead 320-byte tail the
prefetcher fetches anyway. Removing the tail ENTIRELY converts **-0.81% of L2
misses (6/8 rounds)**, and `l2_pf_hit_l2.all` is still **+18.09% with no tail in
existence**. I merged that mechanism into main at `908126d0` and wrote it into
the handoff as the reusable insight. **It was wrong, and only building the
successor exposed it.**

**2. The real cost is the INDIRECTION, not the decode or the tail.** Isolated
inside one binary: the 243 KB row map ALONE costs **+6.02% instructions, +6.49%
task-clock, +15.28% L2 misses** (0/8 negative) — ~1.7 L2 misses per lookup. The
6-bit pack on top buys **-2.21% L2 misses** for **+7.63% instructions**. **So
even a FREE map loses.** Study 64 §6.1 named that table; study 65 designed around
it and blamed the prefetcher.

**3. Locality converts nothing — a two-sided null.** The block transpose cut the
share of a fold's row pairs sharing a 4 KB page by **3.32x (2.794% -> 0.842%,
PHYSICAL offsets)** and moved every counter by <=1.5% at 2-4/8 sign counts. The
agent predicted the opposite direction and its own physical-offset census caught
it. **Mechanism: the fold is MISS-PARALLEL** — one tile walk exposes ~80
independent line accesses inside a ~320-uop window, so misses are already all in
flight and limited by MSHRs, not address proximity. That also kills
"prefetch the next row" BY CONSTRUCTION, since the tile walk touches every row of
the fold inside tile 0. **This closes brief routes 1 and 3 with a number.**

Route 5 (redundant work) priced out: threat folds carry **1.93% exactly-cancelling
rows**, HalfKA **0%**, repeats **0** — 1.05% of folded rows against ~5
comparisons per fold.

Methodology worth keeping: **five physical layouts as five DATA MODES of ONE
binary**, so every contrast shares `.text` byte-for-byte and box load cancels.
All arms bit-exact (5,001,560 nodes + eval hash exact in every mode), 8 shuffled
rounds.

**MY BRIEF CARRIED STALE PROFILE SYMBOLS, AND I VERIFIED IT.**
`grep -rn 'fn i16_deltas_into|fn i16_deltas_dual' src/` returns **NOTHING** —
those functions do not exist; perf study 32's i8 conversion replaced them. The
live hot symbols are **`avx512::i8_into_shaped` (53.61% of L1-miss latency,
`sota_simd.rs:1864`)**, `forward_into` (14.44%, `sota_head.rs:621`),
`finny_refresh_into` (6.72%, `sota_accumulator.rs:632`).

**The standing directive's "Unexplored, from our OWN profile" list still names
`i16_deltas_into 13.14%` and `current_full 3.28%` — it has been pointing studies
at a DEAD SYMBOL.** Any future brief must use the list above.

Gate: `gate66.service` verifying (agent reports exit 0, all three pins unmoved,
724 tests green, off-path +48 B `.text` / +0.163 instructions/node, node- and
eval-identical). It also stripped 14 MB of arm binaries a `git add -A` had swept
into branch history and gitignored `bin66/`.

## 2026-08-01 05:00 — STUDY 67: the search side is FRONTEND-bound, and a dead string was costing real time

**A landed win, a reframe of the engine's cost model, and two nulls with
mechanisms.**

### The re-derived profile (step 0 — the reason it was mandatory)

`i16_deltas_into` / `i16_deltas_dual`: **confirmed absent**, as I found by grep.
Live list on the current PGO binary, three counters:

| symbol | instructions:u | branch-misses | `ic_cache_fill_l2` |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `search::<NonPv>` (45,565 B) | **30.87 %** | **37.05 %** | **34.06 %** |
| `i8_deltas_into_inner` | 15.07 % | 7.72 % | 9.90 % |
| `forward_into` | 12.01 % | 3.68 % | 7.09 % |
| `init_quiets` | 9.84 % | **15.89 %** | 8.44 % |
| `at_full` | 6.38 % | 7.42 % | 7.19 % |
| **search side TOTAL** | **49.4 %** | **64.7 %** | **58.9 %** |

### THE REFRAME: the search side is FRONTEND-bound, not memory- or compute-bound

Per node, startup subtracted: **128.0 L1i line fills** (8.2 KB of CODE fetched
from L2), **30.6 % I-cache miss rate**, **40.5 % of ops delivered by the DECODER
rather than the op cache**, 21.09 % of dispatch slots lost to the frontend, and
**445.7 cycles/node with a completely empty op queue** (11.5 % of the frame).
Hot code working set **120.3 KB against a 32 KB L1i** — one function is larger
than the entire L1i.

**This refines, and does not contradict, the standing "ARCHON is MEMORY-bound"
model** ([[archon-is-memory-bound-not-instruction-bound]]): the EVAL side is
data-bound (the ~106 MB weight tables), the SEARCH side is CODE-bound. Different
halves, different walls. **The project had no instrument for the frontend one
until tonight.**

### The landed win — verified by me, not taken on report

`qtrace` took `mv: &str` and the call site passed `&m.to_string()`. **Rust
evaluates arguments before the call**, so the SHIPPED build ran the whole
`Display` chain, heap-allocated a `String` and dropped it — once per qsearch
move — to feed a function whose body is literally `{}`.

Confirmed on main before the fix: `src/search/mod.rs:324` is
`fn qtrace(_: usize, _: Value, _: Value, _: Value, _: &str, _: &str) {}` and
`mod.rs:4750` passes `&m.to_string()`. The fix changes the parameter to
`mv: Move` (a `Copy` u16 newtype), so nothing is formatted in the shipped build.

14 shuffled rounds: **instructions -1.259 % (14/14)**, `ic_cache_fill_l2`
-5.087 % (12/14), task-clock -2.168 % (11/14) = **-2.33 % of measured search
time**, a derived **+4.1 Elo** (derived — it has played no games).
`malloc`, `<Square as Display>::fmt` and `<char as Display>::fmt` leave the
profile entirely. The null is a **byte-identical binary** (the PGO pipeline is
deterministic here), so every arm difference is real codegen.

### Two nulls, and they overturn the obvious icache intuition

- **Cold-path outlining LOSES.** Cut `search::<NonPv>` 45,565 -> 36,033 B
  (-20.9 %) and raised L1i fills **+3.2 %**. **The bytes removed were never
  fetched** — a line with zero sampled ops never enters L1i — and outlining two
  warm callees added a fetch stream.
- Deduplicating `do_move`/`next_move` monomorphisations confirms it from the
  other side: the only arm that lowers fills (-1.462 %) but pays +1.517 %
  instructions and loses wall clock.
- **Function SIZE is not the instruction-cache cost; the number of distinct lines
  the frame EXECUTES is.** Both arms cut ~20 % of bytes and moved the fill
  counter in OPPOSITE directions.

### The reusable pre-registration number

**445.7 cycles / 128.0 fills = at most 3.48 cycles (~5 instructions) per L1i line
fill.** The losing outlining arm paid 45. **Pre-register that ratio before
building any icache arm, and never price one in bytes.**

Self-caught and recorded rather than buried: its tools matched symbols by
substring and `search::<NonPv>` is a substring of `qsearch::<NonPv>` (every
within-symbol share re-derived, no conclusion changed), and three claims written
from plausibility are now measured or flagged as non-evidence.

`gate67` verifying. New instruments in `tools/perf67/` (12 scripts).

signature for a pure dead-work removal, since deleting an unused `to_string()`

**REWROTE THE STANDING COST-MODEL MEMORY, because it was directing studies at a
CLOSED route.** It said *"aim new studies at cache/memory locality... the three

construction (miss-parallel, MSHR-limited). It now records the **two-halves**
model:

- **EVAL side = DATA-bound**, locality routes closed with mechanisms.
- **SEARCH side = FRONTEND-bound**, 120.3 KB of hot code against a 32 KB L1i.

plus **function size is not the icache cost**, the **3.48 cycles/L1i-fill**
pre-registration budget, and the **dead-work-that-ships** class (`qtrace`) as a
thing to hunt. Second memory rewritten tonight for the same reason: **a memory
written mid-investigation records a hypothesis, and the games or the next study
can invalidate it.**

**PERF STUDY 68 COMMISSIONED — the frontend wall**, which nothing has ever
attacked. Handed the four measured constants, both closed routes WITH their
mechanisms (so it cannot rebuild outlining-by-size), the 3.48 cycles/fill budget
it must pre-register against, and study 67's 12 tools with instructions to reuse
rather than rebuild them.

Four directions, in the brief's order: **code LAYOUT** (executed lines
contiguous, after measuring what PGO already achieved); **the op cache** at
40.5 % decoder delivery, driven by 32-byte-window density and currently
unmeasured; **branch mispredicts**, where the search side owns 64.7 % at a
~15-cycle penalty and nothing has attacked them directly; and **a sweep for more
dead-work-that-ships** — cfg'd-out or no-op sinks with expensive argument
expressions, `format!`/`to_string`/`Vec` on discarded paths. That last class is
pure profit and was worth 2.33 % of search time in a single instance.

**And the first attempt SKIPPED it on a self-match false positive.** I ran
`pgrep -f "$D"` where `$D` was the worktree path — which appeared in my own
command line, so **the pgrep matched its own caller** and reported the worktree
busy. Re-checked with the documented guard (exclude `shell-snapshots`, `$$`,
`$PPID`) and the only hit was a process that had already exited.

This is exactly the failure mode the `pkill -f` rule exists for
([[pkill-matches-your-own-shell]]), and it is worth recording that it bites the
DETECTION path too, not just the kill path: here it cost a skipped cleanup, but
the identical mistake in a kill would have killed the caller. **The guard is
mandatory for any `pgrep -f` whose pattern can appear in the command that types
it.**

Study 69's worktree verified intact afterwards; all 27 worktree registrations
survive.

|---|---:|

SE 2.28).

**The reason to pool rather than quote the newest: the point-to-point swing
(+1.85 to +15.06, 13 Elo) EXCEEDS any plausible training gain over 100
superbatches.** A single 1500-game screen at +/-11 cannot resolve progress at this
resolution — it is mostly sampling noise around a positive level. Quoting
"sb1410 = +15.06" as D49's strength, as I did at 04:32, over-reads one draw;
quoting sb1430's +1.85 would under-read it identically.

**What the pool does and does not claim.** It is an average over the sb1350-1450
RANGE, not one net's strength — six different nets. That is the right object
here: it says **D49 in this range is solidly ahead of canonical**, which is a
claim the individual screens cannot support and the pool can.

**Caveat retained:** all six ran on the OLD binary (`target-sota`, bench 587683),
deliberately, for series consistency. The sb1700 verdict must use the PGO build.

## 2026-08-01 06:32 — tested the plateau question instead of restating it: D49 is STILL LEARNING

**D49:** sb1477, 188.6k pos/s. Disk 145 GB. All units green. Study 69 running.

I had been repeating "heldout ema hovering ~0.01044-0.01045" for several ticks.
That is an impression, not a measurement, and the standing rule
([[quote-levels-not-trends]]) is to **re-test a repeated caveat rather than
restate it**. Regressed the EMA on superbatch:

| window | slope / sb | t | verdict |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| sb1100–1470 (n=38) | −6.06e-07 | **−13.35** | falling |
| sb1280–1470 (n=20) | −2.23e-07 | **−11.45** | falling |
| **sb1360–1470 (n=12)** | **−1.43e-07** | **−3.82** | **still falling** |

**No plateau.** Even the newest 110-superbatch window is significantly negative.
But the **rate has decayed ~4x** from the sb1100 window — precisely what the
`d49z_decay` LR ramp should produce (verified earlier: 1.579e-5 at sb1170 ->
5.57e-6 at sb1430, 0.996/sb, interpolated on the ABSOLUTE superbatch).

**So running to sb1700 is justified by the data, not just by the plan** — the run
is neither converged nor diverging.

**What this explicitly does NOT say:** anything about Elo.
[[loss-is-not-elo]] — heldout loss is a mean over a position distribution, Elo is
decided at decision points, and this project has a receipt of 44e-6 of loss
sitting alongside ~30 Elo of games. This regression is an **abort-condition
instrument**: it says training is healthy and should continue. The strength claim
is the separate pooled one, **+9.70 +/- 4.47 over 9000 games**.

## 2026-08-01 07:10 — STUDY 69: branch mispredicts CLOSED with arithmetic, and it retracted its own claim

**Merged `6d6e3e85`, gate `gate69` verifying. Verified by me: `TABLES` is still a
`LazyLock` (`attacks.rs:789`) — the revert landed.** 15 new instruments.

### Target B — built, measured, REVERTED

Pre-registered **−0.19 to −0.28 %**; measured **+0.321 % instructions, 0/16**
against a byte-identical null at +0.000 %. **The arm worked** (60 → 0 `TABLES`
refs in `search::<NonPv>`) **and still lost.** Two mechanisms:

1. **The guard is worth ~4 i/node, not ~15**: the field read *replaces* the
   `lea`, because x86 RIP-relative addressing has no index register so the base
   must be materialised either way. Study 68's §4.3 lesson repeating —
   **counting a code sequence is not measuring it; you must count what replaces
   it.**
2. **A source change that alters hot function SIZES buys a PGO inline
   re-decision that can cost 4× its direct effect and INVERT ITS SIGN.** Non-PGO
   control: −0.077 %, 10/10 — i.e. the arm is a small win before PGO and a loss
   after.

**It wrote a sweeping claim and then built the null that retracted it.** It first
concluded "the error bar on studies 61–68 is unknown", then built `pgonull69`
(8 inert bytes on `Board`), which reads **+0.000 % (5/12) with every hot symbol
byte-identical in size**. So the effect is real but **confined to edits that
change hot-symbol sizes** — the earlier studies are not impeached. That is
exactly the discipline this project asks for, done unprompted.

### Target A — branch mispredicts, closed with arithmetic rather than opinion

**MY BRIEF WAS WRONG AND IT CORRECTED ME.** I wrote "~15-cycle penalty"; the
engine's **own measured** exchange rate is **9.13 cycles** (study 40 §3, from
`ex_no_retire.empty`). I overstated the budget by ~64 %.

Real budget: 21.65 misp/node × 9.13 = **6.02 % of cycles** — and **there is no
concentration to attack**. The largest single source line is
`movepick.rs:282` (the insertion-sort shift loop) at **4.49 % of the taken-mispredict
population = 0.157 % of cycles**. Break-even is arithmetic: at 2.45 IPC,
**22.4 instructions per mispredict removed**. The one arm built cost **39.1** and
lost by 1.75×. Four candidates priced against the budget, all outside it before a
line was written. My "first suspect" (the run-boundary test) ranks **twelfth**.

New instrument the project lacked: a **per-branch-site mispredict profile
resolved to source**, via AMD **LBR v2** (16-deep stack sampled uniformly in
`instructions:u`) — `perf record -e branch-misses` has no precise mode on AMD.
Plus an execution-rate probe for never-taken code, needed because **an LBR stack
holds only TAKEN branches**: all ten `TABLES` guards show 0 of 418,976 entries
while executing 2.20×/node.

### Target C — dead-work class swept exhaustively and CLOSED

The whole guard class is **4.21 ops/node in three statics**; **133 other candidate
sites never execute**. The other forms sweep clean: `on_dirty` is properly
`WANTS_DIRTY`-gated, no `format!`/`to_string` outside gated code, and the ~260
`tunables!` getters compile to constants. **After paying twice (67, 68), this
class is now empty.**

### ★ Another counter fails its null

**`branch-misses:u` fails its own byte-identical null (−0.803 %, 11/16)** and
joins `ic_cache_fill_l2`, `de_op_queue_empty` and `task-clock` on the
same-panel-null list. **`instructions:u` remains the only counter on this box that
decides anything.**
